ownerGeneration is a process-independent account lineage number. ownerEpoch is the current owner-transition epoch. documentToken identifies this browser document. leaseToken identifies the current coordinator claim. The five fields of OwnerTuple are ownerFingerprint, ownerGeneration, ownerEpoch, documentToken, and leaseToken; they are compared byte-for-byte as one canonical JSON tuple. markerSequence is metadata of OwnerMarkerV1, not part of OwnerTuple, wire identity, query identity, or SyncMetadataV1. ownerFingerprint is the lowercase SHA-256 digest of stable JSON {namespace:"hanks-hits-owner-v1",ownerId}; it is the only account identity persisted in a marker, envelope, or URL, and the raw authenticated subject is never persisted or sent in a query. ownerFingerprintFor("guest") is fixed to the digest of the guest namespace. markerSequence starts at 1 when the marker is first created and increments on every marker write without rotating owner generation; marker B uses A's sequence plus one, and the active B confirmation uses the next sequence. The marker key is hanks-hits-progress-owner.

OwnerTuple remains the client/document identity used by adoption and local purge. OwnerEpochLeaseV1 is a separate server-issued authority proof stored only in the verified marker and sent in the exact wire proof fields. A user owner obtains it with one claim GET only after the recovery probe returns authority-absent; a server response returns the signed lease, and every later GET, POST, beacon POST, and DELETE uses mode use. A local marker transition without a successful server claim remains blocked and cannot call READY or transport. The server row, not a client-supplied epoch or sync.sourceEpoch, decides whether the tuple is current.

Every marker write carries all OwnerMarkerV1 fields. An active authenticated marker has state:"active", quarantineKey:null, recovery:null, pendingTarget:null, pendingQuarantineWrite:null, pendingClaimNonce:null, pendingRecoveryNonce:null, liveReset:null, and the exact verified serverLease; an active guest marker has serverLease:null because guest never contacts the server. A purging or quarantined marker retains the current recovery budget, transition token ledger, exact pending target, exact pending quarantine payload when one exists, quarantine record key when one has been allocated, the durable claim nonce and recovery nonce when a response may need replay, and the last verified server lease. Marker updates use stable serialization and byte-for-byte read-back under the current owner barrier; a partial field set is invalid and cannot become active. A user marker cannot enter READY or send transport until its server lease is obtained and read back in a successful claim or recovery response.

Registration is synchronous at store construction. The coordinator allocates a FIFO registrationOrder, records the binding, and starts one shared claimOnce promise. Concurrent registrations join that promise in allocation order. The first registration does not own a unique generation. A second registration for the same appId and the same store API increments the binding reference count and returns another lease. A second live store API for the same appId is a deterministic configuration error. A released lease decrements the count. Count zero freezes that binding after one microtask grace period, cancels its timers, and retains the owner tuple. It does not rotate generation or delete its marker. StrictMode mount, unmount, remount, hydration, GET, POST, retry, and replay never rotate generation.

The shared claim order is: await resolved authentication status; compute the intended owner fingerprint; read and classify the marker as absent, valid, or invalid; inspect every local envelope proof before any projection is adopted. A new authenticated document first sends a mode:"recover" probe with a durable recoveryNonce, an explicit recoveryReason, and its full newly generated requested OwnerTuple. If the locked server row is absent, the response is an explicit authority-absent result and the coordinator sends one mode:"claim" request with the same durable owner tuple and a distinct durable claimNonce; if a row exists, a new-document or marker-loss recovery atomically returns a fresh lease bound to the requested tuple and the server's next epoch, while a resume recovery returns the exact current authority only for the exact tuple or its durable replay. An authenticated marker that is absent, malformed, or has lost its lease proof uses the marker-loss recovery reason, never a local 1/1 reset. Recovery is an atomic server takeover: a marker-loss or new-document request against an existing row issues one fresh document-bound lease even when the old lease is unexpired, increments the authority epoch once under the user lock, and invalidates the old tuple; a resume request cannot take over another live document. A concurrent recovery cannot receive the same valid document/lease authority. Retrying an identical recovery nonce and tuple returns the committed recovery lease with recoveryReplayed:true; reusing that nonce with another tuple returns recovery_nonce_reuse. A retained envelope with the intended fingerprint is rebased to the returned authoritative epoch only after that response and its structural correlation checks succeed. A guest fingerprint may enter the explicit first-login transfer branch. When a valid marker A has a different fingerprint, a guest-to-user transfer is selected only for the verified first-login branch; every other mismatch freezes writes and allocates B with ownerGeneration:A.ownerGeneration+1 and ownerEpoch:A.ownerEpoch+1. A foreign B is written in purging, verified, and used for the exact purge sequence. If marker B cannot be proven, it enters quarantine with no target deletion. If the active marker has the same fingerprint but another document token, freeze writes, allocate B with both counters incremented once, write the new document tuple, read it back, then rebase every valid local envelope's sync.sourceEpoch to B's epoch while retaining owner fingerprint, path clocks, values, and mutation IDs. If the full tuple is already this document's tuple, reuse it. If the marker is purging or quarantined for the intended fingerprint, resume recovery, while a different intended fingerprint remains blocked. A cross-document marker race that reads back another tuple is not treated as success: reread and retry on [0ms,25ms,100ms,500ms,2000ms], then quarantine. Only the verified read-back result advances the state machine.

For an authenticated owner, the client sends no POST, beacon, DELETE, or READY before the recovery probe, any required claim, and marker lease read-back complete. The browser checks only the lease schema, canonical base64url shape, user-digest correlation, owner fingerprint, full owner-tuple binding including document and lease tokens, generation/epoch relation, lease ID, issue/expiry bounds, expected nonce result, recovery reason, authority-result kind, and response correlation. It never receives the HMAC secret and never verifies HMAC bytes. Server code alone verifies PROGRESS_SYNC_OWNER_LEASE_SECRET. The client stores the returned lease in the marker with a read-back and only then changes B or the newly claimed marker to active. A response that is late, mismatched, malformed, lower than the server authority, or associated with a reused claim or recovery nonce is discarded and leaves the coordinator blocked. The server's next-epoch row makes every prior lease stale for all methods.

Generation and epoch rotate only on these events: a new document coordinator's server recovery or claim response, an explicit authenticated owner transition (guest to user, user to guest, or user A to user B), the single marker-B allocation that retires the old tuple during a foreign-owner transition, or explicit device forget after purge. Marker B allocation is the sole local rotation for that transition: activation reuses B's owner generation and epoch and never increments them again. A store registration, achievements mount, current-game mount, duplicate lease, tab visibility event, pagehide, rehydrate, response adoption, and retry are not rotation events. rotateOwnerInPlace(reason) is the only mounted-document invalidation operation. It synchronously enters FROZEN, records the server recovery/claim as the sole authority transition, aborts and records every in-flight request, clears GET and POST baselines, request counters, retry timers, force latches, and beacon records, marks every mounted registration invalid for the previous tuple, then revalidates all still-mounted registrations against the same document-wide documentProgressSync handle after marker B and any server claim are read back. It retains each registration's stable app/API identity and ref-count, never creates a second coordinator, and invokes the same construction-time storage wrapper rather than replacing it. The auth-session effect, sign-out result, foreign-marker observer, storage-event receiver, and device-forget path all call this operation before clearing or adopting bytes. A mounted hook subscribes to the coordinator owner version and calls ensureRegistration(appId,api) again after rotation; a duplicate call is idempotent. owner.rotation.mounted-a-to-b, owner.rotation.signout-same-tab, owner.rotation.broadcast-sibling, and owner.rotation.strictmode-active-request assert that achievements and the current game remain registered under the new tuple, old GET/POST/beacon completions cannot mutate them, and no registration or generation is leaked. disposeDocument enters TEARING_DOWN and starts one absolute DISPOSE_DRAIN_DEADLINE_MS=1500 timer before awaiting the write barrier and delivery drain. The implementation races the barrier/drain promise against that timer; at the deadline it aborts fetches, records queued logical saves as pending, sends no new beacon, releases all leases, and retires the handle even if the write barrier is still pending. A stalled barrier or request cannot keep teardown or timers alive. It does not delete the marker. The named owner.teardown.hung-barrier case asserts retirement at 1500 ms with a never-resolving barrier. A new document gets a new document token and obtains its generation only from the server recovery or claim result.

2.2 Coordinator states and barriers

StateEntryPermitted workExit
NEWOne browser handle is created.Register bindings. No local hydration, persist write, transport, or READY.FIFO claim begins.
CLAIMINGMarker is read once through the guarded backend.Registrations join the shared claim. Store actions remain in memory and cannot reach raw storage.Marker absent, guest, same owner, or foreign owner branch.
AUTH_WAITAuthentication is unresolved or returned 401.Keep validated local bytes and registrations, but perform no adoption, save, beacon, or READY.Resolved auth consumes forcePending and returns to CLAIMING, or teardown.
OWNEDFull tuple is read back and verified.Each registered adapter may rehydrate. Raw writes require the exact current tuple.All current bindings hydrated, then RECONCILING.
RECONCILINGGET baseline and local meaningfulness are known.Only the specified GET, merge, replace POST, or non-persisting server adoption is allowed.All registered entries reach READY or a typed blocked state.
GET_RETRY_EXHAUSTEDInitial or reconcile GET consumed the fixed automatic budget.No network, adoption, POST, beacon, or READY. A stable force call may start one fresh GET.Fresh GET succeeds, owner/auth recovery, or BLOCKED after a protocol failure.
BLOCKEDProtocol-invalid response, terminal recovery failure, or manual retry exhaustion.Retain diagnostics and verified bytes. No hydration adoption, transport, or READY.Only explicit force, verified owner recovery, or teardown.
TRANSFERRING_GUESTAn authenticated first login finds a verified guest marker.Capture and validate guest projections, write/read back the user tuple, rebase metadata, and run the first-login truth table. No transport occurs before the user marker is verified.RECONCILING after transfer proof, or QUARANTINED.
READYAll registration leases have valid hydration results and any required adoption is acknowledged.Persisted mutations and transport use the current tuple and lineage checks.Owner transition, foreign marker, storage failure, or teardown.
FROZENrotateOwnerInPlace or an owner-control barrier invalidates the current tuple.No persist write, hydration adoption, network, beacon, or READY. It only aborts/records old attempts, resets owner-scoped lineage, verifies marker B and any server claim, and revalidates mounted bindings.PURGING, TRANSFERRING_GUEST, or RECONCILING after the new owner proof is read back; QUARANTINED on failure.
PURGINGMarker differs from the intended owner, or sign-out is explicit.Freeze all store writes, write the new marker, purge exact targets, and read back each result.OWNED after successful purge, or QUARANTINED.
QUARANTINEDAny marker write, remove, read-back, parse, or storage operation fails.No hydration adoption, transport, READY, or unscheduled raw deletion. Only the bounded recovery schedule or explicit device-forget may remove the named app/control/quarantine targets, and every such removal requires read-back.Successful recovery returns to CLAIMING; device forget returns only after proof.
TEARING_DOWNDocument teardown or explicit dispose.Freeze, drain, release, and retain marker.Handle is retired and cannot accept registration.

Every store action and every direct api.setState call is bound before the action can persist. Before ownership is ready, the action may update live memory but the persistence wrapper rejects raw writes with OwnerBarrierPending; the coordinator records the latest full snapshot and commits it after READY. A pending action never sends network traffic. A thrown barrier is surfaced to the source action and is never converted into a successful mutation notice.

pagehide is a delivery event, not an ownership event. It freezes the current tuple, drains the current owned pending queue through one beacon builder, and then retires the document handle. It never clears progress keys, deletes the owner marker, writes a new marker, or sends a beacon after the tuple becomes stale. A pagehide from A after B's marker read-back therefore has no destructive path.

2.3 Fixed guest ownership model

Guest is the fixed owner ID guest. Guest local progress uses the same 32 manifest keys and the same validated envelope. Guest mutations receive full metadata and mutation lineage but never call GET, POST, beacon, or DELETE. Before authentication status resolves, the coordinator keeps registration and storage binding active but does not hydrate or read progress. A resolved unauthenticated session claims guest; a resolved authenticated session claims the user directly. Sign-out calls documentProgressSync.requestSignOut, cancels account delivery, freezes account writes, purges every account and owner-control target, and performs the one transition reset only after every purge and quarantine record is verified. It then writes a new guest tuple, reads it back, and broadcasts a sign-out event only after the verified guest marker. If purge, marker read-back, or the transition reset enters QUARANTINED, the coordinator returns {status:"quarantined"}, leaves no live account state or transport path, and emits a redacted diagnostic; signOutAndClear still invokes NextAuth sign-out so authentication ends, then leaves the document blocked from guest READY until recovery or forced reload. The named auth-client.signout-quarantine case asserts that order. A guest session can start only after the guest marker is verified. A raw marker containing a prior user never becomes a guest marker without the purge sequence.

type LegacyHistoricalZustandRecord =
  | { kind: "v0"; state: unknown; version: 0 }
  | { kind: "checkers-v2-pre-envelope"; state: unknown; version: 2; persistName: "checkers-progress" }
  | { kind: "oregon-v1-pre-envelope"; state: unknown; version: 1; persistName: "oregon-trail-storage" };
type HistoricalMigrationRecipe = PersistMigrationRecipe;

migrateLegacyGuestRecord is the only path that adopts a meaningful unowned Zustand record. After the resolved session is guest and the marker observation is absent or is an active guest marker with the current tuple, it reads one generated primary/legacy key pair at a time, decodes the entry's exact PersistMigrationRecipe, validates the source shape and canonical projection, and evaluates the generated meaningful predicate. The recipes include checkers.zustand-v2-pre-envelope for the live checkers-progress version-2 record, followed by checkers.version-2-to-envelope-v1, and oregon.zustand-v1-pre-envelope for the live oregon-trail-storage version-1 record, followed by oregon.version-1-to-envelope-v1 and the named weather normalization when that source version contains the legacy weather shape. These records are not treated as current envelopes merely because their Zustand version is known. For an empty/default result it performs no write and treats the key as absent. For a meaningful result it creates LegacyGuestMigrationResult with migrationId="legacy:${appId}:${sha256(sourceKey+":"+rawDigest).slice(0,32)}", source epoch equal to the verified guest owner epoch, and path clocks from the injected clock; it writes the version-1 guest envelope to the primary key, reads back canonical bytes, then removes a distinct legacy key only through validatePendingStorageRemoval(...,"legacy-migration") and verifies null read-back. A primary key that is itself historical is rewritten and verified in place. A guest migration failure leaves the original bytes, writes no network request, and blocks READY. An authenticated owner, a foreign marker, a malformed marker with meaningful bytes, and a historical record with an unknown schema all quarantine rather than migrate. guest.legacy-32-key-migration covers every A1-A32 key, and guest.legacy-chess-migration covers the Chess primary/legacy ordering.

documentProgressSync.requestSignOut(): Promise<SignOutResult> is a never-reject API. The successful return is exactly {status:"guest-ready",resetCompleted:true,reloadRequired:false,failureCode:null,failedStoreIds:[]}. It catches synchronous and asynchronous coordinator, backend, marker, index, and reset exceptions, maps an unexpected exception to failureCode:"coordinator_exception", maps any failed store API to failureCode:"store_reset_failed" with exact failed app IDs, enters QUARANTINED, emits a redacted diagnostic, and returns the typed quarantined variant with resetCompleted:false and reloadRequired:true. It owns one sign-out operation token. The transition marker persists LiveResetLedgerV1 as ready-to-claim, then claimed before invoking resetAllLiveStoresWithoutPersist(token), then complete only after all 32 store APIs have default live state and the marker update reads back. The helper never writes reset defaults to storage and is invoked at most once for that transition token. A claimed ledger is never invoked again after reload; a reset throw or completion-marker failure retains QUARANTINED, emits one redacted event, and requires forced reload. It returns the quarantined variant with exact failed app IDs when any store reset throws. It emits one store-reset-failed event containing only failed app IDs and count, never state values. A failed reset leaves no guest owner, READY, transport, or broadcast path; after NextAuth sign-out, signOutAndClear calls the coordinator's forceReloadAfterSignOutFailure to replace the document, ensuring the failed in-memory store cannot remain visible. signOutAndClear only awaits the result, invokes NextAuth sign-out after it for both statuses, and never performs a second reset. The named auth-client.signout-coordinator-throw and auth-client.signout-store-reset-failure cases inject a rejected request and one failed store reset, assert one reset attempt, one redacted failure event, and one NextAuth call, and verify forced reload with no guest READY, transport, or stale broadcast.

Marker before loginLocal projectionServer GETExact outcome
AbsentEmpty/defaultAbsentClaim user, write/read back user marker, hydrate defaults, READY, no POST.
AbsentEmpty/defaultMeaningfulClaim user, adopt server baseline non-persistingly, write canonical local envelope, READY, no POST.
AbsentMeaningfulAbsentClaim user, validate local projection, replace POST once, adopt canonical response, READY.
AbsentMeaningfulMeaningfulClaim user, retain GET baseline separately, merge with metadata, POST merge:true, adopt canonical response, READY.
GuestEmpty/defaultAbsentTransfer owner to user, clear guest marker through owner transition, hydrate defaults, READY, no POST.
GuestEmpty/defaultMeaningfulTransfer owner to user, adopt server baseline, READY, no POST.
GuestMeaningfulAbsentTransfer meaningful guest projection to user with replace POST, then adopt response.
GuestMeaningfulMeaningfulTransfer with merge POST using guest metadata and user owner tuple, then adopt response.
Same userEmpty/defaultAbsentKeep user marker, hydrate defaults, READY, no POST.
Same userEmpty/defaultMeaningfulAdopt GET server baseline, write local envelope, READY, no POST.
Same userMeaningfulAbsentReplace POST with full owner and lineage identity, adopt response, READY.
Same userMeaningfulMeaningfulKeep clean GET baseline separate, merge local mutation, POST, adopt response, READY.
Foreign userEmpty/defaultNot requestedFreeze, marker B before B1, purge all A targets, quarantine on failure, no GET, no POST, no READY until recovery.
Foreign userMeaningfulNot requestedSame purge path; local meaningful data is never uploaded to the foreign account.
Malformed markerAnyNot requestedRecord only marker parse/schema failure, digest, and byte length; retain no marker bytes. Freeze, write/read back marker B when storage permits, purge all app and control targets, and use the bounded recovery schedule. No live adoption, network, or READY occurs until recovery succeeds.
Marker write/read-back failureAnyNot requestedEnter QUARANTINED, retain the last verified marker, record the failure, and wait for a recovery trigger.
Hydrated local observationServer observationBaseline and save gate
Persisted key absent, semantic state defaultAbsentRecord get-server baseline with null data. Enter READY with no POST. The default snapshot is clean and cannot be queued by a poll, force call, visibility event, or pagehide.
Persisted key absent, semantic state defaultPresentAdopt the validated server response non-persistingly, write the verified local envelope, record get-server baseline, enter READY with no POST, and suppress every save path while the canonical state remains non-meaningful.
Persisted key present, semantic state defaultAbsentKeep the validated local envelope as a clean local baseline, record an absent get-server baseline, enter READY with no POST, and do not reinterpret persisted default bytes as a mutation.
Persisted key present, semantic state defaultPresentAdopt the server baseline non-persistingly, replace the local envelope after read-back, record get-server baseline, enter READY with no POST, and never upload or merge the default local projection.
Persisted key absent or present, meaningful stateAbsentCapture one stable request snapshot and send one merge:false replace POST. The replace response is the first post-acknowledged baseline.
Persisted key absent or present, meaningful statePresentKeep the clean get-server baseline separate, merge the meaningful local snapshot with metadata, and send merge:true. Only the exact POST response creates post-acknowledged.

The clean baseline is {kind:"get-server",meaningfulLocal:false,saveSuppressed:true} when the default gate completes without POST. forceSync, debounce, retry, visibility, and pagehide inspect that gate before allocating a delivery identity; they cannot turn default bytes or metadata-only clocks into a save. The first successful commitSourceMutation whose canonical projection differs from the clean baseline clears saveSuppressed, allocates a mutation/logical-save/request identity, and queues exactly that meaningful snapshot. default-save-suppression covers all four default combinations, the first post-READY poll, force, hidden-page, pagehide, and the first meaningful mutation.

Meaningful is an independent generated predicate over canonical paths: a value differs from the schema default, a set has an item, a MAX field is positive, a saved item exists, or a non-default nullable value is present. It never treats a metadata-only clock as meaningful. A local envelope is eligible for account upload only when its owner fingerprint equals the authenticated fingerprint or it is an explicit guest envelope being transferred at first login. On guest-to-user transfer, the coordinator enters TRANSFERRING_GUEST, rejects new guest mutations, awaits the mutation/write barrier and all in-flight guest commits, captures projections only after the barrier is empty, creates a new user owner epoch, rewrites every retained metadata entry's sourceEpoch and owner fingerprint to that epoch and user fingerprint without changing its path clock or value, then runs the exact staging/payload/ledger cleanup in guest-transfer order under marker B. It requires read-back absence for every old guest payload, staging entry, ledger, and sign-out control before clearing the pending fields. It then allocates one transfer mutation ID under the user tuple and sends the rebased envelope. A mutation completing after the freeze is rejected and retried only after the user tuple is active. The truth-table test supplies one fixture per cell for all 32 app entries and includes marker-loss, retained-bytes, legacy-record, guest-transfer, and control-key failure cases.

Guest transfer removes dynamic delivery records before static controls: it validates the guest staging journal and ledger, uses each journal/ledger entry's old guest owner in deliveryPayloadTargetForLedger(entry.owner,entry), and uses marker B only as the freeze barrier. It enumerates every entry in sorted appId,logicalSaveId order, removes each payload through validatePendingStorageRemoval(...,"guest-transfer") with null read-back, removes the matching journal entry, then removes and reads back hanks-hits-progress-delivery-ledger-v1 and hanks-hits-progress-delivery-staging-v1. An absent ledger with a non-empty staging journal is repaired from the staging evidence before deletion; an unknown dynamic key or mismatch quarantines. The transfer then allocates a new user-owned payload and ledger entry for the single rebased transfer mutation. This is covered by guest.first-login.truth-table.test.ts and lineage.replay-retry.test.ts.

Owner-control lineage is explicit. When marker loss or a same-user document takeover changes the owner tuple but retains the owner fingerprint, a validated PendingDeliveryLedgerV1 is rewritten with the new tuple and read back before any pending delivery; firstAttemptAtMs, cumulative attempts, terminal state, and logical save IDs are preserved. A missing empty ledger is created under the new tuple. A malformed ledger or a ledger with a different fingerprint is treated as a foreign control target and enters the same freeze, marker-B, purge, quarantine, and no-transport path as foreign app data. A guest ledger is never sent; guest-to-user transfer deletes/read-backs it and allocates a new user-owned transfer entry.

3. Singleton store binding, hydration, converters, and rollback

3.1 Construction-time Zustand 5 contract

The implementation adds createSynchronizedStore and changes every real persist constructor in the inventory to use it. The helper creates the raw backend, the owner-aware StateStorage seam, and the object-valued PersistStorage wrapper before calling Zustand persist. It passes storage: persistStorage, a generated partialize, generated merge, version: 1, and skipHydration: true in the initial options. No store calls createJSONStorage, relies on Zustand's fallback storage, or calls setOptions after construction. The first real action, api.setState, or persist.rehydrate therefore sees the guarded wrapper.

createSynchronizedStore has an explicit runtime:"browser" | "server" parameter. Browser construction uses the owner-aware backend and registers with documentProgressSync. Server construction uses a request-scoped in-memory backend with a fresh serverRequestToken, never touches window, localStorage, the owner marker, or the browser coordinator, and never rehydrates. Next server components receive request-scoped stores through progress-sync-ssr.ts; no module-level server store can share progress between requests. Client hydration constructs the browser store once, keeps skipHydration:true, and lets the coordinator own rehydration after the browser claim. The SSR test imports every 32 store module under a server runtime, asserts memory backend identity and no browser-global access, then asserts the browser path binds the exact manifest wrapper.

type StoreRuntime = "browser" | "server";
type StorageBackend = {
  backendId: symbol;
  getItem(key: string): string | null;
  setItem(key: string, value: string): void;
  removeItem(key: string): void;
};
type PersistStorageId = symbol;
type OwnedPersistStorage<P> = PersistStorage<P, void> & {
  persistStorageId: PersistStorageId;
  rawBackendId: symbol;
  appId: ValidAppId;
};
type PersistRuntimeAdapter<TState, TPersisted> = {
  api: SynchronizedStoreApi<TState, TPersisted>;
  persistStorage: OwnedPersistStorage<TPersisted>;
  persistOptions: PersistOptions<TState, TPersisted, void>;
  rawBackend: StorageBackend;
};
type RehydrateResult = void | Promise<void>;
type StoreBinding<TFull, TPersisted, TLive, TCanonical> = {
  appId: ValidAppId;
  storeApiIdentity: symbol;
  readFull(): FullStoreState<TFull>;
  readPersisted(): PersistedProjectionObservation<TPersisted>;
  fromFullToLive(full: FullStoreState<TFull>): LiveProgress<TLive>;
  fromPersistedToLive(persisted: PersistedProjection<TPersisted>): LiveProgress<TLive>;

type FullStoreState<T> = Readonly<Pick<T, {
  [K in keyof T]-?: T[K] extends (...args: never[]) => unknown ? never : K
}[keyof T]>>;
type PersistedProjection<T> = Readonly<T>;
type LiveProgress<T> = Readonly<T> & { readonly __liveProgress: unique symbol };
type CanonicalServerData<T> = Readonly<T> & { readonly __canonicalServer: unique symbol };
type MetadataProjection = Readonly<SyncMetadataV1> & { readonly __metadataProjection: unique symbol };
type RequestProgressData<T> = {
  data: CanonicalServerData<T>;
  sync: SyncMetadataV1;
};
type PersistedProjectionObservation<P> =
  | { kind: "absent"; foundStorageKey: string | null }
  | { kind: "valid"; foundStorageKey: string; persisted: PersistedProjection<P>; rawBytes: string; rawDigest: string; rawByteLength: number }
  | { kind: "invalid"; foundStorageKey: string; failure: "parse" | "version" | "schema" | "metadata" | "converter" | "migration" | "owner"; rawDigest: string; rawByteLength: number };
type RequestProgressSnapshot<TLive, TCanonical> = {
  mutationId: string;
  live: LiveProgress<TLive>;
  canonical: CanonicalServerData<TCanonical>;
  request: RequestProgressData<TCanonical>;
  semanticBytes: string;
  excludedPaths: readonly LivePathKey[];
  capturedAtClockMs: number;
};
type SynchronizedStoreConfig<TState, TPersisted, TLive, TCanonical> = {
  runtime: StoreRuntime;
  appId: ValidAppId;
  initialState: TState;
  persistName: string;
  binding: Omit<StoreBinding<TState, TPersisted, TLive, TCanonical>, "storeApiIdentity">;
  registerCaller: (api: SynchronizedStoreApi<TState, TPersisted>) => RegistrationLease;
  requestToken: string | null;
};
type SynchronizedPersistApi<TState, TPersisted> = {
  getOptions(): Partial<PersistOptions<TState, TPersisted, void>>;

};
type ReadonlyStoreApi<TState> = Pick<StoreApi<TState>, "getState" | "getInitialState" | "subscribe">;
type SynchronizedStoreReadonlyApi<TState, TPersisted> = ReadonlyStoreApi<TState> & {
  persist: SynchronizedPersistApi<TState, TPersisted>;
};
type SynchronizedStoreApi<TState, TPersisted> = UseBoundStore<SynchronizedStoreReadonlyApi<TState, TPersisted>> & {
  setState: StoreApi<TState>["setState"];
  persist: {
    getOptions(): Partial<PersistOptions<TState, TPersisted, void>>;

    hasHydrated(): boolean;

  config: SynchronizedStoreConfig<TState, TPersisted, TLive, TCanonical>

  requestToken: string;
};

  config: SsrStoreConfig<TState, TPersisted, TLive, TCanonical>

type AuthSyncSnapshot = {
  state: "loading" | "guest" | "authenticated" | "foreign" | "blocked" | "tearing-down";
  owner: OwnerTuple | null;
  ready: boolean;
  failure: OwnerRecoveryFailure | null;
  lastSynced: Date | null;
  forceSync: () => Promise<void>;
};
type LegacyUseAuthSyncOptions<T extends AppProgressData> = {
  appId: ValidAppId;
  localStorageKey: string;
  getState: () => T;
  setState: (value: T) => void;
  debounceMs?: number;
  onSyncComplete?: (source: "local" | "server") => void;
};
type LegacyUseAuthSyncReturn = {
  isAuthenticated: boolean;
  isGuest: boolean;
  syncStatus: "idle" | "syncing" | "synced" | "error";
  lastSynced: Date | null;
  forceSync: () => Promise<void>;
};
declare function useAuthSync(): AuthSyncSnapshot;
declare function useAuthSync<T extends AppProgressData>(options: LegacyUseAuthSyncOptions<T>): LegacyUseAuthSyncReturn;
declare function useOwnerSnapshot(): OwnerSnapshot;

createSynchronizedStore is the sole browser and test construction API. Its implementation first allocates rawBackend, then the owner-aware StateStorage, then OwnedPersistStorage, then calls Zustand's vanilla createStore with the initial PersistOptions; it passes that vanilla API through Zustand 5 useStore and returns one hybrid UseBoundStore that is callable with no selector or a selector and also exposes getState, setState, subscribe, persist, and progressSync. It returns the hybrid only after registerCaller(api) has bound the same API identity. runtime:"browser" requires requestToken:null and the singleton registration lease. runtime:"server" is reserved for the SSR helper and requires a non-empty request token, a new in-memory backend, no browser globals, no coordinator registration, and no rehydrate call. The helper does not expose persist.setOptions, so no caller can replace the guarded storage after construction. The migration from every current module-level Zustand create/persist call is one generated call with the exact manifest entry, persist name, binding, and caller registration.

useAuthSync() calls useSyncExternalStore(documentProgressSync.subscribe,documentProgressSync.getSnapshot,documentProgressSync.getServerSnapshot) with stable coordinator methods, so every render observes subscription changes and SSR has a deterministic loading snapshot. It returns state:"loading" until authentication and owner claim are complete, state:"guest" or "authenticated" only after valid hydration, state:"foreign" during purge, state:"blocked" for quarantine or terminal recovery, and state:"tearing-down" after disposal. ready is true only when all 32 slots have valid hydration results, including virtual-noop results; owner is the verified tuple or null; failure is the typed terminal failure or null; forceSync is one stable Promise-returning handle. useOwnerSnapshot() is the subscribed owner-only selector used by HomeClient. HomeClient, AchievementCelebrations, and every generated store caller consume these snapshots and never inspect storage themselves.

The generic call sites in the live useAuthSync.ts surface are retained only through a compatibility adapter during the migration. useAuthSync(options) creates one coordinator registration for the manifest appId, subscribes to the same external snapshot, and maps state:"authenticated" to isAuthenticated:true, state:"guest" to isGuest:true, loading|foreign|blocked|tearing-down to syncStatus:"syncing"|"error", and READY to syncStatus:"synced". Its getState, setState, and onSyncComplete fields are bound to the generated store binding, never to raw storage or direct transport. The effect cleanup releases exactly that registration lease; owner rotation invalidates the old lease, aborts its attempts, revalidates the mounted binding, and transfers the stable forceSync Promise handle. The argumentless form remains the public contract for HomeClient and new consumers, while the adapter is proven against every existing live caller listed in Section 11.

The actual helper preserves the installed declaration signatures: StateStorage<void> is the raw string seam; PersistStorage<P, void> is the object seam; PersistOptions<TState, P, void> is the initial option type; Zustand 5 exposes persist.getOptions() as Partial<PersistOptions<TState,P,void>>, so materializePersistOptions narrows required name,storage,partialize,merge,version,skipHydration fields before use; and rehydrate is consumed as void | Promise<void>. The wrapper identity proof runs against every real store API and asserts:

  • store.persist.getOptions().storage is present and is the exact manifest-owned object wrapper.
  • The wrapper's persistStorageId, raw backend ID, app ID, and manifest entry all match.
  • storage is not the default Zustand storage and is never undefined.
  • skipHydration is true before coordinator claim, and only the coordinator invokes the real rehydrate.
  • A valid store action cannot reach raw storage before the owner barrier is ready.

3.2 Persisted envelope and validation order

Every synchronized local key stores one canonical JSON record. Its exact property set is {state, version, sync}. state is the generated persisted projection, version is the per-entry persist version 1, and sync is SyncMetadataV1. The canonical serializer emits properties in that order and uses stable object-key sorting below each property. The raw local record, request body, response body, and JSONB server record all use the same metadata channel name, sync.

The object-valued wrapper follows the installed Zustand contract precisely: getItem parses and validates the full raw record, stores the validated sync in the binding, and returns {state: record.state, version: record.version} to Zustand; setItem receives Zustand's {state,version}, combines it with the binding's current validated metadata, and serializes the full three-property record. Zustand never receives an untyped extension object and the metadata side channel never bypasses the owner barrier.

ownedPersistStorage.getItem first checks the current owner barrier, reads the exact key, and returns null only for a verified absent value. It then performs UTF-8 JSON parse, outer record-shape validation, historical-version classification, and owner/marker consistency before any value can reach Zustand. A current version-1 record proceeds to persisted-schema validation, metadata validation, named converter selection, converter execution, and generated persisted-projection validation. A LegacyHistoricalZustandRecord proceeds through the entry's exact PersistMigrationRecipe.decode and migrate, then migrated persisted-schema validation, metadata construction only in the verified guest branch, named converter selection, converter execution, generated persisted-projection validation, meaningfulness evaluation, and version-1 envelope construction/read-back. The migrated state is validated both as the recipe output and as canonical/live source data. Missing sync is usable only in the verified guest migration branch defined in Section 8.1; meaningful historical bytes under authenticated, foreign, malformed-owner, or unknown ownership remain unchanged, are quarantined, and never enter live state or network. No persisted metadataMigrated field exists. Unknown historical bytes are quarantined and never interpreted by a guessed converter.

InputResult of getItem/mergeLive state, network, READY
Absent keynull; default state remains.Hydration may finish for this entry. No local write is created solely by absence.
JSON parse failureThrow HydrationValidationError("parse_failure"); quarantine raw bytes when the backend permits.Unchanged. No network. Coordinator cannot enter READY.
Well-formed unsupported versionThrow HydrationValidationError("unsupported_version").Unchanged. No network. Coordinator cannot enter READY.
Legacy {state,version:0}, Checkers version:2, Oregon version:1, or missing-version recordDecode with the manifest entry's exact historical recipe, validate the migrated projection, and return legacy-unowned; only the verified guest branch may write a new envelope and read it back.Authenticated/foreign/malformed-owner paths remain unchanged and quarantine. Guest migration has no network and cannot enter READY until the primary write/read-back and any legacy-key deletion/read-back succeed.
Well-formed invalid state/schemaThrow HydrationValidationError("persisted_schema").Unchanged. No network. Coordinator cannot enter READY.
Invalid sync metadata or ownershipThrow HydrationValidationError("metadata_or_owner"); foreign owner enters purge.Unchanged. No network. Coordinator cannot enter READY.
Converter failureThrow HydrationValidationError("converter_failure"); do not fall back to incoming bytes.Unchanged. No network. Coordinator cannot enter READY.
Migration failureThrow HydrationValidationError("migration_failure"); retain the original raw record for quarantine.Unchanged. No network. Coordinator cannot enter READY.
Storage read throwsThrow StorageBarrierError("read_failure").Unchanged. No network. Coordinator enters QUARANTINED.

merge repeats ownership, version, schema, metadata, and converter validation on the value returned by getItem. For a version-0 or missing-version record, the exact order is decode LegacyZustandRecordV0, select the manifest entry's one PersistMigrationRecipe, run migrate, validate migrated state and persisted projection, convert it to live state, and, only in the verified guest branch, emit the version-1 envelope. A migration output is never passed to Zustand before validation and canonical primary read-back. It calls the generated converter only after validation, creates a full live patch, and calls Zustand state mutation under the persistence-suppression guard. A merge error propagates as a typed hydration error. It never returns incoming state, performs a shallow fallback, or lets Zustand adopt an invalid value. The coordinator sets hasHydrated-based readiness only after all registered entries finish valid rehydrate callbacks.

3.3 Exact shape converters and non-persisting application

The production seam is not a direct generic live-state port. Each generated entry supplies exact full-store, live-progress, persisted-projection, request, canonical-server, and metadata conversion contracts in StoreBinding. The binding never passes an unbranded live object to the route, and the request conversion is the only path that combines canonical data with SyncMetadataV1.

Shape classFull store snapshotPersisted projectionLive/canonical rule
flatAll non-function state fields, including UI fields which are excluded by the manifest.Exact flat object returned by the source partialize.getProgress fields are copied through Zod validation. Canonical response returns only declared progress fields. Runtime-only fields stay untouched.
wrapped-progressFull store with progress plus settings/runtime fields.{progress: P} and exact declared persisted settings.Live progress is state.progress. Canonical data nests back under progress. Non-progress settings are kept by the full snapshot.
full-storeA7 2048 full store, including grid, current score, game-over, keep-playing, move history, and persisted progress counters.Exact generated partializer projection: highScore,highestTile,gamesPlayed,gamesWon,lastModified. Grid, current score, game-over, keep-playing, and move history are runtime-only EXCLUDE fields, not silently copied into canonical data.Live/canonical conversion reads and writes only the declared A7 progress counters. Non-persisted board/runtime fields remain byte-for-byte in the full snapshot during GET adoption, POST adoption, and rollback; applyLiveWithoutPersist cannot reset the current grid.
oregon-full-journeyThe complete Oregon GameState: phase, start flag, leader, occupation, party, departure month, day, miles, landmark index, pace, supplies, weather, current event/river, hunting fields, rest, food, rivers crossed, events encountered, and source mutation timestamp.The exact canonical journeySnapshot projection contains all listed journey fields, with no default Zustand partializer and no runtime field omitted by inference. The converter is the named oregon-full-journey-v1 implementation and the persist migration is oregon.version-0-weather.The full journey is one opaque bounded canonical value. applyLiveWithoutPersist replaces only those journey fields, preserves unrelated store actions and runtime references, validates party IDs and health literals, and never calls Date.now().
chess-nestedChess board, history, game status, progress, difficulty, game mode, player color.Current primary persisted state, including the declared nested fields.Canonical progress is flattened with difficulty, game mode, and player color. Applying it nests progress and settings without replacing board/history/runtime fields. Legacy bytes pass the named converter only.
achievements-progressprogress, runtime celebration queue, and runtime watermarks.{progress} only. Watermarks and queue are not synchronized or persisted.getProgress and canonical data contain unlocked achievements and lastModified. Runtime watermarks remain local observation state and cannot enter request, response, DB, metadata, or merge.

converter.full-store-2048 is the named A7 case: a full snapshot with a non-default grid, current score, game-over flag, keep-playing flag, and move history is adopted from a canonical response containing only highScore,highestTile,gamesPlayed,gamesWon,lastModified. The test asserts those runtime fields are unchanged, one persisted projection is generated for a real source action, rollback restores them, and no grid byte appears in request or canonical data.

The non-persisting application path takes a full snapshot, computes a patch containing only declared live fields, and executes api.setState(patch, false) inside withPersistenceSuppressed. The suppression guard drops precisely the one persist write caused by that application and does not suppress unrelated runtime updates. Chess retains board/history. Wrapped stores retain settings not in canonical data. Rollback restores only the captured declared full data fields and leaves actions and unrelated runtime state intact. Adoption, rollback, GET baseline installation, and conflict rebase publish no mutation notice.

The source migration makes the clock contract explicit for the three current stores whose getters synthesize Date.now(). Hill Climb adds lastModified: 0 to its full GameState default, includes it in partialize, copies it in setProgress, and reads it in getProgress; its named recipe is hill-climb.version-0-lastModified. Monster Truck adds lastModified: 0 to its full store state, includes it in the generated partializer and canonical schema, and applies it in setProgress; Oregon adds the same persisted field to its full journey state, canonical schema, and setProgress. The generated source wrapper writes the injected mutation clock to that field only at a successful setter or awaited completion boundary. Their getters read the stored field and never call a clock, storage, network, or random source. This is the fixed contract: lastModified is persisted and synchronized for A1, A2, and A5, not synthesized, nullable, or silently omitted. Existing version-0 bytes receive lastModified:0 in their named migration, and a missing field in an authenticated current envelope is invalid rather than filled from wall time.

Oregon's source PartyMember already carries stable IDs m0 through m3, while the current Zod schema strips them. The implementation adds id to the canonical schema. A legacy party array without IDs is accepted only when it has at most five entries and each generated m${index} ID is unique; the converter writes the canonical IDs and never derives an ID from a mutable name. A legacy row with a duplicate or out-of-bound index fails migration before live state mutation.

Each production getProgress() is a pure, idempotent, wall-clock-free projection of the current store. Hill Climb, Monster Truck, and Oregon Trail getters read only the current state, including the explicitly persisted lastModified field where declared. Oregon has an explicit canonical-only/full-journey partializer rather than Zustand's implicit full-state persistence. provenance.idempotent-source-getters calls each getter twice without a state change and asserts identical canonical bytes, no persist write, no mutation notice, no clock read, and no metadata clock change. request.snapshot-stability captures one RequestProgressSnapshot per logical save and reuses its canonical bytes for fetch POST, beacon POST, retry, replay, and body-hash calculation; a later getter read cannot change the request identity or beacon deduplication key.

3.4 Chess canonical and legacy decision table

Primary hank-chess-stateLegacy chess-storageDecision, writes, deletes
ValidAbsentUse primary. Validate canonical response. No legacy action.
ValidValid semantically duplicatePrimary wins. Canonical write and read-back complete first, then delete legacy and read back deletion.
ValidValid different valuePrimary wins by fixed key precedence. Legacy is quarantined before deletion; never merge two full runtime Chess states.
ValidMalformedUse primary. Quarantine legacy. Primary remains usable; legacy deletion follows successful primary read-back.
MalformedValidQuarantine primary, convert legacy, validate it, write primary, read back primary, then delete legacy and read back deletion.
MalformedMalformedQuarantine both. Leave live state unchanged. No network and no READY.
Unknown historical bytesAnyUnknown bytes are not guessed. The known valid side follows the table; both unknown sides block hydration.
Any valid sideAny valid sideEvery write and delete checks the current owner tuple. If a write/read-back fails, restore prior bytes where possible and enter quarantine.

Every Chess legacy deletion in this table is a legacy-migration removal, never a direct key operation. After the canonical primary write and read-back succeeds, construct pendingTarget:{owner:B,key:"chess-storage",targetKey:"chess-storage",targetDigest:sha256(UTF-8("chess-storage")),kind:"app-data",operation:"remove-and-verify"}, reread the current marker, call validatePendingStorageRemoval(markerB,pendingTarget,"legacy-migration"), require ok:true and target.appId:"chess", call removeItem(validatedRemoval.target.key), and require a null read-back of that exact validated key. A failed legacy removal leaves the primary bytes and legacy quarantine evidence intact; it never deletes the primary as compensation. The same validated sequence is used for semantically duplicate, different-valid, malformed-legacy, and rollback cases.

Chess gives each shape case a named test: chess.primary-valid-legacy-absent, chess.both-valid-semantic-duplicate, chess.both-valid-different, chess.primary-valid-legacy-malformed, chess.primary-malformed-legacy-valid, chess.both-malformed, chess.unknown-historical-bytes, chess.write-readback-deletion-order, and chess.rollback-restores-both. They also cover same-value/different-timestamp and different-value/same-timestamp. A failed legacy delete never causes primary deletion.

4. Foreign ownership, purge, guest transfer, and mutation provenance

4.1 Exact target manifest and purge algorithm

The generated key manifest supplies every primary and known legacy target. It contains all 32 primary keys and the current legacy key chess-storage. The separate generated owner-control set contains the marker key, quarantine index and namespace, sign-out broadcast key, the metadata-only delivery ledger key hanks-hits-progress-delivery-ledger-v1, and the fixed delivery staging journal key hanks-hits-progress-delivery-staging-v1; the ledger, staging journal, and quarantine index are removed/read-back-verified during account transition and contain no progress bytes. The old suffix scan is removed from account clearing. No computed prefix is a purge target. The device-owned alias fwa_myland_v1 is a separate non-account target and is never passed to purge, GET, POST, beacon, DELETE, or owner comparison. The sole guest-to-user first-login transfer is the explicit exception to foreign purge: it captures validated meaningful guest projections before marker transition, writes the user marker, rebases metadata, and transfers through the truth table. User A to user B and malformed-owner transitions always purge.

type MarkerObservation =
  | { kind: "absent" }
  | { kind: "valid"; marker: OwnerMarkerV1 }
  | { kind: "invalid"; failure: "parse_failure" | "schema_failure"; rawDigest: string; rawByteLength: number };

type OwnerRecoveryFailureBase = {

const QUARANTINE_INDEX_KEY = "hanks-hits-progress-quarantine-index-v1";
type NonAccountStorageEntry = {

const NON_ACCOUNT_STORAGE: readonly NonAccountStorageEntry[] = [
  { key: "hanks-hits-recently-played", source: "apps/web/src/app/HomeClient.tsx", scope: "device-local", sync: false, purge: "never", ownerGate: "none" },
