The source oracle scans direct browser storage calls in addition to Zustand persist names. Every call must resolve to one generated 32-app or owner-control target, or to exactly one of the four NON_ACCOUNT_STORAGE entries. These four keys never enter the sync manifest, purge sequence, owner barrier, guest transfer, or account meaningfulness predicate. The unused Drawing constant ARTWORK_STORAGE_KEY is not a target because the current store does not read or write it; the oracle fails if a future direct write appears without a manifest entry or one of the four explicit exclusions.

type HomeAppSnapshot = {
  appId: ValidAppId;
  meaningful: boolean;
  displayStats: Readonly<Record<string, string | number | boolean | null>>;
  source: "owned-local" | "server-baseline";
};
type OwnerSnapshot = {
  state: "loading" | "guest" | "authenticated" | "foreign" | "blocked" | "tearing-down";
  ownerTuple: OwnerTuple | null;
  ownerFingerprint: OwnerFingerprint | null;
  markerSequence: number | null;
  meaningfulAppIds: readonly ValidAppId[];
  homeApps: readonly HomeAppSnapshot[];
  ready: boolean;
};
type LocalProgressReadResult<T> =
  | { kind: "empty"; appId: ValidAppId; sourceKey: string | null; value: null }
  | { kind: "owned"; appId: ValidAppId; sourceKey: string; value: T; owner: OwnerTuple }
  | { kind: "legacy-guest-candidate"; appId: ValidAppId; sourceKey: string; value: T }
  | { kind: "foreign"; appId: ValidAppId; sourceKey: string; rawDigest: string; rawByteLength: number }
  | { kind: "invalid"; appId: ValidAppId; sourceKey: string; failure: "parse" | "schema" | "converter" | "migration" }
  | { kind: "blocked"; appId: ValidAppId; sourceKey: string | null; failure: OwnerRecoveryFailure };
declare function readOwnerSnapshot(): OwnerSnapshot;
declare function accountStorageTargets(appId: ValidAppId): readonly GeneratedStorageTarget[];
declare function readLocalProgress<T>(appId: ValidAppId): LocalProgressReadResult<T>;

apps/web/src/lib/storage-keys.ts exports the generated accountStorageTargets(appId) and no raw key concatenation. readOwnerSnapshot() is the non-hook backing reader used internally by the subscribed useOwnerSnapshot() boundary; HomeClient calls only useOwnerSnapshot(). The snapshot returns state:"loading" with ready:false before authentication/claim, returns guest or authenticated meaningful app IDs and owner-validated homeApps display data only after validated hydration, returns foreign during marker-B purge, returns blocked for malformed bytes, quarantine, terminal recovery, or a failed owner marker, and returns tearing-down after disposal with ready:false. It never reads or compares OWNER_MARKER_KEY directly. HomeClient renders homeApps display stats only from the subscribed snapshot, renders the default/blocked view for foreign, blocked, or tearing-down state, and never displays a raw or foreign projection or calls readLocalProgress.

readLocalProgress(appId) reads the generated primary target first, then the explicit legacy target list in manifest order. A valid canonical primary wins and legacy bytes are retained until the named migration says they can be deleted; an absent primary permits the first valid legacy candidate; a malformed primary is an invalid result and does not fall through to mask corruption, except for the Chess decision table whose canonical/legacy cases are explicit. An owned envelope is returned only when its owner tuple equals the coordinator tuple. A meaningful unowned version-0 record is returned as legacy-guest-candidate only under verified guest claim; empty/default bytes return empty. Foreign, malformed-owner, converter, migration, thrown-read, and silent-read-back outcomes return a typed invalid/foreign/blocked result and never enter HomeClient state. The four NON_ACCOUNT_STORAGE keys use their source-specific accessors and cannot be returned by this function. localProgress.primary-legacy-precedence and home-owner-snapshot assert loading, guest, authenticated, foreign, malformed, primary-wins, legacy-fallback, and device-alias behavior.

GeneratedPurgeTargetManifest is a generated projection of the authoritative input and has exactly PURGE_TARGET_COUNT=36 static entries: the 32 primary app keys in A1-A32 order, the Chess legacy key chess-storage, the delivery-ledger key hanks-hits-progress-delivery-ledger-v1, DELIVERY_STAGING_KEY with literal value hanks-hits-progress-delivery-staging-v1, and SIGNOUT_BROADCAST_KEY with its source-defined literal value hanks-hits-signout-broadcast. Dynamic delivery-payload records are not counted in the 36 static entries. They are valid only when deliveryPayloadTargetForLedger(payloadOwner,entry) recomputes the exact owner-bound key, app ID, logical save ID, digest, and owner-control scope from a validated ledger entry. Every static entry stores targetDigest=sha256(UTF-8(key)), its exact kind, its app ID or null, and its exact scope. The owner marker, quarantine index, quarantine namespace, quarantine records, and every NON_ACCOUNT_STORAGE key are not removable purge targets. Quarantine storage keys are authorized only by the namespace validator below. purgeTargetForKey returns the unique generated static entry or null; it never hashes an unlisted key into a new target. sameOwnerTuple compares the five OwnerTuple fields, and samePurgeTarget compares key, digest, kind, app ID, and scope.

Before the A1 app-data loop, foreign purge and guest transfer read and validate both owner-control journals under the old owner A: hanks-hits-progress-delivery-staging-v1 and hanks-hits-progress-delivery-ledger-v1. The staging journal is written before any payload and ledger write, so a payload-first/ledger-failure crash remains enumerated. Each journal has one owner tuple, at most MAX_PENDING_LOGICAL_SAVES=64 entries, unique lexicographically sorted appId,logicalSaveId pairs, the complete mutation/request/hash/merge/base-version identity, exact payload key/digest/byte length, exact primary local-persist key, and local-envelope digest/length/state. Recovery validates a staging entry's payloadKey=deliveryPayloadKey(staging.owner,logicalSaveId), its localPersistKey against the generated primary target for appId, and the payload read-back before it can remove or promote anything. A localPersistState:"verified" entry requires an exact local-envelope digest and byte-length read-back; a localPersistState:"pending" entry accepts only an absent local key or an exact matching local envelope, and upgrades to "verified" before ledger reconstruction when the bytes match. If the ledger is absent while the staging journal names an entry, recovery uses the staging entry as the old-owner evidence, validates the payload as PendingDeliveryPayloadV1, reconstructs the complete PendingDeliveryEntryV1, writes that ledger with a canonical read-back, and then continues; an unlisted dynamic key, invalid local state, or staging/ledger mismatch is terminal delivery_payload_invalid. For each valid entry, the coordinator writes marker B with pendingTarget:{owner:B,key:payloadKey,targetKey:payloadKey,targetDigest,kind:"delivery-payload",operation:"remove-and-verify"}, rereads marker B, and calls validateDeliveryPayloadTarget(markerB,pendingTarget,ledger). That validator finds the entry by key, recomputes deliveryPayloadTargetForLedger(entry.owner,entry) using old owner A, and returns a validated target whose owner is A; B is only the freeze/write barrier. The coordinator removes only validatedRemoval.target.key through validatePendingStorageRemoval(markerB,pendingTarget,"foreign-purge"), requires null read-back, and clears the pending target. It removes the dynamic payloads in sorted order, then the ledger, then the staging journal, then the sign-out broadcast, then A1-A32 and Chess legacy targets. The ledger and staging static targets are each read back absent. A missing ledger and missing staging journal are success only when the indexed dynamic-key scan proves no delivery namespace key exists; a foreign, malformed, missing-payload, invalid-local-state, or unknown dynamic record quarantines without deletion. Guest transfer uses the same old-owner-A target and B barrier under "guest-transfer".

localPersistState:"unknown" is a valid recovery state only when the same staging entry contains a matching LocalPersistRepairV1 payload and the marker's pending quarantine write names that repair. Recovery never interprets unknown as success, never promotes its local bytes, and must verify restoration of the recorded previous envelope before per-entry cleanup; otherwise it remains quarantined. The static staging and ledger records are repaired by sorted read-modify-write, so a crash at any point preserves all unrelated entries. The local repair record is a quarantine record, not a purge target; its namespace key is validated by the same owner-bound quarantine validator before write, read-back, or cleanup.

Each previousLocalEnvelope is null or is at most MAX_ENVELOPE_BYTES UTF-8 bytes, each canonical staging entry is at most MAX_DELIVERY_STAGING_ENTRY_BYTES=MAX_ENVELOPE_BYTES+8192, and the canonical staging journal is at most MAX_DELIVERY_STAGING_BYTES=64*MAX_DELIVERY_STAGING_ENTRY_BYTES. A journal or entry over its cap is rejected before any source setter, payload write, or local persist write.

type PurgeTargetValidation =
  | { ok: true; target: PurgeTargetV1 }
  | { ok: false; failure: OwnerRecoveryFailure };
declare function validatePurgeTargetForRemoval(marker: OwnerMarkerV1, pending: PendingTargetV1): PurgeTargetValidation;
declare function validatePendingStorageRemoval(marker: OwnerMarkerV1, pending: PendingTargetV1, authorization: StorageRemovalAuthorization): StorageRemovalValidation;
declare function validateOwnerMarkerRemoval(marker: OwnerMarkerV1, pending: PendingTargetV1, authorization: "device-forget"): OwnerMarkerRemovalValidation;
declare function readQuarantineIndexForRecovery(marker: OwnerMarkerV1): QuarantineIndexObservation;
declare function validateQuarantineIndexForOwner(value: unknown, marker: OwnerMarkerV1): QuarantineIndexV1;
declare function validateQuarantineRecordForOwner(value: unknown, listedKey: string, marker: OwnerMarkerV1): QuarantineRecordV1;
declare function validateQuarantineRecordKey(key: string, marker: OwnerMarkerV1): QuarantineRecordKeyV1 | null;
declare function validateQuarantineStorageKey(pending: PendingTargetV1, marker: OwnerMarkerV1): QuarantineStorageTargetV1 | null;

validatePurgeTargetForRemoval first requires the reread marker to be the current full tuple B, requires pending.owner to compare equal to B, requires pending.operation:"remove-and-verify", and looks up pending.key in the generated 36-entry static set. It compares stored digest, target key, kind, app ID, and scope to that entry. A delivery-payload target instead passes validateDeliveryPayloadTarget(markerB,pending,validatedLedger); that validator finds exactly one ledger entry by payloadKey, uses the ledger entry's old owner A in deliveryPayloadTargetForLedger(entry.owner,entry), and validates the payload read-back. A delivery-staging target passes validateDeliveryStagingTarget(markerB,pending,staging); the target owner is the staging journal's old owner A while marker B remains the deletion barrier. It rejects the marker, index key, namespace prefix, any quarantine record key, the device alias, and every unknown key with terminal purge_target_invalid; it performs no remove. validatePendingStorageRemoval is the exhaustive gate for every named storage deletion. pendingRemovalAuthorization(target,liveReset) is selected before validation and is a discriminated value: generated app/control, validated staging, and ledger-enumerated payloads use "foreign-purge" or "guest-transfer"; chess-storage uses "legacy-migration"; an acknowledged payload and its matching staging/ledger chain uses "delivery-ledger-ack"; a generated primary repair uses "local-persist-repair"; a quarantine record or index key uses "recovery-cleanup" only when liveReset.status:"complete", and otherwise is rejected; explicit device forget uses "device-forget" and validateOwnerMarkerRemoval. There is no generic foreign-purge branch for quarantine keys. The dispatcher returns one typed StorageRemovalValidation and performs no removeItem on failure. Every quarantine index/record validator requires version 1, exact owner B, current namespace, sorted unique keys, caps, and generated target agreement. Any owner, namespace, target, kind, or digest mismatch produces terminal quarantine_owner_mismatch or purge_target_invalid. No function accepts an unlisted dynamic key.

recovery-cleanup is a distinct authorization branch, not an alias. It permits only QuarantineStorageTargetV1 keys whose marker B, namespace, owner, listed record, digest, and read-back evidence have already passed validateQuarantineStorageKey; it cannot remove an app key, delivery payload, ledger, staging journal, sign-out broadcast, device alias, or owner marker. foreign-purge and guest-transfer reject every quarantine index and quarantine record before removeItem. After the live reset, quarantine record cleanup calls validatePendingStorageRemoval(markerB,pendingTarget,"recovery-cleanup"), and empty-index cleanup uses the same authorization. Device forget remains the separate owner-marker sequence. The validator has a branch assertion that passes a quarantine target through each authorization and proves only recovery-cleanup and the explicit device-forget path can return ok:true.

validateOwnerMarkerRemoval is a separate branded branch, never a generated purge target. It requires authorization:"device-forget", a current full tuple B, pending.operation:"remove-and-verify", pending.kind:"owner-marker", pending.key===OWNER_MARKER_KEY, pending.targetKey===OWNER_MARKER_KEY, and pending.targetDigest===sha256(UTF-8(OWNER_MARKER_KEY)). It returns the discriminated OwnerMarkerRemovalValidation; its ok:true target is the branded OwnerMarkerRemovalTargetV1 and is produced only after the coordinator has read back every app/control target absent and validated all quarantine evidence. It rejects every other authorization, owner, key, digest, or pending shape without storage access.

Every non-marker storage deletion, including foreign-purge app/control targets, Chess legacy migration, guest-transfer ledger clearing, acknowledged-ledger cleanup, quarantine records, and quarantine index, first rereads the current marker and calls validatePendingStorageRemoval(markerB,pendingTarget,authorization). The function returns the discriminated StorageRemovalValidation; only its ok:true branch may proceed. The owner-marker deletion is the sole separate branded path: it rereads marker B, calls validateOwnerMarkerRemoval, requires OwnerMarkerRemovalValidation.ok:true, and consumes only its OwnerMarkerRemovalTargetV1. Every successful branch passes only its validated target to storage, and its exact read-back uses validatedRemoval.target.key. A failed validation has no storage side effect.

After if (!validatedRemoval.ok) { ... }, the successful union narrows validatedRemoval.target to ValidatedStorageRemovalTargetV1. For an app or control target, it copies owner B, key=target.key, targetKey=target.key, the generated target digest and kind, namespace=target.scope, the generated app ID, and target scope. For a quarantine record or index, it copies owner B, the exact quarantine storage key, exact underlying target key and digest, the quarantine kind, namespace=quarantineNamespace(B), appId:null, and scope:"quarantine". For the owner marker, it copies owner B, key=targetKey=OWNER_MARKER_KEY, targetDigest=sha256(UTF-8(OWNER_MARKER_KEY)), kind:"owner-marker", namespace:"owner-control", appId:null, and scope:"owner-control". The deletion function accepts only this normalized target and never accepts a raw record or pending-target field.

readQuarantineIndexForRecovery defines missing-index behavior. A verified absent index is converted to {kind:"absent-empty",index:{version:1,owner:B,namespace:quarantineNamespace(B),quarantineKeys:[]}} only when marker B has pendingTarget:null, pendingQuarantineWrite:null, and quarantineKey:null. A missing index while any pending write, pending record key, or prior quarantine evidence exists returns terminal quarantine_index_missing and cannot reach reset or active. Present bytes pass validateQuarantineIndexForOwner; parse, version, owner, namespace, ordering, duplicate-key, cap, or record-key failures return terminal quarantine_index_invalid or quarantine_owner_mismatch. The first quarantine write uses the absent-empty result as its exact starting payload, while owner.purge.quarantine-index-missing supplies missing-index fixtures with and without durable evidence and asserts these two distinct outcomes.

On a foreign marker, the coordinator performs this exact sequence:

  1. Read and classify marker A. A missing marker is {kind:"absent"}. A valid marker is {kind:"valid",marker}. A JSON or schema failure is {kind:"invalid",failure,rawDigest,rawByteLength}; raw marker bytes are never retained. Freeze every binding and cancel mutation delivery. A stale binding can no longer call raw setItem, removeItem, transport, or READY.
  2. Allocate transition token T, owner tuple B once, incrementing generation and epoch exactly once. Writing marker B retires A and is the only rotation for this transition. Marker B has state purging, recovery:{attemptNumber:0,recoveryStartedAtMs:nowMs(),terminal:false,terminalCode:null,retryableAtMs:null}, pendingTarget:null, pendingQuarantineWrite:null, quarantineKey:null, and liveReset:{operationToken:T,status:"ready-to-claim",failedStoreIds:[]}. Write it to hanks-hits-progress-owner, read it back, and compare the canonical record byte-for-byte. This is marker B before B1. Every later recovery attempt first increments and persists this marker recovery budget, reads marker B back byte-for-byte, and only then touches a target or quarantine record. The persisted marker budget, pending target, pending quarantine payload, live-reset ledger, terminal code, and retry deadline remain enforceable after reload even when quarantine record or index writes fail.
  3. Validate both old-owner journals, then enumerate every dynamic delivery payload from the validated ledger and staging evidence in sorted appId,logicalSaveId order. For each payload, use old owner A and the B freeze barrier, write/read marker B with its exact pendingTarget, call validatePendingStorageRemoval(markerB,pendingTarget,"foreign-purge"), narrow StorageRemovalValidation.ok:true, remove only validatedRemoval.target.key, require null read-back, and clear/read marker B before the next payload. No static ledger key is deleted before this enumeration completes. A thrown, silent no-op, missing payload, unknown key, or ledger/staging mismatch creates the exact redacted quarantine record, retains marker B as quarantined, and blocks READY and transport.
  4. After every dynamic payload is verified absent, remove the static owner-control targets in this exact order: hanks-hits-progress-delivery-ledger-v1, then hanks-hits-progress-delivery-staging-v1, then SIGNOUT_BROADCAST_KEY. Each operation writes/read-backs marker B, validates the generated target with "foreign-purge", removes only the narrowed validated key, requires null read-back, and clears/read-backs the pending target before advancing. A thrown or silent no-op creates the typed quarantine record and stops the sequence.
  5. After all owner-control targets are verified absent, remove each manifest target in generated order A1 through A32, primary before legacy, with the same validate-marker-remove-read-back-clear sequence. The final two checkpoints are Chess legacy and A32, so owner.purge.order-crash-recovery injects a crash after every boundary, reloads the persisted pendingTarget, and proves that verified earlier absence is not repeated as an unsafe blind delete and that no later target is skipped.
  6. Construct a bounded redacted QuarantineRecordV1 from the validated generated target before any quarantine write: namespace=quarantineNamespace(B), quarantineKey=namespace+":"+target.targetDigest, owner=B, target, targetKey=target.key, targetDigest=target.targetDigest, the typed failure code, digest-and-length evidence only, retry number, recovery start, record time, and lastReadBack:{status:"not-attempted",digest:null,byteLength:null}. The record has no raw foreign bytes. Stable serialization must be at most MAX_QUARANTINE_RECORD_BYTES=8192. Read and validate the current index, set indexPayload={version:1,owner:B,namespace:quarantineNamespace(B),quarantineKeys:[...currentKeys,record.quarantineKey].sort()}, and enforce MAX_QUARANTINE_RECORDS=64 and MAX_QUARANTINE_BYTES=1048576. For every pending payload, payloadDigest=sha256(canonicalJson(payload)) and payloadByteLength=byteLength(UTF-8(canonicalJson(payload))); both are recomputed on read-back and bounded by MAX_PENDING_QUARANTINE_PAYLOAD_BYTES=8192. Before writing the index, persist marker B with pendingTarget:{owner:B,key:QUARANTINE_INDEX_KEY,targetKey:QUARANTINE_INDEX_KEY,targetDigest:sha256(UTF-8(QUARANTINE_INDEX_KEY)),kind:"quarantine-index",operation:"write-and-verify"} and a matching pendingQuarantineWrite:{kind:"quarantine-index",key:QUARANTINE_INDEX_KEY,owner:B,namespace,payload:indexPayload,payloadDigest,payloadByteLength}; read the marker back byte-for-byte and validate the payload digest, byte length, owner, namespace, and exact key before the primary index write. Write the index and require canonical byte-for-byte read-back; a failure maps to quarantine_index_write_failed or quarantine_index_readback_failed. Before writing the record, persist marker B with quarantineKey equal to the exact record key, pendingTarget:{owner:B,key:record.quarantineKey,targetKey:record.targetKey,targetDigest:record.targetDigest,kind:"quarantine-record",operation:"write-and-verify"}, and matching pendingQuarantineWrite:{kind:"quarantine-record",key:record.quarantineKey,owner:B,namespace,payload:record,payloadDigest,payloadByteLength}; read it back and validate the same invariants, then write the record and require canonical byte-for-byte read-back. A record write/read-back failure maps to quarantine_record_write_failed or quarantine_record_readback_failed. Only after the corresponding primary write and read-back succeed are both pending fields cleared and read back; quarantineKey remains the exact record key until quarantine cleanup. If the marker cannot durably contain the matching pending payload, retain marker B as quarantined with quarantine_payload_missing, never activate, and require the typed fail-closed recovery path. The namespace and pending payload are bounded; a missing record referenced by the index is an incomplete purge and remains listed for recovery.
  7. On recovery, first increment and persist marker B's RecoveryBudgetV1, read marker B back byte-for-byte, and resolve a non-null pendingTarget before reading the quarantine index. For operation:"remove-and-verify", require pendingQuarantineWrite:null, reread marker B, compute const authorization = pendingRemovalAuthorization(pendingTarget,markerB.liveReset), reject a quarantine key unless that authorization is "recovery-cleanup" after a complete reset, call validatePendingStorageRemoval(markerB,pendingTarget,authorization), require ok:true with the full owner, key, target key, digest, kind, namespace, app ID, and scope binding, declare const validatedRemoval:StorageRemovalValidation, narrow its ok:true branch, and use validatedRemoval.target, call removeItem(validatedRemoval.target.key), require a null read-back of that same validated key, clear pendingTarget, and read marker B back byte-for-byte. For operation:"write-and-verify", require a present PendingQuarantineWriteV1 whose key, kind, owner, namespace, payload digest, and UTF-8 byte length exactly match the pending target and canonical payload. If that durable payload is absent or mismatched, emit terminal quarantine_payload_missing, retain marker B as quarantined, perform no write or delete, and never activate. Otherwise write the exact bounded payload, require canonical byte-for-byte read-back, clear both pending fields, and read marker B back byte-for-byte. A failed pending operation retains marker B as quarantined and cannot proceed. Then read QUARANTINE_INDEX_KEY and run validateQuarantineIndexForOwner; sort is not a repair operation, so a non-canonical order is a terminal validation failure. For every listed key require a valid byte-for-byte QuarantineRecordV1 and run validateQuarantineRecordForOwner. Before deleting that record's target, create pendingTarget:{owner:B,key:record.targetKey,targetKey:record.targetKey,targetDigest:record.targetDigest,kind:record.target.kind,operation:"remove-and-verify"}, reread marker B, compute the same typed authorization, call validatePendingStorageRemoval(markerB,pendingTarget,authorization), require ok:true plus owner, key, target key, target digest, kind, namespace, app ID, and scope equality with the validated record, declare const validatedRemoval:StorageRemovalValidation, narrow its ok:true branch, and use validatedRemoval.target, call removeItem(validatedRemoval.target.key), and require a null read-back of that same validated key; this owner-guarded second removal covers both the delivery ledger and sign-out broadcast. A thrown or silent no-op target removal creates a retryable failure, retains the record/index and marker B as quarantined, and stops. The named owner.recovery.control-key-thrown and owner.recovery.control-key-silent-noop cases cover both control keys. A tampered index or record, a forbidden target, a mismatched owner, or a namespace mismatch creates terminal purge_target_invalid or quarantine_owner_mismatch; no deletion follows. If a listed record is absent or invalid, create terminal quarantine_record_missing, retain the index key and marker B as quarantined, and stop; the missing record is never pruned and never permits active. After every listed record has been read back valid and its generated target has been read back absent, inspect liveReset. A ready-to-claim ledger is changed to claimed and read back before calling resetAllLiveStoresWithoutPersist(T) once. The helper attempts all 32 store APIs without persistence and returns exact failed IDs. On all-success, write marker B with liveReset:{operationToken:T,status:"complete",failedStoreIds:[]} and read it back before cleanup. A claimed ledger after reload is terminal live_reset_claim_ambiguous and is never called again; a failed ledger is terminal store_reset_failed. A reset failure retains marker B as quarantined, its recovery budget, index, records, and failed IDs, emits store-reset-failed, and forces reload after sign-out; owner.recovery.reset-failure covers this branch. After a complete reset, for each quarantine record create a pending target with key=record.quarantineKey, targetKey=record.targetKey, targetDigest=record.targetDigest, kind:"quarantine-record", and operation:"remove-and-verify"; reread marker B, call validatePendingStorageRemoval(markerB,pendingTarget,"recovery-cleanup") (which invokes validateQuarantineStorageKey), require ok:true, narrow StorageRemovalValidation, use validatedRemoval.target, call removeItem(validatedRemoval.target.key), and require a null read-back of that same validated record key. Then persist an empty owner-bound index with its matching pending payload, read it back, create the exact index removal target with key=targetKey=QUARANTINE_INDEX_KEY and targetDigest=sha256(UTF-8(QUARANTINE_INDEX_KEY)), reread marker B, call validatePendingStorageRemoval(markerB,pendingTarget,"recovery-cleanup"), require ok:true, narrow StorageRemovalValidation, use validatedRemoval.target, call removeItem(validatedRemoval.target.key), and require a null index read-back. Clear quarantineKey only after all records and the index are absent. Only then write marker B with state active, recovery:null, pendingTarget:null, pendingQuarantineWrite:null, quarantineKey:null, and liveReset:null. Continue the specified owner adoption using B without another generation or epoch increment. On failure, retain marker B with state quarantined, retain the index and record when their write/read-back succeeded, and remain blocked. Explicit device-forget uses the separately authorized typed sequence: every quarantine record and index deletion is validated and read back before the owner-marker deletion; missing evidence remains terminal and cannot reach READY. No path treats missing quarantine evidence as purge success.

Reset ledger persistence is exact. When resetAllLiveStoresWithoutPersist(T) returns any failed app IDs, the coordinator writes marker B with liveReset:{operationToken:T,status:"failed",failedStoreIds} and requires byte-for-byte read-back before returning the quarantined result. If that failure-state write or read-back fails, marker B remains claimed, the failure is terminal store_reset_failed, and forced reload is required; recovery never calls the reset helper again for T. A reload that reads status:"failed" returns the stored failed IDs, emits no second reset event, and cannot write active. A reload that reads status:"complete" skips the helper and proceeds only with validated quarantine cleanup.

Owner-transition recovery uses one fixed schedule window [0ms,25ms,100ms,500ms,2000ms], a persisted MAX_RECOVERY_ATTEMPTS=20, and MAX_RECOVERY_AGE_MS=86400000. Every attempt increments and read-backs RecoveryBudgetV1 before target access. Retryable failures persist terminal:false,terminalCode:null,retryableAtMs:nextAbsoluteTime; terminal failures persist terminal:true,terminalCode:OwnerRecoveryTerminalCode,retryableAtMs:null, with the exact code copied to the emitted failure and marker. Exceeding the attempt cap emits recovery_attempt_limit; reaching the age cap emits recovery_age_limit. One shared recoveryPromise coalesces storage, visibility, online, and manual triggers, and no trigger sends network traffic before purge success. Explicit device-forget validates every quarantine record and index with validatePendingStorageRemoval(markerB,pendingTarget,"device-forget"), removes only validatedRemoval.target.key with null read-back, writes the exact owner-marker pending target into B and reads it back, calls validateOwnerMarkerRemoval(B,pendingTarget,"device-forget"), declares const validatedRemoval:OwnerMarkerRemovalValidation, requires ok:true, narrows the union, removes validatedRemoval.target.key, and requires a null read-back. A throw or silent no-op retains the marker and remains blocked.

The coordinator stores the metadata-only ledger under hanks-hits-progress-delivery-ledger-v1 and the journal under hanks-hits-progress-delivery-staging-v1. Neither carries canonical progress. The first successful source mutation commits the journal, payload, ledger, and read-backs in that order, with firstAttemptAtMs=nowMs(), cumulativeDeliveryAttempts=1, currentDeliveryAttempt=1, terminal:"pending", and terminalCode:null. Every retry updates the journal, payload, and ledger with the incremented count, current request ID, delivery attempt, body hash, merge mode, and base server version before scheduling. A visibility, online, authentication, manual, or pagehide trigger rereads both journals and every referenced payload and never resets first-attempt time, cumulative attempts, terminal state, or logical save identity. A new source mutation receives a new logical save ID and a new budget, but allocation fails with PendingDeliveryCapacityExceeded at 64 before the source setter. A successful POST marks the ledger entry acknowledged, removes the payload and staging entry through validated old-owner targets, then removes and verifies the ledger and staging keys; only then is the in-memory entry cleared. A journal, payload, or ledger write/read-back failure marks terminal:"ledger_unavailable",terminalCode:"delivery_ledger_unavailable", sends no network request, and leaves the coordinator blocked. Permanent 400, 401, 404, and 409 responses become rejected entries; an exhausted entry remains inspectable until a new source mutation or owner transition.

progress-sync-diagnostics.ts defines the redacted client event union for hydration, owner-claim, purge, quarantine, adoption, transport, rollback, recovery, and store-reset-failed. Each event contains request/application correlation when available, owner fingerprint, owner epoch/generation, target digest, delivery/recovery attempt, injected timestamp, and terminal reason, never raw progress or raw user ID. A store-reset-failed event additionally contains the exact failed ValidAppId[] and count, never state values. The browser sink is a bounded 256-event ring buffer; emit is synchronous, nonblocking, catches its own serialization/storage errors, and cannot change a state transition. The server sink uses the same no-throw rule and a bounded 256-event request buffer; its event type contains ownerFingerprint, never userId. Diagnostics are flushed after commit/rollback and are never awaited inside DML. diagnostics.redaction.owner-fingerprint serializes every browser and server event and asserts that an authenticated subject string cannot occur in the event JSON. Tests also assert event ordering and reconstruct a terminal quarantine from one redacted record without a network dependency.

4.3 One source-executable mutation API

The removed legacy names notifyMutation and runSourceMutation both map to commitSourceMutation; no caller imports the old names. commitSourceMutation is the only public source mutation seam. A generated wrapper is inserted into every production store constructor and every source action that mutates a partialized field. The wrapper also guards direct store actions and direct setState calls through the store API identity. The generated binding ledger records app, store API, action name, affected PathKeys, source clock read, metadata write, and completion boundary.

type MutationScopeToken = string & { readonly __mutationScopeToken: unique symbol };
type MutationExecutionLock = {
  appId: ValidAppId;
  queueTail: Promise<void>;
  activeScope: MutationScope | null;
};
type MutationScopeHandle = {
  readonly token: MutationScopeToken;
  readonly appId: ValidAppId;
  readonly owner: OwnerTuple;
  commitNested<T>(input: Omit<CommitSourceMutationInput<T>, "parentScope">): Promise<T>;
};
type CommitSourceMutationInput<T> = {
  appId: ValidAppId;
  actionId: string;
  sourceMutation: (scope: MutationScopeHandle) => T | Promise<T>;
  completion: MutationCompletion;
  parentScope: MutationScopeHandle | null;
};
type MutationScope = {
  token: MutationScopeToken;
  appId: ValidAppId;
  ownerAtStart: OwnerTuple;
  depth: number;
  activeAsyncCompletions: number;
  outerMutationId: string;
  outerLogicalSaveId: string;
  fullStateSnapshot: unknown;
  completion: MutationCompletion;
  actionResult: unknown;
  status: "active" | "aborted" | "committed";
};
type MutationCompletion =
  | { kind: "sync" }
  | { kind: "awaited" }
  | { kind: "delayed"; scheduler: "setTimeout"; delayMs: number; resolveAfter: "final-persisted-setter"; cancelOnOwnerChange: true };
type CommittedMutation = {
  mutationId: string;
  logicalSaveId: string;
  canonicalBodyHash: string;
  metadata: SyncMetadataV1;
  committedAtMs: number;
  persistedByteChange: boolean;
};
type SourceMutationResult<T> = {
  actionResult: T;
  committedMutation: CommittedMutation | null;
};
declare function commitSourceMutation<T>(input: CommitSourceMutationInput<T>): Promise<SourceMutationResult<T>>;

Each app has one MutationExecutionLock held by the coordinator. A top-level call has parentScope:null, waits for that app's FIFO queueTail, creates a fresh MutationScopeToken, records the current owner tuple, and owns the lock until its completion, persist, journal, and notice work is finished. A second top-level call for the same app cannot enter or be mistaken for a nested call while an async action is pending; it queues behind the first and receives its own token, snapshot, mutation ID, logical save ID, and notice. Calls for different apps may run concurrently. Only a generated action that receives the active MutationScopeHandle may call scope.commitNested; that method checks the token, app ID, owner tuple, and status:"active", increments depth and activeAsyncCompletions, and joins the existing outer scope. A scope token is never inferred from a module-global variable, promise timing, or an action name. Owner change, cancellation, throw, or lock release invalidates the token. provenance.concurrent-async-actions starts two delayed actions for one app, proves FIFO independent IDs and notices, starts one different-app action concurrently, and verifies interleaved completion cannot join, roll back, or resurrect the wrong scope.

The exact ordering is: read and validate both the staging journal and delivery ledger plus the owner barrier; require their sorted identity lists to match, and if the staging journal or delivery ledger has MAX_PENDING_LOGICAL_SAVES=64 entries, throw PendingDeliveryCapacityExceeded before invoking sourceMutation; snapshot the full state and the exact prior local envelope; allocate mutationId, logicalSaveId, and injected clock value; enter one outer MutationScope that buffers all store persist writes; run the source action; await the action for kind:"awaited" or until the delayed completion promise resolves for kind:"delayed"; read the resulting full state; derive canonical data and metadata; validate; if no declared persisted data or metadata changed, discard the buffer and publish nothing; otherwise build the request payload and exact staging entry, write and read back the staging journal with localPersistState:"pending" plus the prior-envelope digest/length/bytes, write and read back the payload, call the real store persist write once, verify the owner tuple and raw local-envelope byte read-back, update and read back the same staging entry with localPersistState:"verified", write and read back the delivery ledger by read-modify-write, then remove and read back only the matching staging entry before publishing one mutation notice. The staging entry names the local envelope until the local write is verified, so recovery can retry or quarantine each crash boundary without guessing whether local state was durable. If the local persist write throws, returns a silent no-op, or reads absent or different bytes, mark that entry localPersistState:"unknown", read it back, restore the full live snapshot with rollbackFullWithoutPersist, and persist a bounded LocalPersistRepairV1 pending quarantine record containing the generated primary key, expected envelope digest/length, prior envelope digest/length, and observed failure; the raw prior envelope remains only in the bounded staging entry. Attempt repair exactly once: if prior bytes exist in that staging entry, write them to that validated primary key and require exact read-back; if prior bytes are null, remove that validated primary key through validatePendingStorageRemoval(markerCurrent,pendingTarget,"local-persist-repair") and require null read-back. On verified repair, remove only the matching payload, remove only the matching entry from the staging journal, remove only the matching entry from the delivery ledger, read back all remaining entries byte-for-byte, and remove a static journal key only when its remaining entry list is empty. Unrelated pending entries and their payloads remain untouched. On failed repair, retain the local-persist record, marker B, and the matching staging/ledger evidence as quarantined; no network, notice, READY, or reset occurs. A no-op action that calls no state setter returns {actionResult,committedMutation:null}. A successful setter that produces byte-identical canonical data still publishes a new mutation because the ID, metadata clock, and request body changed. An action that throws before completion rejects and publishes nothing. If a direct raw seam writes before throwing, the wrapper reads back the expected bytes: confirmed bytes publish the committed mutation with persistedByteChange; absent or different bytes use the same per-entry repair path. A raw storage throw or silent no-op produces no success notice.

Nested source actions join the current outer MutationScope only through the explicit MutationScopeHandle.commitNested token. The outermost call owns the one snapshot, mutation ID, logical save ID, metadata clock, staging entry, persist write, and notice, and awaits every nested promise before final projection. A nested call increments depth and activeAsyncCompletions, runs its action against the same buffered state, allocates no ID and emits no notice, and returns its action's exact T value to its caller. A nested throw marks status:"aborted", propagates the original error through every caller, restores the outer full snapshot, clears only the outer matching staged bytes through the per-entry repair sequence, and emits neither a persist write nor a notice. A call without the active parent token is a new queued top-level call, never an inferred nested call. The outermost commitSourceMutation promise resolves as SourceMutationResult<T>={actionResult,committedMutation} after the final successful completion boundary; a no-op has committedMutation:null, while the generated store action unwraps and returns actionResult to its existing caller. Direct store actions, generated wrappers, and callers use this same generic contract. provenance.nested-action-return asserts return-value preservation, one allocation for three nested setters, rollback after a nested throw, and no notice from the inner calls.

Retries keep mutation ID, logical save ID, body hash, owner tuple, and merge mode; they allocate a new request ID and delivery attempt. Exact duplicate responses are ignored after one adoption. Replayed source actions allocate new IDs and notices. Guest mutations retain notices for first-login transfer. Hydration, GET adoption, POST adoption, rollback, merge rebase, and retry bookkeeping do not call commitSourceMutation. Drawing's saveArtwork and updateArtwork become awaited actions: the thumbnail promise resolves first, then the final savedArtworks setter receives one clock, metadata update, persist write, and notice. Memory Match flipCard becomes a completion-aware action: the matching branch owns its setTimeout(TIMING.MATCH_CELEBRATION_MS), returns a promise, and resolves only after the final winning progress setter; the no-match branch resolves after its delayed runtime-only setter. The wrapper holds the persistence buffer during each delayed callback, allocates one metadata/notice only at the completion setter, and cancels the callback when the owner tuple changes. Initial card-flip and processing setters never publish a persisted notice. mutation.memory-match-delayed-match proves a delayed win persists exactly once and cannot resurrect after an owner change. A thumbnail or data URL above MAX_DRAWING_BLOB_BYTES=65536 throws PersistedProjectionTooLarge before the setter, restores the previous artwork bytes, writes no persist envelope, sends no network request, and leaves READY unchanged. No chunking path exists. drawing.payload-envelope-boundary covers 65535, 65536, 65537, and the full 196608-byte envelope boundary. Thumbnail failure leaves the previous artwork bytes and publishes no new artwork mutation.

Achievements watermarks currently change in reportProgress while getProgress omits them. The implementation removes watermarks from the persisted synchronized partializer and marks them runtime/local-only in the manifest. The achievement unlock mutation is committed after the unlock record changes. Watermark updates never receive a sync metadata entry and never enter the request, canonical response, DB, or merge. Tests reload achievements, report a watermark without an unlock, and assert no server mutation, then add an unlock and assert one notice at completion.

On achievement store creation, watermarks starts at the existing empty-watermark value. After all 32 coordinator registrations reach a valid local/server baseline, the one achievement observer replays each canonical progress snapshot in manifest order to rebuild watermarks in memory. A reload therefore restores behavior without putting watermarks in account data. The observer is one coordinator subscription, not a second save loop.

broadcastVerifiedSignOut(guestMarker) is the sole sign-out broadcast writer. After the guest marker is active and read back, every reset and account-control removal is complete, and before the successful SignOutResult, it serializes SignOutBroadcastV1 with a fresh generated eventId, the exact guest OwnerTuple, matching ownerFingerprint, the read-back markerSequence, and injected issuedAtMs to stable JSON. It writes that JSON to SIGNOUT_BROADCAST_KEY, reads the same key, and requires byte-for-byte equality. A thrown write, silent no-op, or read-back mismatch enters QUARANTINED, sends no broadcast, exposes no guest READY, and returns the typed quarantined result. A receiver parses the storage event, validates version, kind, event ID, tuple/fingerprint equality, finite timestamp, and sequence, rereads the owner marker, and accepts the event only when the marker is active guest with the same tuple and markerSequence >= event.markerSequence. It ignores stale, foreign, malformed, duplicate, and lower-sequence events, records eventId once, then calls the coordinator's one force-reload path; it never resets a store or removes a raw key directly. owner.signout-broadcast-wire covers writer order, read-back failure, stale tab, foreign tuple, duplicate event, and reload.

5. Attempt lineage, adoption, and client wire builders

5.1 Full attempt identity and baselines

type MergeMode = "replace" | "merge";
type AttemptIdentity = {
  owner: OwnerTuple;
  ownerLeaseId: string;
  ownerEpoch: number;
  ownerGeneration: number;
  mutationId: string;
  logicalSaveId: string;
  requestId: string;
  canonicalBodyHash: string;
  mergeMode: MergeMode;
  baseServerVersion: string | null;
  deliveryAttempt: number;
};
type InitialGetAttemptIdentity = {
  kind: "initial-get";
  owner: OwnerTuple;
  ownerLeaseId: string | null;
  ownerEpoch: number;
  ownerGeneration: number;
  mutationId: null;
  logicalSaveId: null;
  requestId: string;
  canonicalBodyHash: null;
  mergeMode: null;
  baseServerVersion: null;
  deliveryAttempt: number;
};
type ReconcileGetAttemptIdentity = {
  kind: "reconcile-get";
  owner: OwnerTuple;
  ownerLeaseId: string;
  ownerEpoch: number;
  ownerGeneration: number;
  mutationId: string;
  logicalSaveId: string;
  requestId: string;
  canonicalBodyHash: string;
  mergeMode: MergeMode;
  baseServerVersion: string | null;
  deliveryAttempt: number;
};
type GetAttemptIdentity = InitialGetAttemptIdentity | ReconcileGetAttemptIdentity;
type ResponseAttemptIdentity = AttemptIdentity | GetAttemptIdentity;
type BaselineKind = "get-server" | "post-acknowledged";
type ServerBaseline<C> = {
  kind: BaselineKind;
  serverVersion: string | null;
  canonical: CanonicalServerData<C> | null;
  sync: SyncMetadataV1 | null;
  capturedBy: ResponseAttemptIdentity | null;
};
type InFlightAttempt<C> = {
  identity: AttemptIdentity;
  body: RequestProgressData<C>;
  getBaseline: ServerBaseline<C> | null;
  acknowledgedBaseline: ServerBaseline<C> | null;
};

Identity comparison serializes all fields in the order shown, with the owner tuple compared as a nested canonical object and ownerLeaseId compared immediately after it. The repeated ownerEpoch and ownerGeneration fields must equal the tuple fields; a mismatch is a stale response. baseServerVersion is the exact 64-character server version used for replace/merge selection, or literal null for an absent baseline; it is part of identity even when the body bytes match. The canonical body hash is lowercase SHA-256 over stable JSON of canonical data plus sync, excluding request IDs and delivery attempt. logicalSaveId remains stable across retry. requestId, deliveryAttempt, and ownerLeaseId identify one delivery authority. Merge mode is part of identity. A claim GET has null ownerLeaseId until its response installs a signed lease; it cannot create a POST identity before that response.

A successful initial GET creates only a get-server baseline captured by InitialGetAttemptIdentity. A reconcile GET uses ReconcileGetAttemptIdentity and carries non-null mutation, logical-save, hash, and merge fields plus the nullable baseServerVersion; it still creates only a GET baseline. Neither GET form marks a POST request acknowledged. A successful POST creates a post-acknowledged baseline from the exact AttemptIdentity in the response and the response's canonical server data. The coordinator keeps both records until the next source mutation. If a source mutation S1 commits while POST P0 is in flight, P0 may install its canonical server baseline non-persistingly, then S1 is rebased against that baseline and sent under a new mutation/logical-save identity and its captured base version. P0 cannot clear S1. Adoption is permitted only when the response's complete ResponseAttemptIdentity exactly equals the current expected GET or delivery identity and its owner tuple is current. A retry is eligible only after its ledger makes that retry the current delivery; a prior delivery's response remains late and is ignored even when logical save ID, body hash, and base version match. A replay with the same complete current identity is idempotent. A response with a different request ID, delivery attempt, mutation ID, logical save ID, body hash, merge mode, base server version, owner epoch, or owner generation is ignored and logged.

TestScenarioRequired result
lineage.late-attempt.test.tsP0 response arrives after P1 for a newer mutation.P0 is ignored; P1 remains current and local S1 survives.
lineage.s1-during-flight.test.tsS1 commits while P0 is in flight.P0 becomes GET-like server baseline only; S1 is rebased and delivered with a new full identity.
lineage.get-reconcile.test.tsGET returns server baseline during a queued local mutation.GET baseline is stored separately; POST acknowledgement remains false until an exact POST response.
lineage.replay-retry.test.tsSame response is replayed, then retry has a new request ID.Replay adopts once; retry is accepted only with same body, logical save, mutation, owner, and merge mode.

5.2 Exact client transport contracts

All generated IDs use ^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._:-]{0,127}$. Hashes use exactly 64 lowercase hexadecimal characters. Owner fingerprints use the same 64-character lowercase digest grammar and never contain the raw subject. Owner generation and epoch are unsigned decimal integers. Document and lease tokens use the same ID grammar. App IDs are checked against the generated 32-entry registry before typed conversion. Stable JSON is UTF-8 and produces the body hash.
