MethodURL and queryHeaders/bodyResponse contract
GET/api/progress/${encodeURIComponent(appId)}?wireVersion=1&requestId=${requestId}&applicationCorrelation=${applicationCorrelation}&ownerFingerprint=${ownerFingerprint}&ownerGeneration=${ownerGeneration}&ownerEpoch=${ownerEpoch}&documentToken=${documentToken}&leaseToken=${leaseToken}&ownerLeaseMode=${ownerLease.mode}&ownerLease=${base64url(stableJson(ownerLease))}&deliveryAttempt=1&reconcile=false&logicalSaveId=null&mutationId=null&canonicalBodyHash=null&mergeMode=null&baseServerVersion=nullNo body. Accept: application/json plus X-Progress-Request-Id, X-Progress-Application-Correlation, X-Progress-Owner-Fingerprint, X-Progress-Owner-Generation, X-Progress-Owner-Epoch, X-Progress-Document-Token, X-Progress-Lease-Token, and X-Progress-Delivery-Attempt.ProgressGetResponseV1 with wireVersion:1, success,appId,data,sync,serverVersion,updatedAt,lastSyncedAt,ownerLease,correlation, or typed error. A reconcile request sets reconcile=true and fills all four reconcile lineage fields and base version.
Normal POST/api/progress/${encodeURIComponent(appId)}?wireVersion=1, with no lineage query fields.Content-Type: application/json, Accept: application/json, all exact X-Progress-* lineage headers including X-Progress-Owner-Lease containing base64url(stableJson(ownerLease)), and exact body {wireVersion:1,data,sync,merge,logicalSaveId,mutationId,requestId,canonicalBodyHash,owner,ownerLease,deliveryAttempt,transport:"fetch",baseServerVersion}.ProgressPostResponseV1 with wireVersion:1,success,appId,data,sync,serverVersion,updatedAt,lastSyncedAt,merged,conflicts,ownerLease,correlation. Response echoes request ID, hash, and the server-authoritative lease ID.
Beacon POSTSame path with ?wireVersion=1&transport=beacon and every correlation, owner, hash, merge, delivery, ownerLeaseMode, and base64url-encoded ownerLease field in the query. Query values are URL encoded once.No custom headers. Body is the exact POST JSON in new Blob([stableJson(body)], {type:"application/json"}). The body contains the same structured ownerLease proof as the query. sendBeacon false means no acknowledgement.There is no synchronous response. A later GET reconcile uses the same logical save, body hash, and server lease ID. Beacon failures remain retryable only while the owner tuple and authoritative lease are current.
DELETE/api/progress/${encodeURIComponent(appId)}?wireVersion=1&transport=delete&requestId=${requestId}&applicationCorrelation=${applicationCorrelation}&ownerFingerprint=${ownerFingerprint}&ownerGeneration=${ownerGeneration}&ownerEpoch=${ownerEpoch}&documentToken=${documentToken}&leaseToken=${leaseToken}&ownerLeaseMode=use&ownerLease=${base64url(stableJson(ownerLease))}&deliveryAttempt=1No body. Accept: application/json and the exact GET correlation/owner headers. The DELETE proof must be mode:"use"; merge, body hash, and mutation fields are absent.ProgressDeleteResponseV1 with wireVersion:1,success,appId,deleted,ownerLease,correlation; absent row is typed 404 after the shared advisory-lock transaction reads no row, with no DELETE DML.

A recovery probe is a distinct GET wire variant on the same route: it carries ownerLeaseMode=recover, a base64url proof with mode:"recover", lease:null, a valid recoveryNonce, a non-null expectedOwnerFingerprint, a non-null recoveryReason, and the requested owner tuple in the query identity fields. It never attempts an app read. Its success body is ProgressOwnerRecoveryResponseV1: authority.kind:"authority-absent" has lease:null and causes exactly one subsequent claim with a distinct durable claimNonce; authority.kind:"authority-present" carries the authoritative or freshly recovered lease and all replay flags. A recovery response with no authority is a successful typed result, not a 4xx error. The subsequent claim is a second explicit owner-authority GET variant described by ProgressOwnerClaimQueryV1; its success body is ProgressOwnerClaimResponseV1 with authority.kind:"authority-present", the persisted lease, and the requested tuple. A claim inserts only after the recovery probe observed authority-absent; an identical persisted claim nonce and full tuple returns the same lease with replayed:true and no epoch increment, while a non-replay claim against an existing row returns claim_nonce_reuse or owner_mismatch. Claim proofs carry recoveryReason:null, a valid claim nonce, and a null lease; use proofs carry both nonce fields and a null recovery reason. The route dispatches recovery and claim variants before app-lock acquisition and does not reinterpret the two results as a normal GET baseline.

The normal POST header map is exact: Accept, Content-Type, X-Progress-Request-Id, X-Progress-Application-Correlation, X-Progress-Owner-Fingerprint, X-Progress-Owner-Generation, X-Progress-Owner-Epoch, X-Progress-Document-Token, X-Progress-Lease-Token, X-Progress-Owner-Lease, X-Progress-Mutation-Id, X-Progress-Logical-Save-Id, X-Progress-Delivery-Attempt, X-Progress-Canonical-Hash, and X-Progress-Merge-Mode. GET and DELETE use the same owner headers; GET carries its owner proof in exact query fields, while DELETE carries the mode:"use" proof in exact query fields. Beacon query fields are the URL-encoded equivalents of those omitted headers plus wireVersion=1 and transport=beacon. base64url(stableJson(ownerLease)) uses URL-safe alphabet with no padding and is decoded exactly once before schema validation. No header is silently dropped by a builder. Responses set Referrer-Policy: no-referrer; owner fingerprints, document tokens, lease tokens, signed lease bytes, and hashes are never logged as raw values or placed in browser history beyond the required request URL.

POST parsing requires header/body equality: request ID, mutation ID, logical save ID, delivery attempt, canonical hash, merge mode, every owner tuple field, and the decoded owner-lease proof must match. A claim proof is allowed on the initial owner-acquisition request or an exact persisted claim replay; a non-replay claim against an existing authority row is rejected before app access. All delivery POSTs and DELETEs use an unexpired signed proof. X-Progress-Merge-Mode is merge when the JSON boolean is true and replace when false. The server recomputes the body hash over data and sync and rejects a mismatch before transaction creation. Beacon query values, including ownerLeaseMode and base64url proof bytes, must match the JSON body by the same rules.

A GET with reconcile=false sends real non-empty request, application, owner-fingerprint, document, and lease values, plus the literal query token null for nullable logical-save, mutation, hash, merge, and base-server-version fields. A GET with reconcile=true sends the current attempted identity in ProgressGetQueryV1; the response echoes the full owner tuple and lineage identity for matching and creates only a get-server baseline. It never turns a beacon or POST into an acknowledged request.

GET, beacon, and DELETE query parsers require one instance of every declared key, lowercase boolean literals true/false, the literal token null only for typed nullable fields, and no unknown or duplicate keys. The owner proof query has exactly one of three forms: ownerLeaseMode=claim with recoveryReason:null, a null lease, and a valid claim nonce; ownerLeaseMode=recover with a null lease, a valid recovery nonce, a non-null recovery reason, and the expected owner fingerprint; or ownerLeaseMode=use with a base64url stable-JSON lease, both nonce fields null, and a null recovery reason. A mismatch is 400. The GET parser maps reconcile=false and all nullable lineage fields to InitialGetAttemptIdentity; it maps reconcile=true and non-null lineage fields to ReconcileGetAttemptIdentity, rejecting a partial combination. A recovery-mode GET maps to the owner-recovery route variant and never to an app read. A percent-decoded empty app ID, encoded slash, duplicate request ID, malformed numeric field, invalid signature, or non-canonical hash is 400 before any DB call.

type JsonPathSegment = string | number;
type RawJsonDuplicateError = {
  code: "duplicate_json_key";
  path: readonly JsonPathSegment[];
  key: string;
  firstRawKey: string;
  duplicateRawKey: string;
  firstOrdinal: number;
  duplicateOrdinal: number;
};
type RawJsonScan =
  | { kind: "empty-body"; rawText: ""; byteLength: 0; objectKeyOccurrences: readonly [] }
  | { kind: "json"; value: unknown; rawText: string; byteLength: number; objectKeyOccurrences: readonly { path: readonly JsonPathSegment[]; key: string; rawKey: string; ordinal: number }[] };
declare function readAndScanJsonBody(request: Request, maxBytes: number): Promise<RawJsonScan>;

readAndScanJsonBody never calls request.text() or request.json(). It parses Content-Length before reading: a missing header selects streaming mode, a decimal integer above maxBytes returns typed body_too_large without allocation, and a malformed, negative, fractional, or duplicate header returns 400. It reads request.body with one reader, adds each chunk's byte length before appending, cancels the reader immediately when the accumulated UTF-8 bytes exceed maxBytes, and rejects a declared/received byte-length mismatch. It decodes with fatal UTF-8, treats zero bytes as the typed {kind:"empty-body",rawText:"",byteLength:0,objectKeyOccurrences:[]}, and never materializes more than maxBytes plus one chunk. A recursive-descent tokenizer then consumes RFC 8259 whitespace, strings, escapes, numbers, booleans, nulls, arrays, and objects. It decodes every JSON member name before duplicate comparison, so escaped and unescaped spellings with the same semantic key collide; it retains raw spelling only in the occurrence ledger and diagnostic. Each object owns a map keyed by decoded semantic name and a first-ordinal record; a repeated key at the same object path throws RawJsonDuplicateError with both decoded identity and raw spellings before JSON.parse, including top-level, nested, and array-element duplicates. The scanner rejects malformed UTF-16 escapes, invalid numbers, unterminated strings, trailing tokens, and a non-object POST root. Only after the scan reaches end-of-input does it call JSON.parse(rawText) and return the typed kind:"json" result with value, raw text, raw UTF-8 byte length, and occurrence ledger. The server applies the same capped stream reader to fetch and beacon POST bodies, so duplicate preservation and size limits cannot depend on a framework's collapsed object. wire.raw-duplicate-body asserts escaped/unescaped duplicates at top level, nested objects, and array elements; exact-limit acceptance; plus-one rejection; chunked input; declared-length mismatch; empty body; and one stream-reader pass for both transports.

Client parsers reject an empty POST body, omitted or non-boolean merge, unknown app ID, wrong content type, malformed IDs, owner mismatch, hash mismatch, duplicate body fields, invalid sync, missing wireVersion:1, and response correlation mismatch. GET and DELETE have no body; a non-empty body is a 400. The exact limits are MAX_ENVELOPE_BYTES=196608, MAX_POST_PROTOCOL_OVERHEAD_BYTES=65536, and MAX_REQUEST_BYTES=262144. The raw preflight correlation and transport decision run first; then the method-specific authentication, quota, and app checks in the ordering table run before the POST parser checks content type and request-byte length, raw JSON scan, raw surface-path manifest validation, schema, envelope-byte length, owner, IDs, hash, and merge in that order; it requires both byteLength(stableJson({data,sync})) <= MAX_ENVELOPE_BYTES and byteLength(stableJson(body)) <= MAX_ENVELOPE_BYTES + MAX_POST_PROTOCOL_OVERHEAD_BYTES. The raw surface-path check runs before any Zod normalization or schema transform and therefore reports a rogue or excluded nested key instead of allowing a non-strict schema to strip it. Stored JSONB and every merge candidate use the same raw path check before schema normalization; a local full-store input uses its separate allowed set. A raw request path failure is 400 bad_request; a stored or merge candidate path failure is 409 stored_conflict with zero DML. Path percent decoding rejects encoded slash and invalid escapes. An unauthenticated request returns 401 before rate limiting. An authenticated request over the method-specific limit returns 429 before body schema errors. The route never casts an unvalidated app string to ValidAppId. Existing clients that send the current {data,merge} body or consume the old response receive 426 client_upgrade_required with no DML and no state adoption. V1 required fields remain frozen for this rollout; removing one requires a new wireVersion, explicit negotiation, and a separately inventoried compatibility fixture. There is no silent v0 conversion or unannounced v1 field removal. wire.raw-duplicate-body covers duplicate top-level and nested keys, empty body, malformed JSON, and both fetch and beacon requests; wire.envelope-overhead-boundary covers every raw and canonical byte boundary.

Visibility handling uses the same stable RequestProgressSnapshot and BeaconDeliveryKey. On visibilitychange:hidden and pagehide, the coordinator selects the newest meaningful pending snapshot for the current owner tuple and sends at most one beacon for each unique ownerFingerprint,ownerGeneration,ownerEpoch,canonicalBodyHash,mergeMode,baseServerVersion key. A successful sendBeacon records result:"sent" before the document can retire; a false result records result:"rejected", leaves the durable fetch delivery pending, and schedules no beacon loop. A later foreground event may use fetch retry under the normal delivery budget. A different canonical body, including S1 after hidden S0, gets a different key and is eligible for a beacon; a same-body retry, replay, or second pagehide is deduplicated even when its logical save ID or request ID differs. The record is cleared only by owner rotation or durable acknowledgement. A late foreground S0 response cannot overwrite or clear a newer S1 beacon record because response adoption compares the full attempt identity and the current newest-snapshot key. beacon.hidden-s0-mutated-s1-pagehide, beacon.same-body-retry, beacon.acknowledged-body, and beacon.sendBeacon-false assert this matrix.

5.3 Retry-After and scheduling

type PendingDeliveryPayloadV1 = {
  version: 1;
  appId: ValidAppId;
  owner: OwnerTuple;
  logicalSaveId: string;
  mutationId: string;
  requestId: string;
  deliveryAttempt: number;
  canonicalBodyHash: string;
  mergeMode: MergeMode;
  baseServerVersion: string | null;
  data: CanonicalServerData<CanonicalProgressRecord>;
  sync: SyncMetadataV1;
  payloadDigest: string;
  payloadByteLength: number;
};
type PendingDeliveryEntryV1 = {
  version: 1;
  appId: ValidAppId;
  owner: OwnerTuple;
  logicalSaveId: string;
  mutationId: string;
  currentRequestId: string;
  currentDeliveryAttempt: number;
  canonicalBodyHash: string;
  mergeMode: MergeMode;
  baseServerVersion: string | null;
  payloadKey: string;
  payloadDigest: string;
  payloadByteLength: number;
  firstAttemptAtMs: number;
  cumulativeDeliveryAttempts: number;
  lastAttemptAtMs: number;
  terminal: "pending" | "acknowledged" | "delivery_exhausted" | "rejected" | "ledger_unavailable";
  terminalCode: "delivery_exhausted" | "delivery_ledger_unavailable" | "delivery_payload_invalid" | "http_400" | "http_401" | "http_404" | "http_409" | null;
};
type PendingDeliveryLedgerV1 = {
  version: 1;
  owner: OwnerTuple;
  entries: readonly PendingDeliveryEntryV1[];
};
type PendingDeliveryCapacityExceeded = {
  kind: "pending_delivery_capacity";
  maxEntries: 64;
  currentEntries: 64;
  owner: OwnerTuple;
};
const DELIVERY_PAYLOAD_NAMESPACE = "hanks-hits-progress-delivery-payload-v1:";
declare function deliveryPayloadKey(owner: OwnerTuple, logicalSaveId: string): string;

The durable queue uses a fixed journal-before-payload sequence because local storage has no cross-key transaction. Before a new logical save, read and validate both DeliveryStagingLedgerV1 at DELIVERY_STAGING_KEY and PendingDeliveryLedgerV1 at hanks-hits-progress-delivery-ledger-v1; require one owner tuple, equal sorted identity lists, and at most 64 entries. If the staging journal or delivery ledger has 64 entries, throw the typed PendingDeliveryCapacityExceeded before the source setter is invoked; the action does not mutate persisted or live progress, sends no network request, and remains retryable after one entry is acknowledged or purged. Allocate the full mutation identity, serialize PendingDeliveryPayloadV1, derive payloadKey=deliveryPayloadKey(owner,logicalSaveId), and append a sorted PendingDeliveryStagingEntryV1 with localPersistState:"pending" to the staging journal. Read back the journal byte-for-byte. Write the payload under that exact key and read it back, verifying canonical bytes, digest, and UTF-8 byte length. Perform the one real local PersistStorage write and raw-envelope read-back, then mark that staging entry localPersistState:"verified" and read the journal back. Write PendingDeliveryLedgerV1 with the matching entry and read it back. Only after the ledger read-back succeeds is the staging entry removed and the staging journal read back; a crash after payload or local-envelope write but before ledger write therefore leaves old-owner evidence that recovery can enumerate without guessing. A staging-journal write failure before payload write leaves no dynamic key; a payload or local-envelope write/read-back failure leaves the staging journal for bounded recovery; a ledger write/read-back failure leaves the verified staging entry and payload and records delivery_ledger_unavailable without transport. A reload validates journal, ledger, local-envelope evidence, and payload as one tuple before retry. It rejects a missing journal entry, a foreign owner, a duplicate logical save, a payload-key mismatch, a digest/length mismatch, an impossible localPersistState, or an unknown dynamic key as terminal delivery_payload_invalid and retains the evidence for quarantine. Acknowledgement first read-backs the ledger, removes the exact payload under "delivery-ledger-ack", removes the matching staging entry, removes the static ledger, then removes the static staging journal, with null read-back after each deletion. Owner transition uses old owner A from the validated journal/ledger entry and marker B only as the freeze barrier; it never derives an A payload key from B.

The client injects nowMs() and uses fixed clockSkewMs=300000. A valid Retry-After decimal delta with digits only is interpreted as seconds times 1000. A valid IMF-fixdate uses max(0,retryDateMs-effectiveNowMs); when a valid HTTP Date header exists, effectiveNowMs is nowMs + clamp(serverDateMs-nowMs,-300000,300000), otherwise it is nowMs. A valid header wins over exponential backoff. The delay is capped at 300000 ms. A malformed, negative, fractional, duplicate, or overflow header is ignored and uses min(1000 * 2^(deliveryAttempt-1), 30000). Production adds deterministic spread sha256(ownerFingerprint + logicalSaveId) mod 251 ms; tests inject spread zero. MAX_DELIVERY_ATTEMPTS=8, MAX_DELIVERY_AGE_MS=900000, and MAX_PENDING_LOGICAL_SAVES=64 are enforced from the durable ledger before every queue operation. A 429 or 503 with Retry-After schedules one new delivery attempt only when the ledger entry is pending, cumulativeDeliveryAttempts < 8, and nowMs()-firstAttemptAtMs < 900000; exhaustion writes terminal delivery_exhausted, retains the local pending envelope, and waits without resetting the budget. A 400, 401, 404, 409 schema/conflict response, or owner mismatch is terminal for that attempt. sendBeacon false uses one beacon delivery only, records unacknowledged state in the same ledger, and never schedules a beacon loop. wire.envelope-overhead-boundary asserts that an envelope of exactly 196608 UTF-8 bytes is accepted only when the complete POST body is at most 262144 bytes, and one additional byte is rejected before hashing or network send.

Delivery retry is distinct from owner recovery. The first request persists the payload and then cumulativeDeliveryAttempts:1, currentDeliveryAttempt:1, and the complete AttemptIdentity; each retry persists the incremented cumulative count, new request ID, and new payload identity before scheduling. Only a current terminal:"pending" ledger entry with a verified payload may retry. 429 and 503 are retryable under the fixed attempt and age caps; 400, 401, 404, 409, owner mismatch, malformed response, and hash/correlation mismatch are terminal. A successful POST uses the validated acknowledged-ledger removal above. A false sendBeacon result records one unacknowledged delivery and does not start a beacon loop. GET reconcile remains a server-baseline operation and never acknowledges a POST.

type SyncRequestFailure = {
  phase: "initial-get" | "reconcile-get" | "post" | "beacon" | "delete";
  kind: "network" | "aborted" | "http" | "malformed-response" | "owner" | "protocol";
  status: number | null;
  retryClass: "automatic-transient" | "manual-only" | "auth-recovery" | "owner-recovery" | "terminal";
  retryAtMs: number | null;
  diagnosticCode: string;
};
type ForceSyncHandle = {
  forceSync(): void;
  identity: symbol;
};
Phase and failureExact state transitionRetry and force behavior
Initial GET or reconcile GET: network error, AbortError from teardown, 408, 429, 500, 502, or 503Remain non-READY in RECONCILING with SyncRequestFailure.retryClass:"automatic-transient"; no local adoption, POST, beacon, or READY.Use the parsed Retry-After or min(1000*2^(attempt-1),30000), cap at 8 attempts and 900000 ms, then enter GET_RETRY_EXHAUSTED with diagnostic evidence. A pending forceSync cancels the backoff and starts one immediate attempt without creating a second concurrent request.
Initial GET or reconcile GET: 401Enter AUTH_WAIT, discard no owned bytes, adopt no server data, and request one authentication-status resolution.No automatic network loop while auth is unresolved. The stable forceSync callback sets forcePending:true; the resolved auth transition consumes it exactly once.
Initial GET or reconcile GET: 409 stale_owner_epoch or owner_mismatchEnter FROZEN, invalidate old attempt/baseline state, and run the single owner-rotation or claim path. No response bytes are adopted.Only the verified new owner lease can start a new GET. A stale response cannot be retried under its old tuple.
Initial GET or reconcile GET: 400, 422, 426, malformed JSON, invalid correlation, or invalid response schemaEnter BLOCKED with sync_protocol_invalid; retain raw diagnostic digest and no live/server mutation.No automatic loop. Each explicit forceSync call records forcePending:true and permits exactly one fresh GET after the current attempt is settled; it never creates an unbounded timer loop.
Fetch POST: network error, abort not caused by owner rotation, 408, 429, or 503Keep the verified payload and ledger entry terminal:"pending"; do not clear the source mutation or POST baseline.Use the durable delivery budget and Retry-After rules. An owner abort freezes the entry for the new tuple and never retries with the old identity.
Fetch/beacon POST: 401, 409 server_changed, 409 owner error, 400, 404, 422, 426, malformed response, or hash/correlation mismatch401 enters auth recovery; server-changed performs one GET reconcile before allocating a new attempt; owner errors rotate/freeze; protocol and permanent errors mark the ledger rejected with an exact terminal code.There is no automatic resend of a rejected identity. A new source mutation or verified owner transition creates a new logical save; forceSync may only run the GET reconcile path and cannot resurrect the rejected payload.
DELETE: network/503Leave the deletion intent pending under its full identity and keep local owner state unchanged.Use one bounded retry under the DELETE budget. A 401 or owner error enters auth/owner recovery; 404 is a successful absent-row result and is not retried.

useAuthSync exposes the same stable ForceSyncHandle.forceSync function returned by the singleton. The function object is allocated once per document and stored in a coordinator ref; hooks never place a newly created closure in effect dependencies. It sets a monotonic forceSequence, never resets owner generation or delivery budgets, and is consumed after any active GET/POST settles. forcePending survives hydration, bootstrap, active GET, active POST, retry backoff, auth wait, and owner rotation until exactly one eligible action consumes it. client.get-retry-force-liveness covers transient initial GET, malformed response, active GET force, active POST force, backoff cancellation, exhaustion, and manual recovery.

6. Typed server wire, route factory, and Drizzle transaction

6.1 Shared production types

The production adapter is in packages/db and is consumed by the route and auth through one factory. It uses the installed Drizzle and Node pg declarations without an unsafe transaction cast.

import * as schema from "@hank-neil/db/schema";
import type { NodePgDatabase } from "drizzle-orm/node-postgres";
import type { Pool } from "pg";

type OwnerTuple = {
  ownerFingerprint: OwnerFingerprint;
  ownerGeneration: number;
  ownerEpoch: number;
  documentToken: string;
  leaseToken: string;
};
type LeaderboardProjection = {
  appId: ValidAppId;
  score: number;
  scoreType: "high_score" | "wins" | "fastest_time";
  additionalStats: Record<string, string | number> | null;
};
declare const leaderboardProjectorBrand: unique symbol;
declare const leaderboardValidatorBrand: unique symbol;
declare const projectionInvocationEvidenceBrand: unique symbol;
declare const projectionLedgerBrand: unique symbol;
declare const projectionDmlExecutorBrand: unique symbol;
declare const sourceLeaderboardExtractorBrand: unique symbol;
declare const sourceLeaderboardValidatorBrand: unique symbol;
declare const leaderboardSourceModuleBrand: unique symbol;
type ProjectionEvent = {
  sequence: number;
  kind: "projector" | "validator" | "dml";
  invocationId: string;
  table: string | null;
  operation: string | null;
};
type ProjectionExecutionLedger = {
  readonly [projectionLedgerBrand]: true;
  readonly events: () => NonEmptyReadonly<ProjectionEvent>;
  readonly recordProjectorCall: (invocationId: string) => number;
  readonly recordValidatorCall: (invocationId: string) => number;
  readonly beginDml: (table: string, operation: string) => number;
  readonly firstDmlSequence: () => number | null;
};
type ProjectionDmlExecutor = {
  readonly [projectionDmlExecutorBrand]: true;
  run<T>(table: string, operation: string, callback: (tx: ProgressTx) => Promise<T>): Promise<T>;
};
type RealLeaderboardExtractor = ((appId: ValidAppId, data: CanonicalProgressRecord) => unknown) & { readonly [sourceLeaderboardExtractorBrand]: true };
type RealLeaderboardValidator = ((value: unknown) => LeaderboardProjection | null) & { readonly [sourceLeaderboardValidatorBrand]: true };
type LeaderboardSourceModule = {
  readonly [leaderboardSourceModuleBrand]: true;
  readonly moduleId: "apps/web/src/lib/leaderboard-extractors.ts";
  readonly projectLeaderboard: RealLeaderboardExtractor;
  readonly validateLeaderboardProjection: RealLeaderboardValidator;
};
function createLeaderboardSourceModule(realExtractor: RealLeaderboardExtractor, realValidator: RealLeaderboardValidator): LeaderboardSourceModule {
  if (realExtractor[sourceLeaderboardExtractorBrand] !== true || realValidator[sourceLeaderboardValidatorBrand] !== true) throw new Error("real leaderboard source brand missing");
  const source = { moduleId: "apps/web/src/lib/leaderboard-extractors.ts", projectLeaderboard: realExtractor, validateLeaderboardProjection: realValidator } as LeaderboardSourceModule;
  Object.defineProperty(source, leaderboardSourceModuleBrand, { value: true });
  return source;
}
type ProjectionInvocationEvidence = {
  invocationId: string;
  sourceModuleId: string | null;
  projectorCalls: number;
  validatorCalls: number;
  realExtractorCalled: boolean;
  realValidatorCalled: boolean;
  projectorSequence: number | null;
  validatorSequence: number | null;
  firstDmlSequence: number | null;
  calledBeforeFirstDml: boolean;
  outputDigest: string | null;
  combined: boolean;
  mode: "executed" | "not-run";
  notRunReason: "preflight" | "early-validation" | "timeout" | null;
  readonly [projectionInvocationEvidenceBrand]: true;
};
type LeaderboardProjector = ((appId: ValidAppId, data: CanonicalProgressRecord) => unknown) & {
  readonly [leaderboardProjectorBrand]: true;
  readonly evidence: () => ProjectionInvocationEvidence;
  readonly ledger: ProjectionExecutionLedger;
};
type LeaderboardProjectionValidator = ((value: unknown) => LeaderboardProjection | null) & {
  readonly [leaderboardValidatorBrand]: true;
  readonly evidence: () => ProjectionInvocationEvidence;
  readonly ledger: ProjectionExecutionLedger;
};
type LeaderboardProjectionRuntime = {
  projector: LeaderboardProjector;
  validator: LeaderboardProjectionValidator;
  ledger: ProjectionExecutionLedger;
  dmlExecutorFor(tx: ProgressTx): ProjectionDmlExecutor;
};
function createProjectionExecutionLedger(): ProjectionExecutionLedger {
  let sequence = 0;
  let firstDml: number | null = null;
  const events: ProjectionEvent[] = [];
  return {
    [projectionLedgerBrand]: true,
    events: () => {
      if (events.length === 0) throw new Error("projection ledger has no runtime observation");
      return events as NonEmptyReadonly<ProjectionEvent>;
    },
    recordProjectorCall: invocationId => {
      if (firstDml !== null) throw new Error("projector was called after DML");
      const event = ++sequence;
      events.push({ sequence: event, kind: "projector", invocationId, table: null, operation: null });
      return event;
    },
    recordValidatorCall: invocationId => {
      if (firstDml !== null) throw new Error("validator was called after DML");
      const event = ++sequence;
      events.push({ sequence: event, kind: "validator", invocationId, table: null, operation: null });
      return event;
    },
    beginDml: (table, operation) => {
      const event = ++sequence;
      if (firstDml === null) firstDml = event;
      events.push({ sequence: event, kind: "dml", invocationId: "adapter", table, operation });
      return event;
    },
    firstDmlSequence: () => firstDml
  };
}
function createProjectionDmlExecutor(ledger: ProjectionExecutionLedger): (tx: ProgressTx) => ProjectionDmlExecutor {
  if (ledger[projectionLedgerBrand] !== true) throw new Error("projection ledger brand missing");
  return tx => ({
    [projectionDmlExecutorBrand]: true,
    async run(table, operation, callback) {
      ledger.beginDml(table, operation);
      return callback(tx);
    }
  });
}
function createLeaderboardProjector(
  realExtractor: RealLeaderboardExtractor,
  sourceModuleId: LeaderboardSourceModule["moduleId"],
  invocationId: string,
  ledger: ProjectionExecutionLedger
): LeaderboardProjector {
  if (realExtractor[sourceLeaderboardExtractorBrand] !== true || sourceModuleId !== "apps/web/src/lib/leaderboard-extractors.ts") throw new Error("projector received an untrusted leaderboard source");
  let projectorCalls = 0;
  let realExtractorCalled = false;
  let projectorSequence: number | null = null;
  let outputDigest: string | null = null;
  const projector = ((appId, data) => {
    projectorCalls += 1;
    realExtractorCalled = true;
    projectorSequence = ledger.recordProjectorCall(invocationId);
    const output = realExtractor(appId, data);
    outputDigest = sha256(stableJson(output));
    return output;
  }) as LeaderboardProjector;
  Object.defineProperties(projector, {
    [leaderboardProjectorBrand]: { value: true },
    evidence: { value: () => ({ invocationId, sourceModuleId, projectorCalls, validatorCalls: 0, realExtractorCalled, realValidatorCalled: false, projectorSequence, validatorSequence: null, firstDmlSequence: ledger.firstDmlSequence(), calledBeforeFirstDml: projectorSequence !== null && (ledger.firstDmlSequence() === null || projectorSequence < ledger.firstDmlSequence()!), outputDigest, combined: false, mode: "executed", notRunReason: null, [projectionInvocationEvidenceBrand]: true }) },
    ledger: { value: ledger }
  });
  return projector;
}
function createLeaderboardProjectionValidator(
  realValidator: RealLeaderboardValidator,
  sourceModuleId: LeaderboardSourceModule["moduleId"],
  invocationId: string,
  ledger: ProjectionExecutionLedger
): LeaderboardProjectionValidator {
  if (realValidator[sourceLeaderboardValidatorBrand] !== true || sourceModuleId !== "apps/web/src/lib/leaderboard-extractors.ts") throw new Error("validator received an untrusted leaderboard source");
  let validatorCalls = 0;
  let realValidatorCalled = false;
  let validatorSequence: number | null = null;
  let outputDigest: string | null = null;
  const validator = ((value) => {
    validatorCalls += 1;
    realValidatorCalled = true;
    validatorSequence = ledger.recordValidatorCall(invocationId);
    const output = realValidator(value);
    outputDigest = sha256(stableJson(output));
    return output;
  }) as LeaderboardProjectionValidator;
  Object.defineProperties(validator, {
    [leaderboardValidatorBrand]: { value: true },
    evidence: { value: () => ({ invocationId, sourceModuleId, projectorCalls: 0, validatorCalls, realExtractorCalled: false, realValidatorCalled, projectorSequence: null, validatorSequence, firstDmlSequence: ledger.firstDmlSequence(), calledBeforeFirstDml: validatorSequence !== null && (ledger.firstDmlSequence() === null || validatorSequence < ledger.firstDmlSequence()!), outputDigest, combined: false, mode: "executed", notRunReason: null, [projectionInvocationEvidenceBrand]: true }) },
    ledger: { value: ledger }
  });
  return validator;
}
function assertProjectionInvocationBeforeDml(
  projector: LeaderboardProjector,
  validator: LeaderboardProjectionValidator,
  requireRealExtractor: boolean
): ProjectionInvocationEvidence {
  const projectorEvidence = projector.evidence();
  const validatorEvidence = validator.evidence();
  if (projector[leaderboardProjectorBrand] !== true || validator[leaderboardValidatorBrand] !== true) throw new Error("projection wrapper brand missing");
  if (projector.ledger[projectionLedgerBrand] !== true || validator.ledger !== projector.ledger) throw new Error("projection wrappers do not share the execution ledger");
  if (projectorEvidence[projectionInvocationEvidenceBrand] !== true || validatorEvidence[projectionInvocationEvidenceBrand] !== true) throw new Error("projection invocation evidence brand missing");
  if (projectorEvidence.sourceModuleId !== validatorEvidence.sourceModuleId || (requireRealExtractor && projectorEvidence.sourceModuleId !== "apps/web/src/lib/leaderboard-extractors.ts")) throw new Error("projection source identity mismatch");
  if (!projectorEvidence.calledBeforeFirstDml || !validatorEvidence.calledBeforeFirstDml) throw new Error("projection wrapper was not invoked before DML");
  if (projectorEvidence.firstDmlSequence !== validatorEvidence.firstDmlSequence) throw new Error("projection wrappers observed different first-DML events");
  if (projectorEvidence.firstDmlSequence !== null && (projectorEvidence.projectorSequence === null || validatorEvidence.validatorSequence === null || projectorEvidence.projectorSequence >= projectorEvidence.firstDmlSequence || validatorEvidence.validatorSequence >= projectorEvidence.firstDmlSequence)) throw new Error("projection validation followed DML");
  if (requireRealExtractor && (!projectorEvidence.realExtractorCalled || !validatorEvidence.realValidatorCalled || projectorEvidence.projectorCalls !== 1 || validatorEvidence.validatorCalls !== 1)) throw new Error("real projection invocation missing");
  if (projectorEvidence.invocationId !== validatorEvidence.invocationId) throw new Error("projection evidence invocation mismatch");
  return {
    invocationId: projectorEvidence.invocationId,
    sourceModuleId: projectorEvidence.sourceModuleId,
    projectorCalls: projectorEvidence.projectorCalls,
    validatorCalls: validatorEvidence.validatorCalls,
    realExtractorCalled: projectorEvidence.realExtractorCalled,
    realValidatorCalled: validatorEvidence.realValidatorCalled,
    projectorSequence: projectorEvidence.projectorSequence,
    validatorSequence: validatorEvidence.validatorSequence,
    firstDmlSequence: projectorEvidence.firstDmlSequence,
    calledBeforeFirstDml: projectorEvidence.calledBeforeFirstDml && validatorEvidence.calledBeforeFirstDml,
    outputDigest: sha256(stableJson([projectorEvidence.outputDigest, validatorEvidence.outputDigest])),
    combined: true,
    mode: "executed",
    notRunReason: null,
    [projectionInvocationEvidenceBrand]: true
  };
}
function createNoProjectionInvocationEvidence(invocationId: string, reason: "preflight" | "early-validation" | "timeout"): ProjectionInvocationEvidence {
  return { invocationId, sourceModuleId: null, projectorCalls: 0, validatorCalls: 0, realExtractorCalled: false, realValidatorCalled: false, projectorSequence: null, validatorSequence: null, firstDmlSequence: null, calledBeforeFirstDml: true, outputDigest: null, combined: true, mode: "not-run", notRunReason: reason, [projectionInvocationEvidenceBrand]: true };
}
type HandleCandidate = (attempt: number, random: () => number) => string;
type OwnerLeaseSigner = {
  sign(payload: string): string;
  verify(payload: string, signature: string): boolean;
};
type ProgressAdapterDependencies = {
  createLeaderboardProjectionRuntime(source: LeaderboardSourceModule): LeaderboardProjectionRuntime;
  generateHandleCandidate: HandleCandidate;
  ownerLeaseSigner: OwnerLeaseSigner;
};
function createLeaderboardProjectionRuntime(source: LeaderboardSourceModule): LeaderboardProjectionRuntime {
  if (source[leaderboardSourceModuleBrand] !== true || source.moduleId !== "apps/web/src/lib/leaderboard-extractors.ts" || source.projectLeaderboard[sourceLeaderboardExtractorBrand] !== true || source.validateLeaderboardProjection[sourceLeaderboardValidatorBrand] !== true) throw new Error("leaderboard source module identity or brand mismatch");
  const ledger = createProjectionExecutionLedger();
  const invocationId = sha256(source.moduleId);
  const projector = createLeaderboardProjector(source.projectLeaderboard, source.moduleId, invocationId, ledger);
  const validator = createLeaderboardProjectionValidator(source.validateLeaderboardProjection, source.moduleId, invocationId, ledger);
  const dmlExecutorFor = createProjectionDmlExecutor(ledger);
  return { projector, validator, ledger, dmlExecutorFor };
}
type ProgressDb = NodePgDatabase<typeof schema> & { $client: Pool };
type ProgressTx = Parameters<Parameters<ProgressDb["transaction"]>[0]>[0];

The production DiagnosticSink is wrapped by emitDiagnosticSafely: it serializes a redacted event into a 256-event bounded buffer, catches sink and serialization exceptions, never awaits I/O, and returns before the transaction can commit or roll back. Sink failure increments an in-memory diagnostic-drop counter and cannot change DML outcome. The test sink exposes the ordered event buffer for deterministic assertions.

ProgressDb is inferred from the exported schema namespace and installed NodePgDatabase. The compile probe verifies ProgressTx is the callback transaction type and exposes the same typed relational query API as the production database. The sole DML implementation is packages/db/src/progress-adapter.ts; the route imports its createProgressDbAdapter factory through the package boundary and contains no route-local adapter. The adapter accepts a ProgressDbHandle, ProgressAdapterDependencies, and a production or branded integration fault policy, and never imports a private module singleton. createLeaderboardProjectionRuntime(source) accepts only a LeaderboardSourceModule whose projectLeaderboard and validateLeaderboardProjection functions carry the private source brands; those functions are the real exports from apps/web/src/lib/leaderboard-extractors.ts, and no substitute or unbranded callback can cross the package boundary. generateHandleCandidate is the typed pure candidate function injected from apps/web/src/lib/handle-generator.ts. The DB package performs the transaction, candidate loop, exact constraint mapping, and DML; it never imports apps/web. Production construction accepts only the literal ProductionFaultPolicy with null hook and probe; integration construction accepts only the private branded IntegrationFaultPolicy. assertFaultPolicyContext(ctx) rejects a production context carrying a probe, hook, or integration brand, rejects an integration context with a missing or mismatched branded probe/hook, and rejects any separately supplied statement seam because ProgressTxContext has no public statementProbe field. Every request context carries one LeaderboardProjectionRuntime and one bound ProjectionDmlExecutor. Every progress/profile/leaderboard insert, update, or delete runs through ctx.projectionDml.run(table,operation,tx => ...); the raw ProgressTx exists only inside that callback, and a source scan rejects any adapter DML call outside the executor. The shared execution ledger records each DML sequence before the Drizzle callback and rejects a projector or validator call after the first DML event. Every adapter operation receives ctx.owner; database predicates and inserts use ctx.owner.userId, while envelope validation requires data.sync.ownerFingerprint === ctx.owner.ownerFingerprint before a stored row is returned or merged.

6.2 Exact database mapping

TableSelectInsert/update and constraintsFixed rule
progressOwnerEpochsuserId,ownerFingerprint,ownerGeneration,ownerEpoch,documentToken,leaseToken,leaseId,issuedAt,expiresAt,signature,lastClaimNonce,lastClaimOwner,lastClaimLease,lastRecoveryNonce,lastRecoveryOwner,lastRecoveryLease by userId, selected FOR UPDATE while holding the user advisory lock.On a valid claim, insert the first row or update the authority and lastClaim* fields for the same user. A claim replay with the same nonce and the same requested tuple returns the exact committed lease without incrementing the epoch; the same nonce with another tuple or changed lease tuple returns claim_nonce_reuse. A recovery probe returns an explicit absent result when no row exists. A new-document or marker-loss recovery atomically issues a fresh lease bound to the full requested owner tuple and increments the epoch once even when the stored lease is unexpired; concurrent takeover requests serialize under the same user lock. A resume recovery on an unexpired row requires the exact current tuple or its durable replay, while an expired resume may issue one fresh lease. A recovery replay with the same nonce and tuple returns lastRecoveryLease without another increment; a reused recovery nonce returns recovery_nonce_reuse. A valid use performs no write. The unique primary key is userId; the lease ID is unique; the HMAC signature is generated only by the server.There is exactly one authoritative owner epoch per authenticated user and one valid document/lease binding at a time. Stale, expired, skipped, forged, owner-mismatch, or nonce-reuse proofs return typed errors before app-lock acquisition and before progress/profile/leaderboard DML. This table is the only server lease authority and is added by the named migration without touching the metadata channel.
appProgressid,userId,appId,data,updatedAt,lastSyncedAt by userId + appId. data is ServerStoredEnvelopeV1.Insert generated progressId, user ID, app ID, envelope, injected now for both timestamps. On conflict target [userId,appId], update only data,updatedAt,lastSyncedAt; preserve existing id, userId, and appId.serverVersion is lowercase SHA-256 of stable JSON {id,userId,appId,data,updatedAt,lastSyncedAt}; no version column or app-progress-specific metadata migration is added.
gamingProfilesid,userId,handle,showOnLeaderboards,createdAt,updatedAt by user ID.Select existing profile in the same transaction. Insert UUID, user ID, generated handle, true, injected now values. The user-wide advisory lock serializes profile creation across all apps. Each candidate insert runs inside a Drizzle nested transaction/savepoint; on SQLSTATE 23505 for the exact handle constraint, the savepoint rolls back and the next candidate is tried. Retry exactly eight candidates inside the same outer transaction.Never create a second profile for one user. A ninth collision throws ProfileHandleExhausted, rolls back the outer transaction, maps to 503 transaction_failed with Retry-After: 1, and performs no partial progress DML. Never retry a foreign constraint or an aborted outer transaction.
leaderboardEntriesid,gamingProfileId,appId,score,scoreType,additionalStats,achievedAt,syncedAt by profile, app, and score type.Insert generated entry ID. On conflict target [gamingProfileId,appId,scoreType], preserve entry ID and profile/app/type. syncedAt always becomes injected now. additionalStats and achievedAt update only when the new score is better. The installed nullable JSONB column is represented as Record<string,string|number> | null in the row type; null remains null in snapshots and becomes {} only in the validated leaderboard projection.high_score and wins use greater score. fastest_time uses lower score. Score and stats are validated before DML.

The owner authority schema change is one reviewed migration: packages/db/drizzle/0001_progress_owner_epoch.sql creates progress_owner_epochs(user_id text primary key references users(id) on delete cascade, owner_fingerprint text not null, owner_generation bigint not null, owner_epoch bigint not null, document_token text not null, lease_token text not null, lease_id text not null unique, issued_at timestamptz not null, expires_at timestamptz not null, signature text not null, last_claim_nonce text not null, last_claim_owner jsonb, last_claim_lease jsonb, last_recovery_nonce text not null, last_recovery_owner jsonb, last_recovery_lease jsonb), with checks for positive safe generation/epoch, expires_at > issued_at, lowercase digest/ID lengths, bounded nonces, and full tuple/lease JSON validation at the adapter boundary. Existing rows in a deployment upgrade receive deterministic backfill values for document/lease tokens, legacy-authority:${sha256(user_id+":"+lease_id).slice(0,32)} for the claim nonce, and legacy-recovery:${sha256(user_id+":"+lease_id).slice(0,32)} for the recovery nonce, with null replay owners and leases until a fresh authenticated recovery. A client nonce cannot claim the claim or recovery backfill except through the exact replay tuple. packages/db/src/schema/owner-epochs.ts is the Drizzle declaration and schema/index.ts exports it; the migration metadata and journal are updated as generated outputs. Production deployment runs the migration before route import, and the integration harness uses the same schema source plus the preflight above. deleteUserWithProgressLock(dbHandle,userId) is the only production user-delete wrapper: it opens one transaction, takes the exact user advisory lock, deletes the user through the typed Drizzle query, and asserts that the cascading authority row count is zero before commit. Auth.js deletion is wired through this wrapper, and a direct unwrapped user delete is rejected by the source-oracle test. No metadata column is added.

The adapter accepts only the branded LeaderboardProjector and LeaderboardProjectionValidator constructors produced in one LeaderboardProjectionRuntime. The function-valued factories share one private ProjectionExecutionLedger and attach private brands, invocation evidence, and a private ProjectionDmlExecutor. The production projector wrapper calls the real extractor and records its ordered event before returning the raw result; the production validator wrapper records its ordered event and validates the raw result. The adapter must call runtime.dmlExecutorFor(tx).run(table,operation,callback) for every progress/profile/leaderboard insert, update, or delete. That executor records the first real DML event before invoking the Drizzle callback, so a later self-report cannot move projection work earlier. assertProjectionInvocationBeforeDml compares the private event sequence and requires both real calls to precede the first DML event; when validation rejects before any write it requires projector/validator calls plus a null first-DML sequence. A raw tx.insert, tx.update, or tx.delete outside the executor is rejected by the adapter source audit. The evidence object is privately branded and includes projector, validator, first-DML, and output sequences; it is produced only by the wrappers. A complete canonical record whose real extractor output violates the installed score, score-type, or additional-stats schema throws LeaderboardProjectionConflictError; the handler maps it to exact 422 invalid_projection with supportedWireVersion:null, and the transaction has performed no DML. progress.error.invalid-projection-no-dml uses a full-schema-valid fixture plus one branded extractor wrapper that calls the real extractor and corrupts only its returned projection; it proves this is a real production-adapter validation path, not a shadow adapter. A canonical merge whose policy cap is exceeded, including the Cookie Clicker case with an existing valid 500-item array and a disjoint valid 500-item incoming array, throws ProgressMergeConflictError before the projector and maps to exact 409 stored_conflict; progress.atomic.cookie-overflow asserts the merged 1000-item candidate, zero projector calls, and zero DML, and never expects a projection error. A valid projection of null is the named no-score branch: upsert appProgress with injected updatedAt=now and lastSyncedAt=now, preserve the existing gamingProfiles row and all leaderboardEntries rows without insert/update/delete, and return the committed progress envelope. A positive projection first preserves or creates one profile, then upserts the projected leaderboard row under its exact conflict target. progress.atomic.valid-no-score, progress.error.invalid-projection-no-dml, and progress.atomic.cookie-overflow assert these branches. Every query and DML statement receives the same ProgressTx instance. The route calls createProgressDbAdapter(dbHandle, {createLeaderboardProjectionRuntime, generateHandleCandidate, ownerLeaseSigner}, productionFaultPolicy); it contains no unsafe transaction cast, no shadow adapter, and no second DML implementation. The web helper functions are pure injected dependencies at the package boundary, not DB-package imports.

Projection terminal evidence is a combined result, never one wrapper's self-report: the isolated harness calls assertProjectionInvocationBeforeDml(projector,validator,true) after the real route and before terminal evidence for progress.error.invalid-projection-no-dml, progress.atomic.positive-projection-conflict, and progress.atomic.valid-no-score, and stores its returned branded object. That function requires one shared ledger, exactly one real extractor call, exactly one real validator call, matching invocation IDs, sequence numbers before first DML, and a combined digest. A no-projection branch stores the returned createNoProjectionInvocationEvidence object only after the actual route ledger proves the branch stopped before projection. assertRequiredIntegrationInvocation rejects a non-combined object, requires exact real projector and validator evidence for the three named projection IDs, and rejects fabricated zero-call evidence for any branch that reports projection work.

The source identity is runtime-bound. createLeaderboardSourceModule rejects missing extractor or validator brands and installs a private module brand plus the literal source module ID apps/web/src/lib/leaderboard-extractors.ts. createLeaderboardProjectionRuntime, createLeaderboardProjector, and createLeaderboardProjectionValidator all recheck that module identity and the private function brands before constructing wrappers. The combined projection evidence copies the source module ID, and every projection-required terminal gate requires that exact ID. A test substitute can have the same call signature but cannot cross the branded source constructor or become real projection evidence.

Projection scenarios also carry exact route-derived cardinality and DML evidence. The terminal harness captures all four table snapshots, derives IntegrationDmlSummary from the correlated DML event stream, and calls assertExactTableDmlEvidence after deriving the digest again. The ledger's function-valued assertExactTableDml method calls the same assertExactTableDmlObservation helper against the actual route receipt before appending its assertion receipt, so the terminal copy cannot repair a failed scenario. The invalid-projection and Cookie Clicker overflow cases require all four counts unchanged and zero DML. The positive projection collision case seeds no progress or profile row, requires the eight profile-handle insert attempts plus one progress insert in the route trace, and requires outer rollback to return all four after-counts to their before-counts. The valid no-score case seeds one profile and one leaderboard row with no progress row, requires exactly one app-progress insert, and requires exact byte identity for the unchanged profile and leaderboard rows. A missing table, extra table, mismatched digest, or caller-supplied count fails the assertion.

6.3 Transaction algorithm and SQLSTATE mapping

  1. Validate method input, owner identity, canonical data, metadata, and merge baseline before opening a transaction.
  2. Open one Drizzle transaction. Execute select pg_backend_pid(), then select set_config('application_name', $1, true) with ctx.activityTag, where activityTagFor(applicationCorrelation,requestId)=`hhps:v1:${sha256(applicationCorrelation+":"+requestId).slice(0,48)}` is at most 63 ASCII bytes. Execute SET LOCAL lock_timeout = '750ms' and SET LOCAL statement_timeout = '2000ms' before any advisory lock.
  3. Acquire the user advisory lock pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended(concat('progress:v1:user:', $1::text), 0)) first for GET, fetch POST, beacon POST, and DELETE. Do not acquire the app lock until the owner lease authority check below succeeds. After that check, acquire pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended(concat('progress:v1:app:', $1::text, ':', $2::text), 0)) for every app-scoped read or write. The user lock serializes owner-lease claims, recovery, user deletion, and profile creation across apps; the app lock serializes progress and leaderboard rows.
  4. While holding the user lock, call assertAuthoritativeOwnerLease(tx,ctx.owner,ctx.requestIdentity.ownerLeaseProof,ctx.requestIdentity.owner) before the app lock and before any progress/profile/leaderboard DML. The single progress_owner_epochs row for the authenticated user is selected FOR UPDATE. A use proof requires a valid HMAC-SHA-256 signature over stable {version,userIdDigest,ownerFingerprint,ownerGeneration,ownerEpoch,documentToken,leaseToken,leaseId,issuedAtMs,expiresAtMs}, an unexpired lease, exact equality with the locked row, and exact equality between proof owner and request owner. A recover probe with a full requested owner tuple, explicit recovery reason, and durable recovery nonce first distinguishes an absent row from an existing row. An absent result performs no authority write and returns OwnerLeaseAuthorityResult.kind:"authority-absent"; the client then sends exactly one subsequent claim with generation/epoch 1/1. The claim generates document and lease tokens plus one lease ID, signs the exact payload with PROGRESS_SYNC_OWNER_LEASE_SECRET, persists lastClaimNonce,lastClaimOwner,lastClaimLease, and inserts the row in the same transaction. A claim replay with the same nonce and identical requested owner returns the exact persisted lease with replayed:true and no new epoch; a reused nonce with a different tuple returns claim_nonce_reuse. A marker-loss or new-document recovery against an existing row atomically generates a fresh document-bound lease, increments the epoch once even when the prior lease is unexpired, persists lastRecoveryNonce,lastRecoveryOwner,lastRecoveryLease, and returns authority-present,recovered:true; the old tuple becomes stale before any app lock. A resume recovery against an unexpired row is accepted only for the exact current owner tuple or its durable nonce replay; an expired resume recovery may issue one fresh lease bound to the requested tuple. Two concurrent takeover recoveries serialize under the user lock, so the loser cannot receive the first document's lease. A recovery replay with the same nonce and tuple returns its persisted lease with recoveryReplayed:true; a changed tuple or reused nonce returns recovery_nonce_reuse or owner_mismatch. A claim never accepts a client-supplied lease ID or signature. Any invalid, expired, foreign, skipped, nonce-reuse, or stale proof returns a typed owner error before app-lock acquisition and before app-progress, profile, leaderboard, or timestamp DML. A successful claim, replay, or recovery returns the server OwnerEpochLeaseV1; an absent recovery returns no lease; a successful use returns the locked row's lease. This row is the server authority for every GET, fetch POST, beacon POST, and DELETE, so an old self-consistent request cannot pass by copying its own sync.sourceEpoch.
  5. After the authority check, GET acquires the app lock and reads the existing raw JSONB row on that same ProgressTx before returning. Decode it as unknown and validate the envelope before returning or merging. A bare legacy JSONB object is wrapped with ctx.owner.ownerFingerprint and sourceEpoch:0; a present envelope with a null, guest, or mismatching fingerprint returns typed 409 owner_mismatch before DML. GET performs no progress, profile, leaderboard, or timestamp DML. For POST, when merge:false, require baseServerVersion to equal the current derived version, with null matching an absent row; a mismatch returns 409 server_changed before DML. When merge:true, apply the generated policy to the validated envelope and the current row even when the base version is stale; return the exact conflict PathKeys in conflicts. An absent row accepts merge as an initial save. A malformed stored envelope returns typed 409 before DML. Validate the canonical leaderboard projection before any POST DML. If it is invalid, return 422 before any DML. If it is null, upsert only appProgress, set both progress timestamps from ctx.now(), preserve every profile and leaderboard row, and return. If it is positive, upsert appProgress, preserve or create the one profile, and upsert each projected entry using the exact score rule.
  6. DELETE has a separate locked-read path: after the user lock, authoritative lease check, and app lock, select the app-progress row and its owner envelope on that same ProgressTx; only then decide absent versus present. An absent row returns 404 not_found with no DELETE DML and leaves profile/leaderboard rows unchanged. A present row validates ownership, deletes only the progress row and its app-scoped leaderboard rows in declared child-first order, preserves the gaming profile, and returns the deleted progress ID and signed owner lease. A concurrent POST can linearize before or after this read, never between the read and delete. delete.read-after-lock, delete.missing-row-locked, and delete.timeout-no-dml assert the sequence.
  7. Call assertFaultPolicyContext(ctx). When ctx.faultPolicy.kind === "progress-integration-test", invoke ctx.faultPolicy.statementProbe.run(tx) after both locks and before DML, passing the exact ProgressTx callback argument. The probe first executes SET LOCAL lock_timeout = '0ms' on that same transaction, records select pg_backend_pid() and select current_setting('statement_timeout'), requires the PID to equal the adapter diagnostic PID and the normalized statement timeout to equal 2000 ms, then executes SELECT pg_sleep(3) on that same tx. The separate lock case retains lock_timeout=750ms and expects 55P03; this probe case disables lock waiting before the sleep so 57014 is independently reachable. The integration case requires SQLSTATE 57014, an outer rollback, and unchanged progress/profile/leaderboard/version/timestamp snapshots; a probe that receives a pool or another transaction fails the branded test. Invoke ctx.faultPolicy.afterDmlBeforeCommit after all progress/profile/leaderboard DML and before the transaction callback returns. The production policy has no callable seam and the production factory rejects an integration brand, probe, hook, or unknown symbol.
  8. Return the committed canonical envelope, derived server version, IDs, and timestamps. Any error throws through Drizzle so all DML rolls back.

SQLSTATE 55P03 maps to HTTP 503, error lock_timeout, Retry-After: 1. SQLSTATE 57014 maps to HTTP 503, error statement_timeout, Retry-After: 2. The response includes request ID and application correlation only; backend PID and ctx.activityTag remain in the redacted server diagnostic sink and are never returned to a client. Unique-handle retry uses a nested savepoint only for the exact profile handle constraint and remains inside the outer transaction. All other 23505 errors map to 409 and roll back. server.stored-owner-mismatch-no-dml proves that a row whose stored envelope fingerprint differs from ctx.owner.ownerFingerprint is rejected by GET and POST before any write, profile creation, leaderboard write, or timestamp update.

ConditionTyped resultDML and retry rule
SQLSTATE 55P03503 lock_timeout, Retry-After: 1Outer rollback; blocker is never retried inside the request.
SQLSTATE 57014503 statement_timeout, Retry-After: 2Outer rollback; the statement probe and real lock observer use this same mapping.
23505 on exact gaming_profiles_handle_uniqueInternal savepoint retry; after candidate 8, 503 transaction_failed, Retry-After: 1Only the nested savepoint rolls back per candidate. Candidate nine is not attempted; the outer transaction rolls back.
23505 on gaming_profiles_user_id_unique409 profile_conflictNo retry. The outer transaction rolls back.
23505 on user_app_unique or leaderboard_unique outside its declared upsert target409 constraint_conflictNo retry. The outer transaction rolls back.
23505 on app_progress_pkey or leaderboard_entries_pkey503 transaction_failed, Retry-After: 1No retry. The outer transaction rolls back.
LeaderboardProjectionConflictError422 invalid_projectionThe full-schema-valid canonical fixture reaches the injected real extractor, projection validation occurs before every DML statement, and the 422 response has zero progress/profile/leaderboard writes with all row snapshots preserved.
ProgressMergeConflictError409 stored_conflictCanonical merge cap, unknown-path, schema, or metadata failure occurs before leaderboard extraction and every DML statement; Cookie Clicker overflow returns this typed error with all row snapshots unchanged.
Stored envelope owner fingerprint differs from the locked request owner409 owner_mismatchNo DML occurs; this is distinct from a malformed or policy-invalid stored envelope.
ProgressStoredEnvelopeInvalid409 stored_conflictOnly malformed, schema-invalid, policy-invalid, unknown-path, or metadata-invalid stored data uses this code. No DML occurs.
Merge divergence with merge:true200 ProgressCommittedResult with merged:true and exact conflictsThis is successful canonical merge behavior, never an error response.
Stale base with merge:false409 server_changedNo DML occurs.
Branded post-DML fault or unexpected transaction error503 transaction_failed, Retry-After: 1All DML rolls back; the injected snapshot proves progress, profile, leaderboard, version, and timestamps are unchanged.

The constraint names are checked by the integration preflight and by the error-map test. A different 23505 is not treated as a handle collision. The successful merge case is named progress.merge.divergent-success; the sole required no-DML projection-error case is progress.error.invalid-projection-no-dml.

Existing progress rowRequest merge flagResponse merged and DML
AbsentOmitted400 before adapter; no response result.
ExistingOmitted400 before adapter; no response result.
Absentfalse200 with merged:false; one progress insert, no policy merge.
Existingfalse200 with merged:false; replace after exact base-version match, preserving row ID.
Absenttrue200 with merged:false; initial merge accepts the incoming canonical value as the only candidate and inserts one progress row.
Existingtrue200 with merged:true; policy merge runs, exact conflict paths are returned, and the merged canonical response equals the committed row.

merged reports the operation actually performed, not merely the presence of a boolean request field: it is true only for a merge against an existing row. route.merged-response-six-cases runs all six cells for local and server envelopes, asserts the response flag, stored envelope, server version, preserved IDs, and zero DML on omitted merge.

6.4 Route factory and method schemas

type ProgressRequestContextFactory = (input: {
  requestIdentity: ValidatedProgressRequestIdentity;
  owner: ProgressOwner;
  now: () => Date;
  faultPolicy: FaultPolicy;
  diagnostics: DiagnosticSink;
}) => ProgressTxContext;
type ProgressHandlerDeps = {
  authenticate(request: Request): Promise<{ userId: string } | null>;
  rateLimit: {
    get(userId: string, method: "GET" | "POST" | "DELETE"): RateLimitResult;
  };
  isValidAppId(value: string): value is ValidAppId;
  adapter: ProgressDbAdapter;
  ownerFingerprintForUser(userId: string): OwnerFingerprint;
  now: () => Date;
  clockSkewMs: 300000;
  requestId: () => string;
  applicationCorrelation: () => string;
  createRequestContext: ProgressRequestContextFactory;
  createFaultPolicy: () => FaultPolicy;
  createDiagnostics: () => DiagnosticSink;
};
type ProgressRouteHandlers = {
  GET(request: Request, context: { params: Promise<{ appId: string }> }): Promise<Response>;
  POST(request: Request, context: { params: Promise<{ appId: string }> }): Promise<Response>;
  DELETE(request: Request, context: { params: Promise<{ appId: string }> }): Promise<Response>;
};

createProgressRoute(deps) is the only handler factory. GET accepts no body and returns absent data as data:null,sync:null,serverVersion:null,updatedAt:null,lastSyncedAt:null with 200. POST requires one JSON body with all fields in the client contract. merge must be boolean and is never defaulted when omitted. baseServerVersion is a null or 64-character hash. The handler first passes the raw request URL, raw query pairs, method, raw header-occurrence ledger, and nonce to buildPreflightCorrelation, before authentication, quota, app lookup, or body reads. It then parses and validates the method-specific request identity, including request ID, application correlation, full owner tuple, owner-generation/epoch, owner-lease proof, delivery attempt, canonical body hash, merge mode, base server version, and transport. A claim proof contains no lease and is valid only when the same request follows an observed absent authority row or exactly replays the persisted claim nonce and full tuple; a non-replay claim against an existing row is rejected before app access. A recovery proof carries its durable recovery nonce; a use proof contains the signed server lease and is never synthesized from client fields. It derives one ProgressOwner from userId, rejects a guest or any request/body/metadata owner fingerprint mismatch before adapter invocation, rejects sync.sourceEpoch !== requestIdentity.ownerEpoch as typed stale_owner_epoch before DML, calls deps.createFaultPolicy() and deps.createDiagnostics(), then calls deps.createRequestContext({requestIdentity,owner,now:deps.now,faultPolicy,diagnostics}). That factory must return a request-scoped ProgressTxContext whose request identity, request ID, application correlation, owner, clock, fault policy, diagnostic sink, projection runtime, and projection DML executor are exactly those values; no request field can select a database user, fault hook, projection, or DML seam. Production supplies a factory that can return only ProductionFaultPolicy; integration supplies a factory that returns the branded test policy from @hank-neil/db/test-only-faults. The handler calls deps.isValidAppId(appId) as a type predicate before constructing any adapter input, so no unchecked Set.has result or cast supplies ValidAppId. A successful claim or authority-present recovery response includes the signed ownerLease; an authority-absent recovery response includes no lease, and the coordinator persists any returned lease in its verified owner marker before READY. Subsequent GET, POST, beacon, and DELETE builders send the same proof. DELETE accepts no body, opens the same user-lock then app-lock transaction, reads after the authoritative lease check, validates stored ownership before returning or deleting, returns typed 404 with no DELETE DML when the row is absent, and never deletes profile or leaderboard rows for a missing app row. The injected rate limiter preserves the installed result shape {success,remaining,resetIn}: GET and POST use 60 requests per minute per user, DELETE uses 10 requests per minute, and a failed check maps resetIn seconds to Retry-After. route.wire-compatibility.test.ts asserts request identity/header echo, claim replay/rejection, lease claim/recovery/use, signed lease response, and canonical correlation on every method; route.error-order.test.ts asserts stale-owner-epoch for stale GET, fetch POST, beacon POST, and DELETE before app-lock, adapter DML, or timestamp changes.

MethodExact pre-adapter orderQuota effect
GETResolve route path and params, build raw preflight correlation from URL/query/headers, authenticate, apply the GET limiter, validate the app ID, parse exact query keys, reject any body, then build the request context.An authenticated invalid app consumes one GET quota unit and returns 400; an unauthenticated request returns 401 with the same preflight correlation without consuming quota; a limited authenticated request returns 429 before app or query errors.
Normal POSTResolve route path and params, build raw preflight correlation, authenticate, apply the POST limiter, validate the app ID, require JSON content type, read and byte-check the body once, scan duplicate keys, validate raw surface paths, validate the schema and owner/hash/lineage fields, then build the request context.An authenticated invalid app, empty body, duplicate body, rogue path, or malformed body consumes one POST quota unit; a limited authenticated request returns 429 before app, content, or body errors and preserves its preflight correlation.
Beacon POSTResolve route path and params, build raw preflight correlation from the query before reading the body, authenticate, apply the POST limiter, validate the app ID, parse the exact beacon query and transport discriminator, require JSON content type, read and byte-check the body once, scan duplicate keys, validate raw surface paths, validate body/query equality, then build the request context.Beacon attempts use the POST quota and an authenticated invalid app or malformed beacon consumes one unit; 401, 429, and 400 retain transport:"beacon" and the same request/application correlation; sendBeacon false is a client delivery result after the request leaves the page, not a second server call.
DELETEResolve route path and params, build raw preflight correlation, authenticate, apply the DELETE limiter, validate the app ID, parse exact DELETE query keys, reject any body, then build the request context.An authenticated invalid app consumes one DELETE quota unit; an unauthenticated request does not; a limited authenticated request returns 429 before app or body errors with the preflight correlation.

route.auth-rate-order asserts this table with a call-order spy and quota counters for every method, invalid app, unauthenticated, over-limit, malformed-body, duplicate-body, and non-empty GET/DELETE case. This order is the compatibility migration from the current route's authenticate-then-rate-limit behavior; no handler branch performs app validation before its method limiter or invokes the adapter before all listed checks.

route.error-order.test.ts also seeds one authoritative lease for epoch A, claims epoch B in a separate authenticated request, and then sends stale A GET, fetch POST, beacon POST, and DELETE requests with valid old signatures and self-consistent old sync.sourceEpoch. Each returns 409 stale_owner_epoch with the preflight/validated correlation, performs no app-lock acquisition, progress/profile/leaderboard DML, row/timestamp/version change, or lease rollback, and leaves the B authority row intact. The same test proves a forged signature, expired lease, skipped epoch, and claim with epoch B+2 fail before DML.

Every successful response sets Content-Type: application/json; charset=utf-8, Cache-Control: no-store, X-Progress-Request-Id, X-Progress-Application-Correlation, and X-Progress-Server-Version when one exists. A 429 additionally sets X-RateLimit-Limit, X-RateLimit-Remaining: 0, X-RateLimit-Reset equal to resetIn, and integer Retry-After. 400, 401, 404, 409, 422, 426, 429, and 503 responses use one ProgressErrorV1 shape with a stable error code. Every error has supportedWireVersion:null except 426 client_upgrade_required, which has supportedWireVersion:1 and header X-Progress-Supported-Wire-Version: 1. 422 invalid_projection is a no-DML response and carries no stored progress. 426, 429, and 503 include the supported wire version or integer Retry-After seconds as defined by the error code and no response body that contains stored progress. The production route in route.ts only obtains the environment-validated production auth and database handles, calls the factory, and exports its handlers. Integration imports the factory dynamically after env setup and injects auth, clock, rate limit, and adapter.

Preflight failures can occur before request identity validation, so buildPreflightCorrelation is the sole fallback builder and receives the untouched request URL, raw query pairs, method, raw header-occurrence ledger, and one injected nonce before authentication, quota, app lookup, or body reads. The handler captures raw header occurrences as {name,value,ordinal} before constructing Headers; duplicate names remain separate and a duplicate request/application/transport header is a preflight mismatch rather than a collapsed value. It extracts raw query pairs without collapsing duplicates. A single valid transport=beacon query value is authoritative; when the query transport is absent, a single valid X-Progress-Transport: beacon header selects beacon; a query/header disagreement returns 400 transport_mismatch using the same preflight correlation, and only when both raw channels are absent does a POST default to fetch. Method still maps to get or delete. It preserves a raw request ID only when the raw query/header union has exactly one value matching the generated ID grammar; otherwise it emits preflight-${sha256(method+":"+pathname+":"+requestNonce).slice(0,32)}. It preserves an application correlation only when its raw union has exactly one value matching the same grammar; otherwise it emits preflight-${sha256(requestId+":"+method+":"+pathname).slice(0,32)}. It sets owner, owner-lease ID, logical-save ID, mutation ID, hash, and merge mode to null and delivery attempt to 1. The handler constructs this object before auth and quota failures, then uses it for the body correlation and X-Progress-Request-Id/X-Progress-Application-Correlation headers on 400, 401, 429, 426, and 503, including malformed query, malformed header, body-size, and unsupported-client failures. No unvalidated header is echoed. route.preflight-correlation tests missing, malformed, duplicate, disagreement, unauthenticated, and rate-limited requests for GET, fetch POST, beacon POST, and DELETE, and asserts exact body/header equality plus beacon correlation preservation.

7. Isolated PostgreSQL, faults, and Vitest isolation

7.1 Fail-closed PostgreSQL algorithm

The integration command is pnpm --dir apps/web test:integration. In apps/web/package.json, scripts.test:integration is exactly "bash scripts/run-progress-integration.sh"; it has no alternate raw Vitest command. The shell script performs this exact algorithm:

  1. Resolve repoRealpath and homeRealpath with realpath. Set tmpParent to the fixed directory /private/tmp, require realpath(tmpParent)===/private/tmp, require the directory to exist with mode 1777, and create dataDir with mktemp -d "/private/tmp/hhps-progress-pg.XXXXXX". Do not inherit TMPDIR, because the macOS per-user temporary root is not the validated harness parent. Require dataDir to be newly created, mode 700, inside tmpParent, and not equal to the repository, home, root, or any non-temporary parent. Set socketDir="$dataDir/socket", create it mode 700, and store both absolute paths before starting PostgreSQL.
  2. Require /opt/homebrew/bin/initdb, pg_ctl, createdb, dropdb, psql, and postgres to report PostgreSQL 17.9-compatible versions. Run initdb --no-locale --encoding=UTF8 --auth-local=trust --auth-host=scram-sha-256 "$dataDir". Local control connections use the private socket and trust only during provisioning; the application DATABASE_URL uses TCP password authentication with a SCRAM verifier.
  3. Generate nonce=hex(randomBytes(6)), set databaseName=progress_sync_${nonce} and roleName=progress_test_${nonce}, and validate both against their exact lower-case regular expressions ^progress_sync_[a-f0-9]{12}$ and ^progress_test_[a-f0-9]{12}$. Build portCandidates as [55432,...,55463], shuffle it with an injected cryptographic random source, and for each candidate open a Node net server bound to 127.0.0.1, require the bind and close to succeed, then assign port to that candidate. The loop stops at the first verified candidate and fails closed after 32 candidates; PostgreSQL start still treats EADDRINUSE as a failed candidate and consumes the next shuffled port.
  4. Start pg_ctl -D "$dataDir" -o "-p $port -k $socketDir -c listen_addresses=127.0.0.1" -w start. Generate rolePassword=base64url(randomBytes(24)) and keep it only in the shell process. Through the private socket run SET password_encryption = 'scram-sha-256', create the role with the exact login password, and assert rolpassword LIKE 'SCRAM-SHA-256$%'. Create the database with createdb ... -O "$roleName" "$databaseName", assert pg_get_userbyid(datdba) = roleName, assert has_schema_privilege(roleName,'public','CREATE'), and verify a TCP password login as the role can create and drop a disposable schema object. Construct one canonical postgresql://roleName:encodedPassword@127.0.0.1:port/databaseName?sslmode=disable URL; no second URL form is accepted.
  5. Export DATABASE_URL, PROGRESS_SYNC_TEST_DATABASE_URL, and the in-process password value before importing any web auth, route, database package, schema, or test module. Open a fresh TCP connection with that exact URL, assert current_user=roleName, current_database=databaseName, inet_server_addr()=127.0.0.1, and the SCRAM login succeeds. Reject an externally supplied URL unless every scheme, user, host, port, database, password digest, and sslmode component matches the just-created cluster.
  6. Run pnpm --filter @hank-neil/db exec drizzle-kit push --config drizzle.config.ts --url "$DATABASE_URL" --force.
