Run a preflight query that requires progress_owner_epochs, app_progress, gaming_profiles, and leaderboard_entries and confirms progress_owner_epochs_pkey, progress_owner_epochs_user_id_users_id_fk with ON DELETE CASCADE, progress_owner_epochs.last_claim_nonce, progress_owner_epochs.last_recovery_nonce, progress_owner_epochs.document_token, progress_owner_epochs.lease_token, user_app_unique, gaming_profiles_user_id_unique, gaming_profiles_handle_unique, and leaderboard_unique. Require PROGRESS_SYNC_OWNER_LEASE_SECRET to be present, at least 32 random bytes after UTF-8 encoding, and identical to the integration dependency factory input. A failed push, preflight, or secret validation exits before dynamic import.
  7. Dynamically import only @hank-neil/db/factory, @hank-neil/db/progress-adapter, @hank-neil/db/schema, and the web route/auth factories after env setup. Create the integration-sourced handle from the isolated URL, inject it into the production-shaped adapter and route/auth dependencies, and never import the root entry as a pool creator. The web integration harness obtains blocker, observer, and seed clients only through the exported ProgressDbHandle.pool and its pool.connect() method; it imports no pg module and adds no web dependency or lockfile entry. Assert the adapter's pool identity and auth adapter identity point to this handle, globalThis[Symbol.for("hanks-hits.progress-production-db")] is absent, the pool registry has one URL, and no module-created singleton exists.
  8. Run integration Vitest with fileParallelism:false, maxWorkers:1, minWorkers:1, and sequence.concurrent:false. Before every case, clear FK children in this exact order: leaderboard_entries, app_transactions, app_progress, progress_owner_epochs, gaming_profiles, accounts, sessions, authenticators, then users; verification_tokens is not user-linked in the installed schema and is never used as a fixture child. Reset identities. Insert one deterministic users row with the fixture user ID, null name,email,emailVerified,image,password, and injected createdAt,updatedAt before inserting any child row, because app_progress.user_id, progress_owner_epochs.user_id, and the three auth-child user foreign keys require the parent. Allocate one deterministic app ID fixture per required ID. After each case, delete the same child tables in reverse dependency order, assert zero rows in users, app_progress, app_transactions, progress_owner_epochs, gaming_profiles, leaderboard_entries, accounts, sessions, and authenticators, and fail closed on any FK setup/cleanup error or non-empty assertion. The production user-delete fixture takes the user advisory lock before deleting users, then asserts the cascade leaves zero progress_owner_epochs. db-harness.cleanup-leaderboard-row intentionally inserts one orphan-resistant leaderboard row, verifies it is removed by the child-first cleanup, and fails if the post-case zero-row assertion omits any listed table.
  9. Register the one route-owned ProgressDbHandle and client lease records for blocker, observer, and contender in pool-registry.ts; never register the raw Pool as a second close owner. The blocker, observer, and contender release their checked-out clients, while only the handle's idempotent close() ends the pool. run-progress-integration.sh owns the sole idempotent cleanupOnce and its absolute 5000 ms deadline on success, test failure, signal, and import failure. It calls closeAllRegisteredHandles() exactly once after the test runner stops, then runs dropdb -h "$socketDir" -p "$port" --if-exists "$databaseName", stops PostgreSQL with pg_ctl -D "$dataDir" -w stop -m fast, and removes only the validated dataDir. db-harness.ts owns row reset and empty-row assertions only; pool-registry.ts records handles and client release order but never drops the database, stops PostgreSQL, removes dataDir, or runs a second cleanup. The shell attempts every client release even after one release fails, calls the handle close guard exactly once, reports any release, close, drop, stop, or removal failure as a test failure, and never runs cleanup twice. db-harness.no-root-singleton covers success, test failure, import failure, and signal cleanup and asserts exactly one pool end() call.

The shell generates ownerLeaseSecret=base64url(randomBytes(32)) once per isolated run, exports PROGRESS_SYNC_OWNER_LEASE_SECRET with that exact value, and passes the same value to the integration dependency factory. A caller-supplied secret is rejected unless it is the generated value for this run. The secret remains in the shell and process environment only, is never printed, and is cleared before cleanup completes.

packages/db/src/index.ts is side-effect-free: importing it performs no new Pool, no drizzle(pool), and no module-scope database initialization. Its exact package export map is "." -> ./src/index.ts, "./schema" -> ./src/schema/index.ts, "./factory" -> ./src/factory.ts, "./progress-types" -> ./src/progress-types.ts, "./progress-adapter" -> ./src/progress-adapter.ts, and "./test-only-faults" -> ./src/test-only-faults.ts. The test-only subpath is physically available for the integration condition and is not re-exported by the root production entry or imported by production routes. test-only-faults.ts is the sole runtime definition of export const integrationFaultBrand: unique symbol and export const integrationProbeBrand: unique symbol; the shared type declarations use those exact unique-symbol types, and only createProgressIntegrationFaultPolicy and createIntegrationStatementProbe can construct the branded values. createProgressDbHandle(connectionString,source) creates exactly one Pool, one ProgressDb, one poolIdentity: symbol, one redacted databaseUrlDigest, and one close handle after exact URL validation; source is "production" or "integration". createOwnerLeaseSignerFromEnv() reads PROGRESS_SYNC_OWNER_LEASE_SECRET, requires at least 32 UTF-8 bytes, rejects missing, short, or non-string values before route construction, and returns the only OwnerLeaseSigner implementation. It signs and verifies the exact stable lease payload with HMAC-SHA-256, never logs or returns the secret, and rejects a production dependency graph that supplies any other signer. packages/db/src/factory.ts exports createProductionDbHandle(): ProgressDbHandle and getProductionDbHandle(): ProgressDbHandle; the first validates DATABASE_URL and the owner-lease secret, creates the production handle, and stores it under exactly one Symbol.for("hanks-hits.progress-production-db") registry record, while the second returns that same object and rejects a different URL, pool identity, or signer identity. There is no eager exported db value. apps/web/src/lib/auth.ts exports createAuth(dbHandle: ProgressDbHandle): AuthExports and deleteUserWithProgressLock(dbHandle,userId); it passes dbHandle.db, not the wrapper, to Auth.js DrizzleAdapter and first asserts that dbHandle.db.$client is the same object as dbHandle.pool. The deletion wrapper opens one typed transaction, takes the same progress:v1:user advisory lock as the progress adapter, executes the sole typed users delete, reads progress_owner_epochs in the same transaction, and returns the branded UserDeletionEvidence only when one user row was deleted and zero authority rows remain before commit. Auth.js account-management deletion calls this wrapper; no direct users.delete remains in production auth code. getProductionRouteDependencies() returns the same handle object, its poolIdentity, its validated ownerLeaseSigner, and the auth adapter dependency. The progress route, existing leaderboard routes, and auth adapter all assert object identity against this registry handle, so no route can bind another database. Integration dynamically imports @hank-neil/db/factory, @hank-neil/db/progress-adapter, and the route/auth factories only after the isolated URL and secret exist, creates one integration handle and one signer from the same validated secret, and injects those exact identities into auth and route dependencies. pool-registry.ts asserts one pool identity, one URL, one signer identity, no root singleton sentinel, no second pool, and no adapter bound to any other URL; db-harness.no-root-singleton includes production Auth.js construction, user-delete.lock-and-cascade as a subcase, and fails if the adapter receives the wrapper, a second pool, a different client identity, a different signer, or an unwrapped user delete.

7.2 Real blocker, observer, timeout, and rollback tests

The blocker connection and observer connection are separate clients acquired through dbHandle.pool.connect() from the same production-shaped ProgressDbHandle; apps/web integration code never imports pg, Pool, or a second driver dependency, and pool-registry.ts owns the typed handle and releases each checked-out client. The production adapter sets application_name with activityTagFor(applicationCorrelation,requestId)=`hhps:v1:${sha256(applicationCorrelation+":"+requestId).slice(0,48)}` through set_config('application_name',ctx.activityTag,true) before acquiring locks; the tag contains no raw user ID and is at most 63 ASCII bytes. The blocker starts a transaction, executes the exact user and app advisory locks for a seeded user/app, and holds them. The observer computes the same tag from the known correlation and request IDs, then polls pg_locks joined to pg_stat_activity by PID every 25 ms for at most 200 attempts or 5000 ms, whichever comes first, requiring the target lock request, exact application_name tag, and expected request/application correlation mapping. If the condition is absent at the deadline, it throws BlockerObserverTimeout with the last diagnostic snapshot and the harness fails closed. A contender then calls the production adapter and must receive 55P03 after 750 ms; that transaction is terminal and is not expected to resume. The blocker commits, the observer sees the waiter released, and a new contender attempt succeeds. A separate statement-timeout test constructs the branded integration fault policy; the adapter derives ctx.faultPolicy.statementProbe after both locks, executes SET LOCAL lock_timeout='0ms' on that same transaction, and SELECT pg_sleep(3) receives 57014 after 2000 ms and the transaction rolls back. All timing uses injected barriers and monotonic elapsed-time assertions; sleep alone is not authority.

progress.first-save.concurrent-absent is a real two-request proof, not a sequential replay. Seed the authenticated user and its valid owner lease, leave both app_progress and gaming_profiles absent for the target app, and start one blocker transaction that holds the exact user and app advisory locks through dbHandle.pool.connect(). Launch both authenticated POSTs with distinct request IDs, the same user/app/owner lease, distinct meaningful canonical payloads, merge:false, and baseServerVersion:null. Poll pg_locks until both request activity tags have granted=false waiters for the held lock and require the target progress row to remain absent at that observation. Release the blocker only after both waiters are observed, await both real route responses, and assert that exactly one request wins the lock and commits 200 merged:false with one progress row, one profile row, and one server version. The other request must receive exact 409 server_changed because its null replace baseline follows the committed winner; it performs no DML. Issue an authenticated mode-use GET after both responses and require its bytes, server version, and owner lease to match the committed winner response. If one waiter is missing, a row is observed before release, the loser returns 200, or a second profile exists, the case fails closed.

The branded fault construction is the test-only export createProgressIntegrationFaultPolicy({afterDmlBeforeCommit,statementProbe:createIntegrationStatementProbe()}) from packages/db/src/test-only-faults.ts; it returns one object carrying the private integrationFaultBrand and its separately branded IntegrationStatementProbe, and neither brand is exported by the production package entry point. The integration wrapper imports that factory only in integration setup and passes the returned object as the sole fault seam in ProgressTxContext.faultPolicy. The adapter invokes ctx.faultPolicy.statementProbe.run(tx) after locks and invokes ctx.faultPolicy.afterDmlBeforeCommit after progress upsert, profile creation/reuse, and all leaderboard upserts, before returning from the Drizzle transaction callback. The production constructor accepts only the literal production policy and rejects the test brand, a non-null probe, a non-null hook, and a fault object from an unknown module symbol. The rollback test snapshots owner authority row including ownerFingerprint,ownerGeneration,ownerEpoch,leaseId,issuedAt,expiresAt,signature, progress row including id,data,updatedAt,lastSyncedAt, profile including id,handle,updatedAt, leaderboard including id,score,scoreType,additionalStats,achievedAt,syncedAt, derived server version, and timestamps. After the injected throw, every snapshot is byte-for-byte unchanged and no partially created profile or leaderboard row remains.

7.3 Ordinary and integration Vitest isolation

SurfaceExact configurationFail-closed condition
Ordinaryapps/web/vitest.config.ts; jsdom; setup src/__tests__/setup.ts; include src/**/*.{test,spec}.{js,jsx,ts,tsx}; exclude src/integration/**; passWithNoTests:false; reporter scripts/progress-integration-reporter.ts with runner ordinary.Any integration file is collected, no ordinary test runs, the reporter brand is absent, or a terminal event is not recorded.
Integrationapps/web/vitest.integration.config.ts; node; setup src/integration/progress/setup.ts; include exactly src/integration/progress/**/*.integration.test.ts; exclude only node_modules/**, dist/**, and src/**/node_modules/** because the narrow include is the ordinary-test exclusion; passWithNoTests:false; fileParallelism:false; maxWorkers:1; minWorkers:1; sequence.concurrent:false; reporter scripts/progress-integration-reporter.ts with runner integration.Missing PROGRESS_SYNC_TEST_DATABASE_URL, setup failure, DB push failure, reporter event missing, unhandled error, skipped/todo/filtered test, duplicate terminal observation, an uninvoked required oracle, or a test name without one required ID exits nonzero.

Both configs receive a generated ProofRunnerConfig with the same invocation runId and manifest digest, a distinct runner config digest, the exact expected IDs, and its own private execution registry. The harness constructs one createProofRunController before starting the two processes, wires its completion callback to assertProofCaseRunnerCoverage, forwards each reporter's serialized summary through the channel, and calls finalize() only after both process promises settle. No in-memory registry is shared across processes and no direct object handoff is accepted.

The integration script passes no name filters and no focused-file arguments. The reporter implements the installed Vitest 4 onTestCaseResult and onTestRunEnd events. It records one terminal observation per exact generated required ID and per named integration proof ID, rejects duplicate events, rejects unknown proof evidence IDs, ignores unrelated ordinary test cases, rejects a nonterminal expected proof state, and compares the expected union to the observed terminal multiset. Before a required case can report terminal success, its test calls the generated oracle's function-valued setup, assertion, and terminal-evidence hooks; each hook records the observed response and row/lock/envelope evidence under that exact ID. The terminal reporter requires the oracle invocation token and a private combined projection evidence record; the combined record proves the shared ledger, real extractor and validator calls, exact call counts for projection-required IDs, and ordering before first DML. createNoProjectionInvocationEvidence is accepted only for a harness-observed no-projection branch and carries an explicit reason. The generated required subset has exactly 21 IDs, and every one must pass exactly once. One integration test file may contain all required cases and named proof cases, but every ID must appear once in a fully collected test case.

8. SyncMetadataV1 and recursive policy grammar

8.1 One metadata channel

type SyncMetadataV1 = {
  version: 1;
  ownerFingerprint: OwnerFingerprint | null;
  sourceEpoch: number;
  entries: Readonly<Record<LivePathKey, SyncPathMetadataV1>>;
};
type SyncPathMetadataV1 = {
  sourceEpoch: number;
  resetEpoch: number;
  clockMs: number;
  mutationId: string;
  kind: "value" | "tombstone" | "reset";
  inheritedFrom: LivePathKey | null;
};
type LocalProgressEnvelopeV1<P> = {
  state: P;
  version: 1;
  sync: SyncMetadataV1;
};
type ServerStoredEnvelopeV1<C> = {
  data: C;
  sync: SyncMetadataV1;
};
type PersistedDecodeResult<P> =
  | { kind: "current"; envelope: LocalProgressEnvelopeV1<P> }
  | { kind: "legacy-unowned"; state: P; sourceKey: string; sourceVersion: number; migrationId: string; requiresGuestBinding: true };
type LegacyZustandRecordV0 = {
  state: unknown;
  version: 0;
};
type LegacyGuestMigrationResult<P> = {
  sourceKey: string;
  sourceVersion: number;
  migrationId: string;
  state: P;
  envelope: LocalProgressEnvelopeV1<P>;
  primaryReadBack: string;
  legacyRemovalRequired: boolean;
};
type LegacyServerBareJsonbMetadataV1 = {
  source: "server-bare-jsonb";
  sourceEpoch: 0;
  mutationId: "legacy-server-bare-jsonb";
  clockMs: 0;
  presentValuePaths: readonly LivePathKey[];
  absentPathsHaveNoCandidate: true;
  tombstones: readonly LivePathKey[];
};
type MetadataMigration = "none" | "legacy-missing-sync-guest-only" | "server-bare-jsonb-source-epoch-zero";
declare function synthesizeLegacyServerMetadata<C>(
  canonical: CanonicalServerData<C>,
  policy: PolicyRoot,
  ownerFingerprint: OwnerFingerprint
): { envelope: ServerStoredEnvelopeV1<C>; evidence: LegacyServerBareJsonbMetadataV1 };

SyncMetadataV1 is embedded beside state in the local record and beside data in every request, response, and appProgress.data JSONB value. The wire field is also named sync. There is no metadata column and no metadata-specific database migration. A metadata entry is concrete and records the leaf value, tombstone, or reset, or records that the leaf inherits the nearest validated ancestor entry through inheritedFrom. Inherited entries are compact protocol data, not wildcard paths: their source/reset/clock/mutation tuple must equal the ancestor and the ancestor must be a strict concrete prefix. The server adapter reads a bare legacy JSONB progress object, validates it against the app schema and policy, and calls synthesizeLegacyServerMetadata: every present canonical leaf and identified present collection item receives a kind:"value" entry at sourceEpoch:0, clockMs:0, and mutation ID legacy-server-bare-jsonb, with inherited descendants sharing the nearest concrete parent binding; explicit nullable null values receive a value entry; no tombstone is invented for absent members; resettable subtrees receive reset epoch 0; absent paths have no merge candidate. The envelope owner fingerprint is the authenticated user's fingerprint. The synthesized envelope is used immediately for GET, merge, and canonical response, then written back on the next successful save. This preserves meaningful legacy server values for MAX, LWW, reset, nullable, and tombstone-aware paths instead of replacing them with entries:[]. metadata.server-bare-jsonb-merge covers each of those policy classes, proves absent paths remain absent candidates, exercises 5000 seen-joke IDs and nested saved-beat scopes, and asserts byte-stable write-back. A client historical local record without sync decodes to an internal unowned migration result, never to an already-owned synchronized envelope. The client runs that result only after a verified guest owner claim with an absent marker or an active guest marker; it validates the entry's named migration, converter, schema, and meaningfulness, writes a guest-owned version-1 envelope to the primary key, reads back the exact canonical bytes, and only then removes a legacy key through the validated removal path. An authenticated owner, a foreign user marker, a malformed marker with meaningful bytes, or any unverified ownership state quarantines the original bytes and performs no migration or adoption. Empty/default historical bytes may be rebound only in the verified guest branch. The canonical response always returns data and sync together.

synthesizeLegacyServerMetadata executes visit(node,value,concretePath,resetEpoch) over the compiled PolicyRoot, not over a guessed leaf list. It applies the node's generated PresenceRule before allocation, so valid nullable lastLocation, currentEvent, and currentRiver values are preserved while absent and invalid composite values remain distinct. exclude emits nothing; alias visits its target; object requires an object and visits every declared child; scalar LWW, MAX, MIN, balance, boolean, virtual-day, validated-static, and bounded opaque nodes emit one value entry when present, including explicit nullable null; resettable visits its child with reset epoch 0 and emits no invented reset or tombstone; setUnion validates a bounded array, canonicalizes values, and emits one concrete identity entry per present item; unionById, timestampedItemArray, and authoritativeCollection validate arrays, extract declared identities, reject conflicting duplicate identities, and visit items under |k:${encodeURIComponent(identity)}; dynamicRecord validates each key against its declared pattern and visits its child under the encoded record key; indexedArray validates dense indices below its cap and visits each item under canonical decimal |i:; timestampedRecord validates each record's declared valuePath and timestampPath as epoch milliseconds, applies its min/max comparison, and uses mutation ID then canonical bytes for equal timestamps; and authoritativeScalarCollection emits value-identity entries in source order. There is no implicit unknown-child policy: an unknown child rejects unless the manifest explicitly declares an opaqueBounded node with its own max bytes, nullable rule, and metadata binding. It rejects unknown or excluded members, missing declared children beneath a present parent, invalid identities, null at a non-nullable node, duplicate identities, depth/path/byte caps, and opaque payloads over their node cap before allocating an entry. The output has source epoch 0, clock 0, mutation ID legacy-server-bare-jsonb, concrete live paths only, no tombstones, no candidates for absent paths, and entries sorted by concrete path before stable serialization. Matrix-driven tests cover A5 opaque root, A7 excluded full-store fields and canonical counters, A10 union and timestamped arrays, A12 nullable identity collections, A17 authoritative collections and opaque save state, A22 bounded opaque thumbnails, A25 dynamic records and indexed patterns, and A26 resettable/missing values; each case has malformed, duplicate, null, absent, cap, and valid recursive branches.

Metadata bindings are templates whenever a policy path contains a wildcard or dynamic identity. The compiler emits one concrete binding for a fixed scalar and one MetadataBindingTemplate for each union-by-ID, record-key, timestamped-array, authoritative-collection, indexed-array, and resettable subtree, including identity extractor, ordered wildcard scopes, clock path, reset action IDs, source range, setter fingerprint, completion mode, and mutation fingerprint. expandMetadataBindings(policy,canonical,provenance) recursively walks the same validated value as the converter, expands outer then inner scopes, extracts declared identities, materializes concrete LivePathKeys, and refuses a wildcard or template path in SyncMetadataV1.entries. A nested A25 binding expands saved-beat ID, dynamic pattern key, and dense pattern index in that order; an A10 set with 5000 IDs produces 5000 concrete item entries without exceeding the derived metadata capacity. Removal emits a tombstone at the materialized identity path; a reset action emits a reset entry at the resettable root; an equal-clock mutation carries its mutation ID and canonical-byte fingerprint. commitSourceMutation supplies the actual action ID, source range, setter fingerprint, completion result, and mutation fingerprint to every expansion, so a direct setProgress, direct API setter, collection removal, reset, replay, or asynchronous completion cannot share a guessed concrete binding. provenance.partialized-paths.test.ts covers template expansion, direct-caller provenance, removal tombstones, reset entries, equal-clock ties, and no-resurrection after late asynchronous adoption; provenance.async-adoption-no-resurrection.test.ts covers the same binding after Drawing and Memory Match completion.

The single shared protocol type module is packages/db/src/progress-types.ts. It owns OwnerFingerprint, OwnerTuple, SyncMetadataV1, SyncPathMetadataV1, PathKey, PolicyNode, both envelope types, wire identity types, and the Drizzle adapter types. apps/web/src/shared/lib/progress-sync-metadata.ts imports those types and supplies browser validators, codecs, and converters; it does not redeclare them. Coordinator-only registration and browser runtime types remain in apps/web and never flow back into packages/db, so the package dependency graph remains packages/db -> no web package and apps/web -> @hank-neil/db.

type MetadataCapacityProjection = {
  maxConcreteEntries: number;
  maxSerializedBytes: number;
  witness: { appId: ValidAppId; pathCount: number; nestedScopeDepth: number };
};
declare function deriveMetadataCapacity(manifest: ProgressSyncManifestInput): MetadataCapacityProjection;
declare function deriveIndependentMetadataCapacity(rows: readonly SourcePolicyOracleRow[]): MetadataCapacityProjection;
function assertMetadataCapacity(
  manifest: ProgressSyncManifestInput,
  entries: Readonly<Record<LivePathKey, SyncPathMetadataV1>>,
  serializedByteLength: number
): void {
  const projection = deriveMetadataCapacity(manifest);
  const independent = deriveIndependentMetadataCapacity(deriveIndependentSourcePolicyOracle());
  if (projection.maxConcreteEntries !== GENERATED_METADATA_FANOUT_CAP || projection.maxConcreteEntries !== independent.maxConcreteEntries || projection.maxSerializedBytes !== independent.maxSerializedBytes) throw new Error("generated metadata capacity diverges from independent source capacity");
  if (Object.keys(entries).length > projection.maxConcreteEntries || serializedByteLength > projection.maxSerializedBytes) throw new Error("metadata capacity exceeded");
  if (projection.witness.pathCount < 5000 || projection.witness.nestedScopeDepth < 3) throw new Error("metadata capacity projection witness missing");
}
const GENERATED_METADATA_FANOUT_CAP = 32768;
const GENERATED_METADATA_CAPACITY: MetadataCapacityProjection = { maxConcreteEntries: GENERATED_METADATA_FANOUT_CAP, maxSerializedBytes: 4194304, witness: { appId: "joke-generator", pathCount: 5000, nestedScopeDepth: 3 } };
const MAX_METADATA_ENTRIES = GENERATED_METADATA_CAPACITY.maxConcreteEntries;

Validation uses MAX_METADATA_ENTRIES=GENERATED_METADATA_CAPACITY.maxConcreteEntries, where the generated capacity projection is recomputed from all 32 manifest policies and compared to an independently parsed source-policy capacity. The projection's witness includes A10's seenJokeIds cap 5000, A10 ratings cap 2000, A25 saved-beat and nested-pattern fanout, and the largest reset/inheritance expansion; the validator recomputes both concrete-entry and serialized-byte bounds and fails when the generated bound, independent bound, or nested-scope depth is insufficient. The checked-in generated capacity is 32768 entries and 4194304 serialized bytes only as a rendered projection of that comparison, not as an authority. MAX_PATH_LENGTH=512, MAX_MUTATION_ID_LENGTH=128, MAX_ENVELOPE_BYTES=196608, and unsigned safe integers for independent source/reset epochs remain fixed. The wire body additionally uses MAX_POST_PROTOCOL_OVERHEAD_BYTES=65536 and MAX_REQUEST_BYTES=262144. clockMs is finite, non-negative, and no more than 86400000 ms ahead of the injected server clock. Entries must have valid concrete LivePathKeys, valid mutation IDs, source epoch equal to the envelope source epoch, reset epoch at most MAX_RESET_EPOCH=9007199254740991, and one of the three kinds. An inherited entry must name a strict concrete ancestor, have no tombstone/reset kind, and match the ancestor's source/reset/clock/mutation tuple. Wire and server validation require a non-null owner fingerprint; only the internal legacy-local migration result may have a null fingerprint. Duplicate paths, duplicate metadata records, unknown metadata fields, caps exceeded, invalid timestamps, invalid inheritance, and malformed version throw SyncMetadataValidationError. Missing metadata is the single historical migration case. Metadata failure never falls back to a timestamp-only merge.

Rendered projectionValueIndependent witness
maxConcreteEntries32768A10 seenJokeIds pathCount 5000
maxSerializedBytes4194304A25 nestedScopeDepth 3

sourceEpoch changes on every owner-epoch rotation, including same-user document claims, explicit guest/user transitions, and foreign-owner claims, and is copied into every new path entry. Rebinding to a new epoch retains the owner fingerprint, values, path clocks, and mutation IDs. A reset at a subtree path increments that path's independent resetEpoch, writes a kind:"reset" entry, and causes descendants with lower reset epoch to lose. Equal reset epoch compares clockMs, then kind precedence, then mutation ID. Server canonical returns preserve metadata received after validated merge. A missing path means no candidate. A null value is an explicit value candidate for nullable LWW. A tombstone deletes an identified set or collection member and prevents resurrection while its reset epoch is at least the candidate's reset epoch; a higher reset epoch is required for resurrection. An authoritative collection owns absence in its current snapshot.

A POST body is valid only when sync.sourceEpoch equals the request owner's ownerEpoch; a mismatch is a typed 409 stale_owner_epoch before DML. A GET may return a server envelope from a lower source epoch. The coordinator validates it, rebases its metadata to the current owner epoch without changing canonical values, and stores it only as a GET baseline until the local owner transition is complete. A server epoch higher than the current owner epoch is a typed ownership conflict and blocks adoption.

8.2 Compilable PathKey grammar and codec

type DecimalIndex = string & { readonly __decimalIndex: unique symbol };
type PathSegment =
  | { kind: "property"; value: string }
  | { kind: "index"; value: number }
  | { kind: "identity"; value: string };
type PathSuffix = ReadonlyArray<PathSegment>;
type PathSuffixText = string & { readonly __pathSuffixText: unique symbol };
type PathKey = string & { readonly __pathKey: unique symbol };
type MetadataKind = "value" | "tombstone" | "reset";
type LivePathKey = PathKey & { readonly __livePathKey: unique symbol };
type MetadataPathKey = string & { readonly __metadataPathKey: unique symbol };
type MetadataPathSegment = { kind: "metadata"; metadataKind: MetadataKind; livePath: LivePathKey };
type WildcardScope = {
  ordinal: number;
  parent: LivePathKey | ScopedWildcardPolicyPath;
  collectionKind: "unionById" | "dynamicRecord" | "timestampedItemArray" | "indexedArray" | "authoritativeCollection";
  identityPath: LivePathKey | ScopedWildcardPolicyPath | null;
};
type ScopedWildcardPolicyPath = string & { readonly __scopedWildcardPolicyPath: unique symbol };
type WildcardPolicyPath = ScopedWildcardPolicyPath & { readonly scopes: readonly WildcardScope[] };
type IdentityFieldPath = WildcardPolicyPath & { readonly __identityFieldPath: unique symbol };
type PolicyPath = LivePathKey | WildcardPolicyPath;
type PolicyPathMode = "concrete" | "dynamic-wildcard";
function encodePathPart(value: string): string;
function decodePathPart(value: string): string;
function parseDecimalIndex(value: string): DecimalIndex;
function pathKey(suffix: PathSuffix): LivePathKey;
function metadataPath(kind: MetadataKind, livePath: LivePathKey): MetadataPathKey;
function parseMetadataPath(value: string): MetadataPathSegment;
declare function policyPath(parent: LivePathKey, suffix: PathSuffix): LivePathKey;
declare function wildcardPolicyPath(parent: LivePathKey, property: string, scopeOrdinal: number): WildcardPolicyPath;
declare function identityFieldPath(parent: WildcardPolicyPath, property: string, scopeOrdinal: number): IdentityFieldPath;
declare function expandPolicyPath(path: WildcardPolicyPath, scopes: readonly WildcardScope[], value: unknown): readonly LivePathKey[];
declare function compilePolicyPath(raw: string, mode: PolicyPathMode): PolicyPath;
declare function resolvePolicyChild(parent: PolicyPath, suffix: PathSuffix): PolicyPath;
declare function renderPolicyPath(path: PolicyPath): string;

The concrete live grammar is $ for root, then a sequence of |p:${encodeURIComponent(property)}, |i:${DecimalIndex}, or |k:${encodeURIComponent(identity)}. DecimalIndex is canonical decimal, zero is written 0, leading zeroes are rejected, and the value must be a safe integer below the generated array cap. PathSuffix is the typed segment sequence before serialization. PathKey is the serialized result and LivePathKey is the branded concrete synchronized key. Metadata paths use $|m:${kind}|t:${encodeURIComponent(livePath)}; parseMetadataPath returns a MetadataPathSegment after decoding the target exactly once and parsing it as a LivePathKey. Metadata keys are derived diagnostics only: SyncMetadataV1.entries contains live-path keys exclusively, and a MetadataPathKey can never be used as a policy path or an entries key. A policy wildcard is a separate branded AST path with an ordered scope list: |* follows its declared dynamic collection or record boundary, carries one increasing scope ordinal, and may be followed by concrete property, index, or identity-child segments. A second nested scope is valid only after the first scope's child collection boundary. identityFieldPath is the only constructor for a union or authoritative item identity and returns a dedicated IdentityFieldPath branded to its scoped item property; the compiler rejects a collection root, a relative label, or a concrete identity segment in that position. The valid A25 shapes are $|p:savedBeats|*|p:id, $|p:savedBeats|*|p:pattern|*, and $|p:savedBeats|*|p:pattern|*|i:0; $|p:trucks|*|* and a wildcard without its declared parent are rejected. policyPath(parent,suffix) appends typed concrete segments, wildcardPolicyPath(parent,property,scopeOrdinal) returns a scoped path with an ordered scope record, and expandPolicyPath(path,scopes,value) recursively materializes outer then inner scopes into concrete paths. compilePolicyPath(raw,mode) returns a branded PolicyPath only after root, segment, scope-order, cap, and re-encode validation. Relative labels such as id, record, baseStats, vehicleUpgrades.*.*, and problemsSolved.+ are renderer labels, never AST input.

encodePathPart is exactly encodeURIComponent. The codec examples are fixed: encodeURIComponent("art-17") is art-17, while encodeURIComponent("a|b:c/$") is a%7Cb%3Ac%2F%24. Decode must decode once, reject malformed percent sequences, reject reserved raw separators, re-encode to the same canonical bytes, and reject an empty property, empty identity, non-canonical index, metadata segment in a live path, duplicate identity segment, property after an index where the constructor forbids it, a wildcard in a concrete value, a raw relative path, .*, and .+. Root has no suffix. The parser rejects a path depth above 64 and a serialized length above 512. compilePolicyPath rejects any raw string that does not begin with $, any unencoded separator, any relative child label, a wildcard before its collection scope, a wildcard after a scalar leaf, a repeated wildcard at one boundary, a nested wildcard whose ordinal skips or reverses order, and any concrete path containing |*; it accepts an ordered prefix wildcard followed by a concrete child segment or a declared nested scope.

8.3 Policy AST constructors and comparison order

type PolicyOrder = "identity-then-timestamp-then-bytes" | "timestamp-desc-then-identity" | "source-order";
type PresenceRule = {
  nullable: boolean;
  missing: "absent" | "default" | "invalid";
  null: "value" | "absent" | "invalid";
  compositeNull: "value" | "absent" | "invalid";
};
type ScopeOrdinals = readonly number[];
type PolicyNode = { presence: PresenceRule } & (
  | { kind: "object"; path: PolicyPath; children: readonly PolicyNode[] }
  | { kind: "scalarLww"; path: PolicyPath; nullable: boolean; clock: "metadata" }
  | { kind: "validatedStatic"; path: PolicyPath; nullable: boolean; comparison: "equal-or-reject" }
  | { kind: "maxCounter"; path: PolicyPath; bound: number }
  | { kind: "booleanMonotonic"; path: PolicyPath; resettable: boolean }
  | { kind: "minBestTime"; path: PolicyPath; nullable: boolean; bound: number }
  | { kind: "balanceLww"; path: PolicyPath; bound: number }
  | { kind: "virtualPetDay"; path: PolicyPath; fallbackPath: PolicyPath; nullable: boolean }
  | { kind: "sumLedger"; path: PolicyPath; deltaId: PolicyPath; bound: number }
  | { kind: "setUnion"; path: PolicyPath; item: "string"; tombstones: boolean; cap: number }
  | { kind: "unionById"; path: LivePathKey; identity: IdentityFieldPath; children: readonly PolicyNode[]; cap: number; tombstones: boolean; scopes: ScopeOrdinals }
  | { kind: "timestampedItemArray"; path: LivePathKey; identity: IdentityFieldPath; timestamp: PolicyPath; cap: number; tombstones: boolean; order: "identity-then-timestamp-then-bytes"; scopes: ScopeOrdinals }
  | { kind: "dynamicRecord"; path: LivePathKey; keyPattern: string; keyIdentity: "record-key"; timestamp: PolicyPath | null; child: PolicyNode; cap: number; tombstones: boolean; scopes: ScopeOrdinals }
  | { kind: "timestampedRecord"; path: LivePathKey; keyPattern: string; keyIdentity: "record-key"; valuePath: PolicyPath; timestampPath: PolicyPath; timestampType: "epoch-ms"; comparison: "min" | "max"; equalTimestamp: "mutation-id-then-bytes"; cap: number; tombstones: boolean; scopes: ScopeOrdinals }
  | { kind: "indexedArray"; path: PolicyPath; indexCap: number; child: PolicyNode; scopes: ScopeOrdinals }
  | { kind: "opaqueBounded"; path: PolicyPath; maxBytes: number; nullable: boolean }
  | { kind: "resettable"; path: PolicyPath; resetActions: readonly string[]; child: PolicyNode }
  | { kind: "authoritativeCollection"; path: LivePathKey; identity: IdentityFieldPath; cap: number; order: "timestamp-desc-then-identity" | "source-order"; tombstones: boolean; scopes: ScopeOrdinals }
  | { kind: "authoritativeScalarCollection"; path: LivePathKey; item: "string"; cap: number; order: "source-order"; tombstones: false }
  | { kind: "alias"; path: PolicyPath; target: PolicyPath }
  | { kind: "exclude"; path: PolicyPath; reason: "runtime-only" | "device-only" | "server-derived" }
);
type PolicyCaps = {
  maxDepth: 64;
  maxPathLength: 512;
  maxArrayItems: 5000;
  maxRecordKeys: 500;
  maxOpaqueBytes: 1000000;
};
const MAX_COUNT = 9007199254740991;
const MAX_CURRENCY = 9007199254740991;
const MAX_DRAWING_BLOB_BYTES = 65536;
type PolicySemanticEvidence = {
  path: LivePathKey;
  policyKind: PolicyNode["kind"];
  sourceModule: string;
  sourceRange: SourceRange;
  sourcePath: string;
  sourceFingerprint: string;
  sourceFact: "counter" | "boolean-monotonic" | "absolute-balance" | "best-time" | "set" | "identity-collection" | "authoritative-collection" | "authoritative-scalar-collection" | "timestamped-value" | "resettable" | "opaque" | "static-definition" | "runtime-only";
  migrationId: string | null;
};
type MutationProvenanceBinding = {
  actionId: string;
  sourceModule: string;
  sourceRange: SourceRange;
  setterFingerprint: string;
  completion: MutationCompletion;
  metadataKind: MetadataKind;
  mutationFingerprint: string;
};
type MetadataBindingTemplate = {
  bindingId: string;
  pathTemplate: WildcardPolicyPath;
  identityTemplate: IdentityFieldPath | null;
  identityKind: "none" | "union-id" | "record-key" | "array-index" | "scalar-value";
  clockPathTemplate: WildcardPolicyPath | null;
  resetActionIds: readonly string[];
  provenance: readonly MutationProvenanceBinding[];
};
type MetadataBinding =
  | {
      mode: "concrete";
      bindingId: string;
      livePath: LivePathKey;
      kind: MetadataKind;
      clockPath: LivePathKey | null;
      resetActionIds: readonly string[];
      provenance: readonly MutationProvenanceBinding[];
    }
  | {
      mode: "template";
      template: MetadataBindingTemplate;
    };
type ExpandedMetadataBinding = {
  bindingId: string;
  livePath: LivePathKey;
  identity: string | null;
  kind: MetadataKind;
  clockPath: LivePathKey | null;
  resetActionIds: readonly string[];
  provenance: readonly MutationProvenanceBinding[];
};
type PolicyRoot = {
  appId: ValidAppId;
  root: PolicyNode;
  caps: PolicyCaps;
  metadataBindings: readonly MetadataBinding[];
  semanticEvidence: readonly PolicySemanticEvidence[];
};
declare function expandMetadataBindings(
  policy: PolicyRoot,
  canonical: CanonicalServerData<CanonicalProgressRecord>,
  provenance: readonly MutationProvenanceBinding[]
): readonly ExpandedMetadataBinding[];
type PersistedUnknownPathFailure = {
  code: "unknown_persisted_path";
  path: LivePathKey;
  surface: PersistedSurface;
  source: "local" | "wire" | "stored" | "merge";
};
type PersistedSurface = "local-full-store" | "local-canonical" | "wire" | "server-jsonb" | "merge";
type PersistedAllowedPathSet = {
  surface: PersistedSurface;
  allowedPaths: readonly LivePathKey[];
  excludedPaths: readonly LivePathKey[];
  preserveRuntimeState: boolean;
};
declare function assertNoUnknownPersistedPaths(
  raw: unknown,
  schemaRoot: PolicyPath,
  allowed: PersistedAllowedPathSet,
  source: PersistedUnknownPathFailure["source"]
): void;

Every scalar constructor has an explicit comparison. validatedStatic accepts equal canonical values and rejects divergent values before merge; it is used for source-owned definitions whose content is not a user progress decision. booleanMonotonic accepts only false or true, merges false with true as true, and requires resettable:true only when a named reset wrapper owns the false transition. The current source stores absolute snapshots, so no current 32-schema path uses sumLedger. A future sum path must have a source delta ID and one bounded event ledger; it cannot be added by classifying a total as a sum. Balances and spendable quantities use balanceLww with metadata clock because independent absolute balances cannot be safely added. Maxima and monotonic counters use maxCounter. Best times use minBestTime. Sets use union plus tombstones. Timestamped arrays carry identity, timestamp, cap, tombstone behavior, and deterministic order. Dynamic records carry record-key identity, a declared timestamp field or null, cap, tombstone behavior, and a child policy. Timestamped records use record-key identity plus epoch-ms values and an explicit min/max comparison. Authoritative collections declare identity, cap, timestamp-descending order, and tombstone behavior; scalar collections use value identity, source order, no per-item timestamp, and no tombstones. Opaque values use bounded timestamped LWW. Runtime, server-derived, and device-only fields use explicit exclusions.

The exact constructor set is object(path,children,presence), scalarLww(path,nullable,presence), validatedStatic(path,nullable,presence), maxCounter(path,bound,presence), booleanMonotonic(path,resettable,presence), minBestTime(path,nullable,bound,presence), balanceLww(path,bound,presence), virtualPetDay(path,fallbackPath,nullable,presence), sumLedger(path,deltaId,bound,presence), setUnion(path,cap,tombstones,presence), unionById(path,identity,children,cap,tombstones,scopes,presence), timestampedItemArray(path,identity,timestamp,cap,tombstones,order,scopes,presence), dynamicRecord(path,keyPattern,keyIdentity,timestamp,child,cap,tombstones,scopes,presence), timestampedRecord(path,keyPattern,keyIdentity,valuePath,timestampPath,timestampType,comparison,equalTimestamp,cap,tombstones,scopes,presence), indexedArray(path,indexCap,child,scopes,presence), opaqueBounded(path,maxBytes,nullable,presence), resettable(path,resetActions,child,presence), authoritativeCollection(path,identity,cap,order,tombstones,scopes,presence), authoritativeScalarCollection(path,item,cap,order,presence), alias(path,target,presence), and exclude(path,reason,presence). The compiler rejects a missing identity, timestamp, order, tombstone rule, cap, presence rule, or metadata binding. Matrix labels omit the explicit presence object only as a rendering abbreviation; generated AST nodes always carry it. Achievements unlocks compile to timestampedRecord by achievement key with valuePath, timestampPath, epoch-millisecond validation, earliest valid unlock timestamp, equalTimestamp:"mutation-id-then-bytes", and explicit tombstones. Virtual Pet inventory compiles to unionById by item ID with zero-quantity tombstones. Retro Arcade recently played compiles to authoritativeCollection with source order timestamp-desc-then-identity and cap 20. Toy Finder recently viewed compiles to authoritativeScalarCollection(path=$|p:recentlyViewed,item=string,cap=20,order="source-order",tombstones=false); its source array order is retained after value de-duplication and cap enforcement, with no item timestamp or item-object identity. virtualPetDay compares the parsed local calendar day, then the declared fallback metadata clock at lastModified, then the common equal-time tie rule; it is not an untyped scalar alias.

Merge validation and comparison order is fixed: validate stored and incoming canonical data; validate metadata; apply each node's PresenceRule; compare the nearest reset epoch; compare authoritative collection snapshots; compare tombstones; compare union item identities; compare node-specific values; then compare metadata clock. A missing path follows only its declared missing rule: absent creates no candidate, default materializes the schema default without a mutation clock, and invalid rejects. A null follows only its declared null or compositeNull rule; nullable scalar and collection values may emit a value entry, while a non-nullable composite rejects null before child traversal. At equal clock, reset beats tombstone, tombstone beats value, and lexicographically greater mutation ID breaks a value tie. A missing field is absence and never clears a present value unless an authoritative collection owns that absence. An empty set is not a tombstone. Duplicate set items collapse by canonical JSON; union-by-ID duplicates collapse by identity only when canonical bytes are equal, otherwise they reject; timestamped records validate the declared value and timestamp child paths, apply the declared min or max rule, then use mutation ID and canonical bytes for an equal timestamp. Arrays are canonicalized by identity then timestamp then stable bytes, never input order. Caps are enforced before allocation and after merge.

policyAstCoverage recursively walks every PolicyNode for all 32 roots. It rejects wildcard-only concrete paths, wildcard identity shorthand, an untyped static-field note in place of a validatedStatic node, an unbound source field, an omitted child, and a node lacking its declared cap, identity, timestamp, order, tombstone rule, reset action list, or metadata binding. It compares the source path projection and AST path projection in both directions with exact cardinality, and requires dynamic key patterns to be declared only in dynamicRecord/unionById nodes. policy.no-shorthand-ast.test.ts is a named assertion inside the policy test file and fails before merge tests when any shorthand or unbound path appears.

PolicySemanticEvidence is mandatory for every non-excluded semantic node. assertPolicyEvidence(root,sourceOracle) requires one evidence row for each compiled concrete policy kind, exact LivePathKey/policyKind bidirectional equality, a source module and source range whose SHA-256 fingerprint matches the independent source parse, a source path that exists in the source schema/partializer, a declared source fact, and the exact migration ID when a source literal is being canonicalized. The independent oracle supplies expected semantic facts from source actions, schemas, fixtures, and golden cases; it never reads the generated node to infer MAX, MIN, BAL, AUTH, RESET, or OPAQUE. The oracle separately proves that Oregon's current source literal "very poor" has a legacy source range and fingerprint, maps through migration ID oregon.health-very-poor-to-very_poor, and compiles to canonical "very_poor"; a matrix row containing only the future literal without legacy evidence fails oregon.health-migration-source-evidence. The same independent evidence check covers Math Attack totalCorrect, every other additive/monotonic field, balances, minima, identity collections, authoritative collections, resettable subtrees, and runtime exclusions.

Every persisted object schema is recursive strict(), and the generated source graph emits a distinct PersistedAllowedPathSet for each surface. local-full-store is the A7 full Zustand record and permits the explicitly listed runtime/local fields needed to preserve an owned board snapshot; local-canonical is the generated synchronized projection and excludes those runtime fields; wire, server-jsonb, and merge use the canonical set only. The local full-store set never crosses into canonical conversion, request construction, server JSONB, or merge. Before Zod normalization, converter selection, stored-row merge, or canonical response construction, assertNoUnknownPersistedPaths walks raw object keys and array indices, resolves each concrete path against the surface's allowed and excluded sets, and throws PersistedUnknownPathFailure with the surface for an unknown nested key, excluded field, duplicate identity, or unbounded index. A strict schema therefore cannot strip a rogue child silently. The same no-network/no-DML rule applies to every surface: a failure leaves live state and the stored row unchanged, performs no DML, sends no network, and cannot set READY. converter.full-store-2048 proves the allowed local board/runtime paths remain local while canonical/wire/server/merge reject them; policy.unknown-nested-path supplies rogue and excluded nested fields to local-full-store, local-canonical, wire, server-jsonb, and merge, including a 2048 local hydration at the exact boundary.

8.4 Virtual Pet fallback

Virtual Pet lastPlayDate uses the local source form produced by new Date().toDateString(), exactly Www Mmm DD YYYY with the source's padded day. The current source default "" is the sole canonical missing sentinel for this field. A legacy converter accepts the unpadded day for migration only, validates the local calendar date, and writes the canonical padded form. The metadata binding uses the parsed local date at lastPlayDate; the canonical missing sentinel and a schema-level missing/null value fall back to the root lastModified clock. An invalid date, ISO timestamp, unknown timezone, or empty value on any other date path is invalid. A valid date later than the injected local today is a typed VirtualPetFutureDate failure: the live state, storage bytes, network queue, and READY state remain unchanged. policy.virtual-pet-empty-default covers the source default, legacy day, missing, null, duplicate dates, future date, and equal-time tie.

9. Source-grounded 32-schema semantic matrix

The following matrix is a generated HTML rendering of the 32 PolicyRoot ASTs and their PolicySemanticEvidence; it is not a second semantic input. The authoritative manifest stores rooted PolicyPath nodes and the renderer shortens child labels only for display. parsePlanProjection treats these cells as output and compares them to regenerated rows, while compilePolicyPath never accepts the displayed relative labels. The matrix is projected from current PROGRESS_SCHEMAS, current store partializers, current getProgress/setProgress paths, source actions, and leaderboard extractors. The abbreviations are rendered labels: MAX is monotonic maximum; MIN is lower best-time; BAL is timestamped balance LWW; LWW is timestamped scalar; SET is union; ID is union by declared identity; AUTH is authoritative collection; OPAQUE is bounded payload LWW; RESET is reset-epoch comparison; EXCLUDE is runtime/local-only. Absolute additive totals are MAX, never SUM, because the current source has no delta ledger.

A30 math-attackhighScore,totalCorrect,totalAnswered,longestCombo,problemsSolved.+,problemsSolved.-,problemsSolved.×,problemsSolved.÷,gamesPlayed:MAX; settings.soundEnabled,settings.difficulty,lastModified:LWW.The independent test uses local totalCorrect:8, server totalCorrect:5, older local clock, and expects 8. A32 achievementsunlocked:timestampedRecord(keyIdentity=record-key,timestampValue=epoch-ms,comparison=min,cap=500,tombstones=true) by achievement ID, earliest valid unlock timestamp wins; lastModified:LWW.watermarks and celebrationQueue are runtime/local-only, removed from synchronized partialization, and have no metadata or server path.

The generated policy compiler rejects a field present in the source partializer but absent from the matrix, and rejects a matrix field absent from the source schema/projection. The independent oracle mutates divergent values at the source action boundary, not through generated merge helpers. It includes Math Attack totalCorrect, every MAX row, every MIN row, every BAL row, every SET/ID row, every RESET action, every nullable field, every opaque cap, and every authoritative collection.

A10 identity handling is exact. For favorites, an exact duplicate canonical object with the same id and identical bytes collapses to one item; the same id with any differing setup, punchline, category, or savedAt rejects before merge with DuplicateIdentity. removeFavorite emits one tombstone for that ID. For ratings, each jokeId has one winner: higher ratedAt wins; equal ratedAt compares metadata clock, then lexicographically greater mutation ID, then canonical bytes; an exact equal-provenance replay is idempotent. Ratings have no tombstone path because the source has no rating-delete action. policy.a10-source-grounding includes direct duplicate favorites, direct duplicate ratings, older/newer/equal timestamp cases, equal-time mutation and byte ties, removal tombstones, and cross-device adoption.

A5 is the explicit virtual-group exception required by its source comment that the full journey syncs as one state: oregon-full-journey-v1 maps the listed source fields into canonical PathKey $|p:journeySnapshot and unwraps them losslessly on adoption. The source oracle proves every listed source field has exactly one alias into that group and no source field is silently dropped. The opaque bounded LWW comparison happens once at the group root, after schema validation, so a merge cannot combine a day from one journey with supplies or event data from another.

BAL is an intentional absolute-balance policy, not arithmetic addition: the current stores expose balances after earn/spend operations and no source delta ledger exists. For divergent valid balances, the comparator selects the higher metadata clock, then mutation ID at a tie, emits the losing PathKey in conflicts, and never fabricates a sum or lets a lower-clock retry resurrect a spend. The independent golden cases cover concurrent earn/spend divergence for Hill Climb, Endless Runner, Cookie Clicker, and Virtual Pet, replay, retry, equal-clock tie, and server canonical adoption. These cases document the bounded source-grounded loss policy and fail if a future implementation silently changes BAL to MAX, SUM, or generic LWW without changing the explicit matrix.

Every EXCLUDE field is removed from the synchronized Zustand partializer during the source edits. It remains available in FullStoreState so server adoption and rollback can preserve unrelated runtime memory, but it has no persisted projection path, metadata entry, request field, or server schema path. The source oracle asserts that each real partializer path is present in the canonical matrix or was removed by this migration. This is the fixed local/runtime-only treatment for current game boards, animation state, fetch caches, and achievement queue/watermarks.

  actionIds: readonly string[];
  metadataPaths: readonly LivePathKey[];
  proofIds: readonly string[];
  requiredIntegrationIds: readonly string[];
  requiredIntegrationOracles: Readonly<Record<RequiredIntegrationId, RequiredIntegrationOracle>>;
  fixtures: readonly ProgressSyncFixture[];
  policyRows: readonly SourcePolicyOracleRow[];
  metadataCapacity: MetadataCapacityProjection;
  fileInventory: readonly FileInventoryInput[];
};
declare function renderPlanProjection(input: ProgressSyncManifestInput): string;
declare function parsePlanProjection(html: string): RenderedPlanProjection;
declare function assertRenderedPlanProjection(input: ProgressSyncManifestInput, html: string): void;
declare function deriveIndependentSourcePolicyOracle(): readonly SourcePolicyOracleRow[];
declare function assertSourcePolicyOracle(
  generatedRows: readonly SourcePolicyOracleRow[],
  independentRows: readonly SourcePolicyOracleRow[]
): void;
declare function generateProgressSyncManifest(input: ProgressSyncManifestInput): ProgressSyncManifestGenerated;
declare function assertGeneratedProjectionEquality(
  input: ProgressSyncManifestInput,
  generated: ProgressSyncManifestGenerated,
  rendered: RenderedPlanProjection,
  independentSourceOracle: SourceOracleProjection
): void;
type ProgressSyncManifestGenerated = {
  version: 1;
  entries: readonly ManifestEntryInput[];
  appIds: readonly ValidAppId[];
  registrations: readonly GeneratedRegistration[];
  storageTargets: readonly GeneratedStorageTarget[];
  ownerControlTargets: readonly GeneratedStorageTarget[];
  policyRoots: readonly PolicyRoot[];
  metadataLedger: readonly MetadataBinding[];
  mutationWrappers: readonly GeneratedMutationWrapper[];
  converters: readonly GeneratedConverter[];
  routeProjections: readonly GeneratedRouteProjection[];
  canonicalProjections: readonly GeneratedCanonicalProjection[];
  fileInventory: readonly GeneratedFileInventoryRow[];
  renderedInventory: readonly GeneratedInventoryRow[];
  closureRows: readonly GeneratedClosureRow[];
  proofCases: readonly ProofCaseBinding[];
  proofContracts: Readonly<Record<string, ProofCaseContract>>;
  policyRows: readonly SourcePolicyOracleRow[];
  metadataCapacity: MetadataCapacityProjection;
  requiredIntegration: GeneratedRequiredIntegrationProjection;
  renderedPlanProjection: RenderedPlanProjection;
  testIds: readonly string[];
};
const EXPECTED_FILE_INVENTORY_CARDINALITY = 162;
function assertFileInventoryCardinality(rows: readonly FileInventoryInput[]): void {
  const paths = rows.map((row) => row.path);
  const unique = new Set(paths);
  if (
    rows.length !== EXPECTED_FILE_INVENTORY_CARDINALITY ||
    unique.size !== EXPECTED_FILE_INVENTORY_CARDINALITY ||
    paths.some((path) => !path.startsWith("apps/web/") && !path.startsWith("packages/db/")) ||
    paths.some((path) => path.includes("..") || path.includes("//"))
  ) {
    throw new Error("implementation file inventory cardinality, uniqueness, or root check failed");
  }
}

The sole hand-authored input is apps/web/src/shared/lib/progress-sync-manifest-input.ts. It exports exactly 32 defineEntry calls in the current VALID_APP_IDS source order plus implementationFiles, proofCaseSeeds, closureRows, and 21 requiredIntegrationSeeds. Each required seed carries its exact ID, function-valued setup and assertion factories, terminal-evidence kind, file, and test name; the compiler maps those seeds to REQUIRED_INTEGRATION_ORACLES and REQUIRED_INTEGRATION_CASES. Each proof seed also carries function-valued setup, assertion, and terminal-evidence factories and compiles to one private ProofCaseContract without adding a proof ID. Each entry also carries one typed ProgressSyncFixtureBuilder, including the A32 caller apps/web/src/shared/components/AchievementCelebrations.tsx. Those seeds are the one typed input graph; PROOF_CASES, PROOF_CASE_CONTRACTS, REQUIRED_INTEGRATION_ORACLES, REQUIRED_INTEGRATION_CASES, closure rows, required-ID projections, fixture projections, and rendered tables are generated outputs and are never hand-maintained second sources. Each entry contains app ID, store module, caller module, primary key, explicit legacy keys, shape class, source schema root, converter names, one PersistMigrationRecipe, partializer path capture, action binding recipe, policy AST whose metadataBindings is the only metadata ledger, caps, fixture builder, and named semantic cases. A recipe explicitly declares all supported source versions: ordinary stores use version 0 or missing version, Checkers uses the live checkers-v2-pre-envelope record and version 2, and Oregon uses the live oregon-v1-pre-envelope record and version 1 before its weather/health canonicalization. The usual recipe is identity migration with missingVersion:"treat-as-v0"; Oregon's recipe ID is oregon.version-0-weather and maps legacy rainy, snowy, and stormy to canonical rain, snow, and storm before schema validation. Missing-version decoding is allowed only in the verified guest branch. The exact order is decode the declared source version, run the named migration, validate migrated persisted state, convert, validate canonical/live state, construct the version-1 envelope, and read it back. A migration or read-back failure leaves the original bytes, quarantines them, sends no network, and blocks READY. implementationFiles has exactly EXPECTED_FILE_INVENTORY_CARDINALITY=162 unique normalized repository-relative paths, and the rendered Section 11 table is a byte-for-byte path/purpose/consumer projection of it. No second hand-authored inventory, policy source, metadata ledger, or runtime test-ID list exists.

apps/web/scripts/generate-progress-sync-manifest.ts compiles that input to apps/web/src/shared/lib/progress-sync-manifest.generated.ts and calls assertFileInventoryCardinality(input.implementationFiles) before emitting any projection. It derives runtime registration data, all key targets, owner accessors, policy AST, metadata ledger, mutation wrappers, fixture builders, rendered inventory, converters, route app set, canonical projections, closureRows, proofCases, requiredIntegration:{ids,cases,oracles}, test IDs, and the exact file-inventory projection. The runtime/test dependency graph is input -> generated projection -> runtime/tests/rendering. The independent source-oracle dependency graph is live source/fixtures -> oracle projections -> equality tests; those oracle projections are compared with generated output but never imported by or used to generate it. Generated output never feeds input generation. The compiler rejects cycles, duplicate apps, duplicate keys, duplicate PathKeys, duplicate action IDs, duplicate closure/proof/required IDs, missing converters, missing metadata bindings, missing fixture builders, missing required cases, missing oracle assertions, unbounded policy nodes, app IDs not present in the live source registry, and any file-inventory length, uniqueness, normalization, root, or rendered-equality failure.

generate-progress-sync-manifest.ts also calls renderPlanProjection(input) to produce the HTML table projections and calls assertRenderedPlanProjection(input,checkedInPlanHtml) in the manifest test. parsePlanProjection(html) parses file-inventory, sync-inventory, semantic-matrix, metadata-capacity, the closure table, the required-ID block, REQUIRED_INTEGRATION_CASES, and PROOF_CASES into RenderedPlanProjection; each policy row carries typed policyFacts in addition to display-only matrixHtml. It compares every inventory path, exact purpose string, exact consumer list, all A1-A32 key/shape rows, all 32 policy cells, the exact global metadata-capacity rows, every closure proof ID, every required integration ID/binding, every proof binding, every rooted policy node, every presence rule, every source range/fingerprint, every ordered scope ordinal, every metadata binding, every expansion witness, and the derived capacity fields. assertGeneratedProjectionEquality compares generated closure/proof/required/policy/capacity projections with the rendered projection and the independently parsed SourceOracleProjection by exact sorted set, order, field, and cardinality equality for apps, paths, actions, metadata, cases, files, IDs, semantic facts, scopes, witnesses, and bounds. The check is deep equality, not path count or matrix HTML equality. A generated renderer never reads the checked-in HTML to create the manifest, and the parser is used only to verify the checked-in rendering.

progress-sync-source-oracle.ts and its tests independently derive expected source facts from live files and fixtures using a source parser that never imports ProgressSyncManifestInput or generated policy rows: VALID_APP_IDS, PROGRESS_SCHEMAS, every caller's localStorageKey, every real persist name, every partializer return shape, every public action that changes a partialized field, every getProgress/setProgress shape, every direct caller setProgress callsite, every direct store-api setter callsite, every adoption callback, every leaderboard extractor, every policy semantic fact, and independent semantic golden cases. The independent parser emits one SourcePolicyOracleRow per app with a source policy fingerprint, non-empty rooted node facts, exact PresenceRule facts, source ranges/fingerprints, ordered ScopeOrdinals, typed PolicySemanticEvidence, MetadataBindingTemplate rows, nested expansion witnesses, and its own capacity projection. It explicitly derives A10 seenJokeIds fanout 5000, A25 saved-beat/dynamic-key/index expansion, A12 country/admin1, A14 notes, A25 favoriteKitId, and A26 petName presence facts from live schema/source evidence. The oracle does not import generated inventory rows, matrix HTML, or generated capacity to discover expected facts. Exact bidirectional equality and cardinality are required for apps (32), primary/legacy key targets, persisted paths, store actions, direct caller invocations, direct API invocations, adoption partitions, policy.metadataBindings, policy nodes, presence rules, semantic evidence, expansion witnesses, capacity, golden cases, and the 162-row implementation-file projection. assertSourcePolicyOracle fails on any generated/source row, node, path, presence, range, fingerprint, scope, metadata, witness, or capacity divergence. The file-inventory oracle asserts 162 unique paths, no path outside the allowed repository roots, no duplicate path, and exact equality between implementationFiles, the parsed Section 11 rows, and the generated file-inventory projection. It compares purpose and consumer content as well as paths. A rendered row count equal to 32 without source equality is a failure.

Each manifest entry's ProgressSyncFixtureBuilder is independently checked against the real store constructor, partializer, schema, getProgress, setProgress, and leaderboard extractor. For every A1-A32 it emits default and meaningful fixtures, a divergent local/server pair, and an invalid-projection fixture where the source exposes that branch. ProgressSyncFixture.invalidProjection is null for ordinary/default/meaningful fixtures and is populated only by the controlled extractor-wrapper case; it is never treated as a schema-valid leaderboard result. The source oracle asserts that default fixtures satisfy the source default predicate, meaningful fixtures differ on at least one declared persisted path, persisted fixtures equal the real partializer output, canonical fixtures equal the real getProgress shape after the fixed clock field migration, excluded runtime fields never appear in canonical/request/DB projections, and every enabled fixture passes validateProgress, the real extractor, and leaderboardEntrySchema or returns the one declared null no-score result. The same populated fixtures feed ordinary policy tests and the isolated route suite; a fixture is never accepted merely because a generated schema accepts it. Exact bidirectional equality is required for 32 app IDs, 32 schema roots, 32 fixture builders, 32 primary keys, every actual caller including AchievementCelebrations.tsx, every discovered partializer path, every policy path, every action binding, and every leaderboard projection.

The independent integration oracle derives the 21 scenario factories from the required integration fixture contract. Each factory returns a private branded RequiredIntegrationOracle with function-valued setup(context), assert(context,evidence), and terminalEvidence(context) members. setup must create the exact fixture and barrier evidence in the context; assert must inspect the real response, lock observation, DML ledger, and row snapshots; terminalEvidence must return an IntegrationTerminalEvidence containing the exact ID, response status/body/correlation, row snapshot, lock snapshot when required, envelope round-trip when required, and invocation token. The runner sets private setupInvoked, assertInvoked, and evidenceInvoked bits only when it calls those functions; a passing reporter event rejects false bits, a mismatched oracle ID, an empty evidence ledger, or a terminal event without the oracle invocation token. The independent map compares factory IDs, files, test names, function brands, terminal evidence kinds, and runtime invocation records, not string tokens. A scenario with no terminal event or with a function that was never called is a failure. This comparison is separate from the generated map and prevents the required-ID union from becoming a green coverage count with no concurrency, rollback, envelope, lock, or DML proof.

The same source oracle parses getter ASTs for Hill Climb, Monster Truck, and Oregon and rejects Date.now(), performance.now(), Math.random(), storage reads, network reads, or injected-clock reads inside getProgress. Getters are pure projections of source state. Only commitSourceMutation may read the injected mutation clock and write lastModified, metadata, and the mutation notice at the actual setter completion. The idempotent getter case calls each getter twice with unchanged state and requires byte-identical output and no clock read.

The independent action oracle parses each source store and caller and first partitions every invocation as "user-source-mutation" or "adoption-callback". A store action produces MutationBindingInvocation:{kind:"store-action",partition:"user-source-mutation",sourceModule,methodName,sourceRange,setterFingerprint} and action ID sourceModule+"#"+methodName. A direct setProgress caller produces MutationBindingInvocation:{kind:"direct-caller",partition:"user-source-mutation",callerModule,calleeName:"setProgress",sourceRange,callsiteFingerprint} and action ID callerModule+"#setProgress@"+formatSourceRange(sourceRange). The Breakout callsite useBreakoutStore.setState produces MutationBindingInvocation:{kind:"direct-api-setState",partition:"user-source-mutation",callerModule:"apps/web/src/games/breakout/Game.tsx",apiExpression:"useBreakoutStore.setState",sourceRange,callsiteFingerprint}; its wrapper records the canonical field diff before the API call. Virtual Pet, Checkers, and AchievementCelebrations adoption callbacks are separate partition:"adoption-callback" fixtures and cannot allocate a mutation notice or metadata clock. The oracle records the partialized paths changed by the setter or direct call, whether the final setter is synchronous or awaits a completion promise, and the exact AST fingerprint. Explicit async recipes cover Drawing thumbnails and every delayed completion. Explicit runtime-only recipes cover achievement watermarks and queue. assertMutationBindingRegistry requires one discriminated invocation, one exact range whose setter, API expression, or callsite fingerprint matches the independent source AST, one completion mode, one partition, and exact persisted-path equality; it rejects duplicate action IDs, duplicate ranges, unmatched store actions, unmatched direct setProgress calls, an unbound direct API setState, an adoption callback classified as a user mutation, a direct call with another callee name, and a recipe with a range outside its module. The five current direct setProgress callers are Virtual Pet, Drum Machine, Asteroids, Hextris, and Bomberman, plus the Breakout direct API caller. Every discovered store action, direct caller, direct API setter, and adoption callback maps to one generated MutationBindingRecipe; every generated recipe points to one discovered source invocation and partition.

Runtime test IDs are generated from the one input graph, but the tests also compare them to the assertion-only golden multiset in required-test-id-oracle.ts, whose 21 literal IDs are not imported by the generator. The oracle requires cardinality 21, exact set equality with generated integration cases, and exact one-terminal-event equality with the reporter. Removing a case from the input therefore fails the independent comparison instead of shrinking the expected set.

This table is the complete future implementation inventory with exactly EXPECTED_FILE_INVENTORY_CARDINALITY=162 unique rows. Every named consumer, source mutation, adapter, harness, schema, converter, reporter, migration, and test appears here. The eighth amendment itself edits none of these files. No pnpm-lock.yaml or other lockfile is in the inventory.

11.1 TDD order

  1. Apply the source migrations first: Oregon canonical party IDs and very_poor health literal, Toy Finder item timestamps and recent-view cap, Retro Arcade cap 20, Drum Machine saved-beat patternLength normalization, Virtual Pet empty lastPlayDate sentinel validation, achievement partializer removal of watermarks/queue, Drawing awaited completion, Memory Match delayed completion binding, Monster Truck and Oregon persisted lastModified:0 clock fields with version-0 migrations, the four non-account storage exclusions, the owner-epoch schema migration, and every persisted-path/schema change listed in the inventory. Capture the resulting source fixtures, write the manifest compiler, independent oracle, path codec, metadata validators, and all 32 generated projections. Run source bidirectional equality after these source edits and before coordinator/runtime changes.
  2. Implement the typed local envelope, raw/storage wrappers, construction-time store helper, flat/wrapped/Chess/achievement converters, and real-store identity/hydration tests.
  3. Implement the coordinator state machine, marker accessors, exact purge/recovery, guest truth table, ownership races, owner-control delivery ledger, and the auth-client.ts/useAuthSync.ts sign-out delegation.
  4. Implement commitSourceMutation, generated action wrappers, Drawing and Memory Match completion boundaries, achievement watermark exclusion, all four direct-storage exclusions, and provenance tests.
  5. Implement metadata policy merge, lineage baselines, transport builders/parsers, response adoption, Retry-After, and client wire tests.
  6. Implement DB factory, typed adapter, route factory, method schemas, SQL timeouts, diagnostics, leaderboard rules, and ordinary route tests.
  7. Implement isolated PostgreSQL shell, dynamic integration setup, fault seam, reporter, all 21 required integration IDs, then run build/typecheck and the exact verification commands.
